André–Quillen cohomology in the context of curved algebras Thanks: B.-M. and Y. were supported by ANR-17-CE40-0014 CatAG; B.-M. was supported by ANR-20-CE40-0016 HighAGT; Y. was supported by ANR-16-CE40-0003 ChroK, funded by Agence Nationale pour la Recherche.
Joan Bellier-Millès, Sinan Yalin

Abstract
. The André–Quillen cohomology of an algebra with coefficients in a module is defined by deriving a functor based on Kähler differential forms. It can be computed using a cofibrant resolution of the algebra in a model category structure where weak equivalences are quasi-isomorphisms. This construction works for algebras over an operad, providing a cohomology theory tailored for each type of algebra. For curved algebras however, the notion of quasi-isomorphism is meaningless. The occurrence and importance of curved structures in various research topics (symplectic topology, deformation theory, derived geometry, mathematical physics…) motivate the development of their homotopy theory and André–Quillen cohomology theory. To get a homotopical context with an appropriate notion of weak equivalence, we consider filtered complete modules with a predifferential inducing a differential on the associated graded. Curved algebras in such modules are algebras over a curved operad. In this article, we consider curved operads which are not necessarily augmented. Bar and cobar constructions adapted to these curved operads are developed, as well as Koszul duality theory. Consequently, we obtain homotopy versions of our curved algebras and make it explicit for interesting cases. Two main examples are the curved operads encoding curved unital associative algebras and curved complex Lie algebras. In particular, homotopy curved unital associative algebras describe the structure of Floer complexes of lagrangian submanifolds and Fukaya categories in symplectic topology. Bar and cobar constructions for curved algebras are also developed, and we obtain resolutions from which we compute their André–Quillen cohomology with module coefficients. Our computations of André-Quillen cohomology in the case of curved complex Lie algebras reveal an interesting way to implement integrable almost complex structures in derived analytic geometry.



Introduction

Motivations and antecedents

In this article, our goal is to further develop the homotopical and homological theory of curved algebras in continuity with
[
BMDC20
]
.
A first example of algebras is associative algebras.
They are made of an underlying vector space or module and a multiplication that satisfies the associativity relation.
The study of extensions and deformations of an associative algebra leads to the definition of the Hochschild cochains and cohomology.
We can enrich the notion of associative algebra by adding a unit, by assuming that the underlying object is a chain complex or even by relaxing the associativity relation (
\mathrm{A}_{\infty}
-algebras).
The definition of the Hochschild cohomology extends to these settings.

It happens that an associative algebra
(A,\cdot)
may have a derivation
d
whose square is not zero but is equal to the bracket with an element
\theta
in
A
, called
curvature
:
d^{2}=[\theta,-]=(\theta\cdot-)-(-\cdot\theta)
.
Examples can be built by means of a vector bundle endowed with a connection (and the curvature coincides with the curvature of the connection) or can be obtained from a matrix factorisation (nevertheless, in this example,
\theta
is central so
d^{2}=0=[\theta,-]
).
Defining Hochschild cohomology in this setting poses some problems as for example the fact that it vanishes for curved algebras built from matrix factorisations, see Theorem 4.2 in
[
CT13
]
.
Other definitions are proposed there but the relation with Hochschild cohomology is not well-understood.
A different direction has been followed in
[
FOOO07
]
where the authors assume that the algebraic objects are filtered and complete.
This is also the context used in
[
BMDC20
]
.
Let us note also that curved
A_{\infty}
-structures appear naturally as deformations of an
A_{\infty}
-algebra or an
A_{\infty}
-category, in the formal moduli problem controlled by its (full) Hochschild complex (see for example
[
vdK23
]
and
[
LVdB15
]
).

The first cohomology theories of algebras (associative and Lie algebras) were defined by describing explicit cochain complexes.
Later, in order to study commutative algebras, Quillen
[
Qui70
]
and André
[
And74
]
have proposed a cohomology theory described by means of a derived functor.
These ideas have been applied to many types of algebras using the notion of operads, and widely studied under the name of André–Quillen cohomology (
[
GH00
,
Mil11
,
HNP18
]
for example).
Following this guideline, the article
[
BMDC20
]
presents model category structures on a base category of
gr-dg modules
, on curved operads and on algebras over a curved operad (that we consider as curved algebras in a wide sense).
In order to compute the cotangent complex which appears in the definition of the André–Quillen cohomology, we need cofibrant resolutions.

Part of this effort has been made in
[
BMDC20
]
.
However, in the present article, we are concerned in a more general situation than the one presented there.
While the curved operads studied in the aforementioned article are augmented, we are interested with curved operads that are not.
Two interesting examples of non-augmented curved operads are the one encoding curved unital associative algebras (
\mathrm{cuAs}
) and the one encoding curved complex Lie algebras (
\mathrm{cCx}
). Beware that in the second example, the operad is defined over
\mathbb{R}
and not over
\mathbb{C}
(so it is not the
\mathbb{C}
-linear operad parametrising complex Lie algebras).
The related operad
\mathrm{Cx}
encoding (uncurved) complex Lie algebras appears in the study of formal complex structures as integrable almost complex structures (see
[
BM18
]
).
In order to define meaningful homotopy versions of these algebras, we extend the bar and cobar constructions and the Koszul duality theory in this context.
We then provide cofibrant resolutions of algebras over such curved operads and we use them to compute their André–Quillen cohomology.

One of our motivations for these, sometimes technical, generalisations, comes from symplectic topology. Given an ambient symplectic manifold
M
, the Floer complex of a lagrangian submanifold inherits the structure of a filtered homotopy
\mathrm{cuAs}
-algebra, whose operations encompass the counting of pseudo-holomorphic disks in
M
bordered by the lagrangian as well as pseudo-holomorphic polygons. There is a relative version defined for a pair of lagrangians, which acquires the structure of a filtered
A_{\infty}
-bimodule. At a categorical level, the Fukaya category of
M
consists in lagrangian submanifolds with Floer complexes as morphisms and forms a curved unital
A_{\infty}
-category
[
FOOO07
]
. Together with Lagrangian Floer cohomology, these are central objects of study in symplectic topology and are closely related to homological mirror symmetry. In particular, their deformation and obstruction theory has still to be well understood.

Another motivation comes from derived complex analytic geometry, which has experienced a striking development during the past few years relying in particular on the works of Lurie, Porta and Porta-Yu
[
Lur
,
Por16
,
PYY20
]
. Indeed, complex structures on a formal manifold are examples of homotopy
\mathrm{cCx}
-algebras (see
[
BM18
]
for its uncurved version
\mathrm{Cx}
). The peculiarity of
\mathrm{Cx}
is that it is an
\mathbb{R}
-linear (not
\mathbb{C}
-linear) operad parametrising complex structures as integrable almost complex structures, which offers an explicit way to set up a homotopy coherent notion of integrability suitable for
derived
geometric objects.

As detailled below, the current literature does not allow for an operadic treatment for theses two motivations.

In relation with the André–Quillen cohomology, we prove that there is a fully faithful functor from formal derived complex structures to EFC-dgas (dg algebras with an entire functional calculus), defined in
[
Pri20
]
as the building blocks of a dg model for derived analytic geometry. In
[
Pri20
]
, derived analytic spaces locally structured by these algebras are proved to be equivalent to the derived complex analytic spaces of
[
Lur
,
Por16
]
whose
0
-truncation is a complex analytic space.

We use this bridge between operadic homotopical algebra and derived complex analytic spaces in an ongoing work devoted to integrability in derived complex geometry. We say a few more words about this perspective at the end of the introduction.

Homotopy algebras in the curved context

Given a type of algebra described by a curved operad
\mathcal{P}
, homotopy
\mathcal{P}
-algebras are algebras over an (
\mathbb{S}
-)cofibrant resolution
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}
of
\mathcal{P}
.
When the curved operad
\mathcal{P}
is augmented, two classical resolutions are given by the bar-cobar resolution and the Koszul resolution.
In the present article, our curved operads are not assumed to be augmented.
A bar construction for (not necessarily augmented) curved operads and a cobar construction for (not necessarily coaugmented) curved cooperads already exist in
[
Lyu14
]
.
However they don’t form the adjunction that is required to describe the bar-cobar resolution (see Propositions 6.1.2 and 6.1.3 in
[
Lyu14
]
).
We provide in this article similar constructions adapted to the gr-dg context. Our bar and cobar constructions represent (on the right and on the left respectively) a bifunctor of operadic curved twisting morphisms. These operadic curved twisting morphisms are maps from a curved cooperad
\mathcal{C}
to a curved operad
\mathcal{P}
,
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}
, of homological degree
-1
solutions to a curved Maurer–Cartan equation
\Theta+\partial\alpha+\frac{1}{2}[\alpha,\alpha]=0
where
\Theta
is a curvature term.
In order to obtain this adjunction, we have to consider (as in
[
Lyu14
]
) lax notions of morphisms between curved operads and between curved cooperads. The counit of the bar-cobar adjunction provides then a functorial cofibrant resolution. To summarize our main results of this part, we have:

Theorem 0.1

(Theorems
1.26
and
1.30
)
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a curved semi-augmented operad and
\mathcal{C}
be a curved altipotent cooperad.

(1)

The bar construction
\hat{\mathrm{B}}
and cobar construction
\hat{\Omega}
define an adjunction between curved sa operads and curved altipotent cooperads with lax morphisms, and the adjunction relation factors naturally through twisting morphisms

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{curv.\,sa\,op.}^{\mathrm{lax}}}(\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\cong\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{curv.\,alti.\,coop.}^{\mathrm{lax}}}(\mathcal{C},\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}).

(2)

Suppose that
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}
is a connected bounded below weight filtered operad. Then the counit of the adjunction

\hat{\Omega}\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{P}

is a graded quasi-isomorphism and a strict surjection.

(3)

Under good assumptions on the filtration and the curvature of the curved operad
\mathcal{P}
, this counit forms a functorial cofibrant resolution in the model category structure on curved operads described in
[
BMDC20
, Appendix C]
.

To obtain a smaller resolution of our curved operads, we develop a curved Koszul duality adapted to some inhomogeneous quadratic curved operads.
In good situations, this produces a resolution of the curved operad which is cofibrant in the underlying category of
\mathbb{S}
-modules (
\mathbb{S}
-cofibrant resolution):

Theorem 0.2

(Theorem
1.35
)
.

(1)

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a Koszul inhomogeneous quadratic curved operad such that
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}
is connected bounded below weight graded.
The curved operad morphism

f_{\kappa}:\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\to\mathcal{P}

is a graded quasi-isomorphism and a strict surjection.

(2)

Under good assumptions on the filtration and the curvature of the curved operad
\mathcal{P}
, the Koszul resolution
\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}
is
\mathbb{S}
-cofibrant in the model category structure on gr-dg
\mathbb{S}
-modules described in
[
BMDC20
, Appendix C]
.

Remark 0.3
.

By Theorem C.45 in
[
BMDC20
]
, the model categories of
\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}
-algebras and of
\hat{\Omega}\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}
-algebras are both equivalent to the model category of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras (because all operads are
\mathbb{S}
-split in characteristic 0).
Consequently, up to equivalence, the
\infty
-category of homotopy
\mathcal{P}
-algebras does not depend on a choice of resolution, similarly to what happens for uncurved algebras over operads.

Our two main examples of interest are the curved operad
\mathrm{cuAs}
encoding
curved unital associative algebras
and the curved operad
\mathrm{cCx}
encoding
curved complex Lie algebras
as algebras over an
\mathbb{R}
-linear operad. Both satisfies the conditions of the Theorems above, by Theorems
2.3
and
2.9
.
We describe the
\mathbb{S}
-cofibrant resolutions obtained by means of the curved Koszul duality and denote them by
\mathrm{cuA}_{\infty}
and
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
respectively.

Resolutions of curved algebras

As for curved operads, we construct a cofibrant resolution of algebras by means of the bar-cobar adjunction.
Given an algebra
A
over a curved (semi-augmented) operad
\mathcal{P}
, a coalgebra
C
over a curved (altipotent) cooperad
\mathcal{C}
and an operadic curved twisting morphism
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}
, we define a curved
\mathrm{L}_{\infty}
-algebra structure on the module of maps
\hom(C,A)
following ideas given in
[
Wie19
]
.
It is defined by means of the following results:

•

Proposition
3.21
says that
\hom(C,A)
is an algebra over the curved operad
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
;

•

Proposition
1.28
ensures that there is a bijection

\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{curv.\,sa\,op.}^{\mathrm{lax}}}(\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cL_{\infty}},\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P}))

where
\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cL_{\infty}}
is the curved operad encoding shifted curved Lie algebras;

•

and Proposition
3.9
makes explicit the map
\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cL_{\infty}}\to\mathrm{End}_{\hom(C,A)}
obtained from
\alpha
as a collection of maps
\{l_{n}^{\alpha}\}_{n\geq 0}
.

Algebraic twisting morphisms with respect to
\alpha
are then degree 0 maps
\varphi:C\to A
solutions to the Maurer–Cartan equation

\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{1}{n!}l_{n}^{\alpha}(\varphi,\ldots,\varphi)=0.

We use these algebraic twisting morphisms as follows:

Theorem 0.4
.

(1)

The bifunctor of algebraic twisting morphisms with respect to
\alpha
can be represented on the left by the cobar functor
\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}
and on the right by the bar functor
\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}
.

(2)

Under good assumptions, the counit of this adjunction
\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A\to A
provides a cofibrant resolution of
A
.

The cobar construction
\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}
allows us to define a notion of
\infty
-morphisms for curved algebras in the same way they are defined in the uncurved case. That is, an
\infty
-morphism from
A
to
B
is a curved coalgebras morphism
\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}A\rightarrow\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}B
. By the theorem above, such an
\infty
-morphism corresponds to a zig-zag of curved algebra morphisms
A\xleftarrow{\sim}\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A\rightarrow B
of morphisms.

André–Quillen cohomology

Given an algebra
A
over a curved operad
\mathcal{P}
, we define the notion of
A
-module as a gr-dg module
(M,d_{M})
with a coherent action
\gamma_{M}
of
A
by means of the operations in
\mathcal{P}
on
M
.
The difference coming from the curved context is that the equality
{d_{M}}^{2}=\gamma_{M}(\theta_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes-)
has to be satisfied.
It follows from this equation that the underlying module of the free
A
-module on a gr-dg module
(N,d_{N})
depends on the gr-differential
d_{N}
.
As usual, we can define derivations as a linearized version of the notion of morphisms and we can represent on the left (by means of Kähler differential forms) and on the right the module of derivations.
Deriving the module of Kähler differential forms, we obtain the André–Quillen cohomology:

Theorem 0.5

(Theorems
4.22
and
4.24
, Definition
4.23
, Proposition
4.25
)
.

(1)

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a curved operad and
A
be a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra. Let us note
\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P},0}
the category of operadic
A
-modules with degree zero
A
-module morphisms commuting with the predifferentials.
The following two functors (abelianisation induced by Kähler differential forms on the left, and square zero extension by
A
on the right)

A\hat{\otimes}_{-}^{\mathcal{P}}\Omega_{\mathcal{P}}-\ :\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}/A\rightleftharpoons\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P},0}\cong\mathsf{Ab(\mathcal{P}\text{-}alg}/A)\ :\ A\ltimes-

form a Quillen adjunction, and the
cotangent complex
of a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
A
, denoted by
\mathbb{L}_{A}
, is then the derived abelianisation
\mathbb{L}_{A}\coloneqq\mathbb{L}(A\hat{\otimes}_{-}^{\mathcal{P}}\Omega_{\mathcal{P}}-)(A)
of
A
.

(2)

Given an
A
-module
M
in
\mathsf{Ab(\mathcal{P}\text{-}alg}/A)\cong\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P},0}
, the
André–Quillen cohomology of
A
with coefficients in
M
is defined by

H_{AQ}^{n}\left(A,M\right)\coloneqq\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Ho}(\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P},0})}\left(\mathbb{L}_{A},M[n]\right)=H^{n}\left(\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P}}}\left(\mathbb{L}_{A},M\right),\partial\right).

(3)

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a curved sa operad,
\mathcal{C}
be a curved altipotent cooperad and
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}
be an operadic curved twisting morphism. Let
A
be a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra. Assume that
\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A
is a cofibrant resolution of
A
. Then the corresponding cotangent complex is a
quasifree
A
-module over the bar construction
\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A
.

We provide explicit chain complexes to compute it in certain good situations such as for curved unital associative algebras (or
\mathrm{cuA}_{\infty}
-algebras) and for curved complex Lie algebras (or
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
-algebras), the two main examples presented at the beginning of the introduction.

\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
-algebras in derived complex analytic geometry

We show for example that 0-cycles of a
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
-algebra
A
with coefficients in the trivial
A
-module
\mathbb{C}
of complex numbers coincide with
\infty
-morphisms between the
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
-algebras
A
and
\mathbb{C}
.
In particular, when
A
encodes a formal complex structure (see
[
BM18
]
), 0-cycles are formal holomorphic functions on a formal complex manifold.
We also show that a chain complex computing the André–Quillen cohomology of
A
with coefficients in
\mathbb{C}
is a
\mathbb{C}
-cdga (see Proposition
5.5
) and that it produces a fully faithful functor to EFC-dgas (see Theorem
5.7
):

Theorem 0.6
.

The functor

\begin{array}[]{rccl}C_{AQ}^{\bullet}:&(\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}\textsf{-alg.},\infty\text{-}\mathrm{mor})&\to&\textsf{EFC-dga},\\
&(A,\gamma_{A},d_{A})&\mapsto&\left(\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}}(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A,\mathbb{C}),\partial_{\tau}\right)\end{array}

is well-defined and fully faithful.

Here
(\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}\textsf{-alg.},\infty\text{-}\mathrm{mor})
is the category whose objects are
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
-algebras and morphisms are
\infty
-morphisms.
We also note
EFC
the category whose objects are the
\mathbb{C}^{n},n\in\mathbb{N}
and the morphisms are the complex analytic maps.
It is a small category closed under finite products and generated under products by
\mathbb{C}
, that is, a Lawvere theory. An EFC-algebra is then a product-preserving set-valued functor
\textsf{EFC}\rightarrow\textsf{Set}
. This means that an EFC-algebra is a set
A
equipped with operations induced by the ring of holomorphic functions
\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{C}^{n})
on
\mathbb{C}^{n}
for every
n
(in particular, for
n=2
it implies that
A
is a commutative
\mathbb{C}
-algebra). An important example of EFC-algebra is the ring of global functions of any complex analytic space. An EFC-dga is then a dg algebra whose
0
-cycles carries an EFC-algebra structure.
Derived analytic spaces à la Pridham
[
Pri20
]
are homotopy sheaves over the site of EFC-dgas, so these play a role similar to affine schemes in algebraic geometry and cdgas in derived algebraic geometry.
The comparison with derived analytic spaces in the sense of
[
Lur
,
Por16
]
follows from
[
Pri20
, Proposition 4.3, Proposition 4.5]
.
Briefly, derived analytic spaces in the sense of Lurie-Porta whose
0
-truncation is a classical analytic space are equivalent to Pridham analytic spaces.

The theorem above points out a very interesting relationship between
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
-algebras and such derived complex analytic spaces. Indeed, as mentioned above, André–Quillen complexes of the appropriate
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
-algebras are dg enhancements of formal holomorphic functions on formal complex manifolds and, (bar) dually,
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
-algebras encode a
homotopy coherent
notion of integrable almost complex structure on tangent spaces of such formal manifolds. Therefore, we consider such
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
-algebras as the local picture of yet-to-be-defined derived integrable almost complex analytic spaces. This indicates how to implement a homotopy coherent notion of integrable almost complex structure in derived complex analytic geometry (a derived flavour of the Newlander–Nirenberg integrability theorem), which does not appear in the currently existing models based on the locally ringed space approach. Theorem
0.6
provides then a way to compare our model with Pridham’s model (and consequently with Lurie-Porta’s model), showing that “going back to the ring of functions” gives a dg EFC-algebra structure whose
0
-truncation is a standard complex analytic structure. The detailed construction of this new model with various examples and applications in mind is the focus of a subsequent ongoing work.

Model category structures on curved algebras

There exist other model category structures related to curved algebras.
In the references
[
CLM16
,
Gri19
,
Roc23
]
, the model category structure on curved algebras or curved operads is obtained via transfer through the bar-cobar adjunction.
This is possible because the category on the other side of the adjunction has a well-defined model category structure where weak equivalences are quasi-isomorphisms.
In the present article, the same idea cannot be used since our objects are curved on both sides of the bar-cobar adjunction, and therefore quasi-isomorphisms aren’t relevant on both sides.

In
[
Pos11
, Section 9.3]
, Positselski describes a model category structure on curved coassociative coalgebras.
He considers increasing filtrations on the coalgebras with conditions based on the fact that the coalgebra admits a coaugmentation.
Weak equivalences are then generated by graded quasi-isomorphisms (called filtered quasi-isomorphisms).
This model category structure is Quillen equivalent through the bar-cobar adjunction to a model category structure on a category of augmented dg associative algebras (see the end of Section 9.3 in
[
Pos11
]
). In this latter model structure, weak equivalences are quasi-isomorphisms.
In the present paper, both our algebras and coalgebras have curvature and they’re not augmented or coaugmented on the algebraic level. This forces us to use different model category structures.

The reference
[
Pos12
]
deals with curved homotopy associative algebras which are modules over a local ring and such that the curvature is divisible by the maximal ideal. In this context, it proposes a Koszul duality theory based on the derived functor
\mathrm{Tor}
and
\mathrm{Ext}
.
This a specific case of a filtration that we can consider in our article but our examples,
\mathrm{cuAs}
and
\mathrm{cCx}
, don’t fit in the context described in
[
Pos12
]
. It is not only because they are curved operads instead of curved algebras, but also because we are working over a field (
\mathbb{R}
) and in this case, the maximal ideal is zero so the curvature would necessarily be zero. This constraint is highlighted in the introduction of
[
Pos12
]
, paragraph 0.23.

The notion of weak equivalences defined in
[
CCN21
]
is similar to ours but the context is slightly different and the authors starts from operads which are augmented and connected.
It is not clear whether their results extend to our examples of curved operads.

In their recent paper
[
BL24
]
, Booth and Lazarev propose model category structures on the category of curved associative algebras and on the category of curved coassociative coalgebras.
These model category structures don’t rely on any filtration conditions on the curvature.
Nevertheless, they explain in the end of the introduction that their article do not extend directly to other kind of algebras or to symmetric operads.
Maybe more problematic, they restrict to curved coassociative coalgebras for which the comultiplication of an element is a finite sum.
Our Koszul dual cooperad does not satisfy this property.

Notations and conventions

We fix a field
\mathbf{K}
of characteristic 0.
We have to work over a field of characteristic 0 for two reasons: having a well-defined functor
\mathrm{Gr}
from complete filtered cooperads to complete graded cooperads and having model category structures on the involved categories (on the algebraic side: operads, algebras).
In the paper,
\mathbf{R}
will denote a unital
\mathbf{K}
-cdga (
commutative differential graded algebra
) and
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}
the category of dg-modules over
\mathbf{R}
.
The category
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}
is a Grothendieck category endowed with a closed symmetric monoidal structure given by the tensor product and we can apply the results of
[
BMDC20
]
.
We denote by
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{pg}}
the category of complete filtered predifferential graded (pg for short) objects in
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}
and by
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{gr}}
the category of complete filtered gr-dg objects in
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}
, called
\widehat{\Filt}^{\mathsf{gr}}\!\!(\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}})
in
[
BMDC20
]
.
The categories of complete filtered gr-dg modules are reflective subcategories of the categories of complete filtered predifferential graded objects.
An object
(M,F,d)
in
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{pg}}
is an object
M
in
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}

•

endowed with an
\mathbb{N}
-filtration
M=F_{0}M\supset F_{1}M\supset F_{2}M\supset\cdots
, and

•

complete
with respect to this filtration, that is
M=\lim_{n}M/F_{n}M
,

•

endowed with a
\mathbb{Z}
-cohomological degree
M^{\bullet}
and with an endomorphism
d:M^{\bullet}\to M^{\bullet+1}
of cohomological degree
1
called a
predifferential
.

Such an object
(M,F,d)
is more specifically in
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{gr}}
when

•

(M,F,d)
is
gr-dg
, that is the endomorphism
d
satisfies

d^{2}:F_{p}M\to F_{p+1}M

for all
p\geq 0
, or equivalently
d
induces a differential on the associated (
\mathbb{N}
-)graded
\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}M
defined by
\mathrm{Gr}_{p}M\coloneqq F_{p}M/F_{p+1}M
, for all
p\geq 0
.

Morphisms in
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{pg}}
(resp.
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{gr}}
) are morphisms in the category of graded objects in
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}
which respect the filtration and the cohomological degree and which commute with the predifferentials.

By abuse of notation, we often write only
M
instead of
(M,F,d)
for a complete dg object (or a complete gr-dg object).

In the following, the algebra
\mathbf{R}
might be dg and complete. We emphasize that in the dg setting, the module structure map
\rho_{M}
of an
\mathbf{R}
-module
M
doesn’t commute with the differential but satisfies the following compatibility relation

d_{M}\cdot\rho_{M}=\rho_{M}\cdot(d_{\mathbf{R}}\otimes_{\mathbf{K}}\operatorname{id}_{M}+\operatorname{id}_{\mathbf{R}}\otimes_{\mathbf{K}}d_{M}),

where
\otimes_{\mathbf{K}}
denotes the complete tensor product over the ring
\mathbf{K}
.

In this article, we use the notations
\mathcal{T}(M)
and
\mathcal{T}^{c}(M)
for the free operad and cofree cooperad given by trees indexed by element in
M
. The notations
\mathcal{T}(M)^{(k)}
and
\mathcal{T}^{c}(M)^{(k)}
stand for the trees with exactly
k
vertices.

0.1.
Sign conventions

We mainly work with homological conventions and we denote the homological degree
n
elements in a graded object
M
by
M_{n}
. However, for geometric examples, we use cohomological convention and we denote the cohomological degree
n
elements in
M
by
M^{n}
or
M[-n]
. We go from one convention to the other by
M^{n}=M[-n]=M_{-n}
.

The (homological) suspension of a graded object
M
is denoted by
sM=s\mathbf{K}\otimes M
where
s
is a homological degree 1 element. It follows that
(sM)_{n}=M_{n-1}
. Similarly we denote by
s^{-1}M
the (homological) desuspension of
M
. From a cohomological point of view, the element
s
has a cohomological degree equal to
-1
and the element
s^{-1}
has cohomological degree
1
.

In the whole article, the signs are computed by means of the Koszul sign rule, that is a sign appears when graded elements or maps are switched. For example given two maps
f
and
g
and two elements
v
and
w
in their respective domains, we have
(f\otimes g)(v\otimes w)=(-1)^{|g||v|}f(v)\otimes g(w)
where
|x|
denote the degree of the element or map
x
.

When working with homotopy algebras as
\mathrm{A}_{\infty}
-algebras or
\mathrm{L}_{\infty}
-algebras, we have to choose a convention on the signs appearing in the relations between the maps defining the
\mathrm{A}_{\infty}
structure (respectively the
\mathrm{L}_{\infty}
structure). For example, in the case of
\mathrm{A}_{\infty}
-algebras, as it is written in Part 1, Section 4.2 in
[
Maz21
]
, two possibilities are:

(0.1)

\displaystyle\sum_{p+q+r=n}(-1)^{pq+r}m_{n+1-q}\circ_{p+1}m_{q}=0

(0.2)

\displaystyle\sum_{p+q+r=n}(-1)^{p+qr}m_{n+1-q}\circ_{p+1}m_{q}=0.

The difference is induced by the sign convention of the homotopy for the associativity relation.
In the first situation we have:

\partial(m_{3})=m_{2}\circ_{1}m_{2}-m_{2}\circ_{2}m_{2}

whereas in the second one:

\partial(m_{3})=-m_{2}\circ_{1}m_{2}+m_{2}\circ_{2}m_{2}.

In our article, we follow the convention (
0.1
). This choice is different from the one made in some references that we use as
[
BMDC20
,
HM12
,
LV12
]
to cite a few.
We can go from one convention to the other by considering
(-1)^{\frac{(n-1)(n+2)}{2}}m_{n}
instead of
m_{n}
.
The main differences in our article are visible for (homotopy) curved Lie algebras:

•

the equation appearing in the definition of curved Lie algebras is
d^{2}=[\theta,-]
instead of
d^{2}=[-,\theta]
;

•

there is a similar sign difference in the formulas for homotopy curved Lie algebras and for the definition of curved coalgebras or cooperads;

•

a Maurer–Cartan element in a curved Lie algebra is a degree
-1
element satisfying the equation
\Theta+\partial\alpha+\frac{1}{2}[\alpha,\alpha]=0
instead of the equation
\partial\alpha+\frac{1}{2}[\alpha,\alpha]=\Theta
;

•

there is a similar difference for the definition of a Maurer–Cartan element in a homotopy curved Lie algebra.

Of course some signs appearing in the construction of this kind of objects likewise the bar construction of a curved operad or the convolution curved Lie algebra are adapted accordingly.
Our feeling is that these sign conventions are closer to the original definitions in the field.

1.
Operadic twisting morphisms in a curved context

In this section we are interested in building a cofibrant resolution of a curved operad.
When the operad
\mathcal{P}
is differentially graded (the curvature is zero), we are able to provide a cofibrant resolution of
\mathcal{P}
in
[
HM12
,
HM23
]
.
When the curved operad is augmented, we provide an
\mathbb{S}
-cofibrant resolution in
[
BMDC20
]
. We prove here that the bar-cobar resolution described in
[
BMDC20
]
is in fact cofibrant.

The bar-cobar adjunctions exchange the default of (co)augmentation and the curvature.
In order to define a model category structure on curved operads and curved algebras, we require a filtration condition on the curvature (
\theta\in F_{1}\mathcal{P}
) in
[
BMDC20
]
.
It isn’t possible to require such a condition relative to the default of augmentation of an operad or for the curvature of a curved cooperad.
The consequence is a small difference on the base categories on which we consider our (co)algebraic structures:

•

the base category for curved operads (or algebras over a curved operad) is
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{K}}^{\mathrm{gr}}
, and we have a model category structure on it,

•

the base category for curved cooperads is
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{K}}^{\mathrm{pg}}
, and we don’t provide a model category structure on it.

1.1.
Complete and curved context

Similarly as in
[
BMDC20
]
, we consider the categories
(\mathbb{S}\textrm{-}\mathsf{Mod}(\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{K}}^{\mathrm{pg}}),\hat{\circ},I)
of
\mathbb{S}
-modules in
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{K}}^{\mathrm{pg}}
and
(\mathbb{S}\textrm{-}\mathsf{Mod}(\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{K}}^{\mathrm{gr}}),\hat{\circ},I)
of
\mathbb{S}
-modules in
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{K}}^{\mathrm{gr}}
.
In particular, objects in this category are filtered and complete with respect to their filtration.

1.1.1.
Curved operad and cooperad

For operads and cooperads, we define two notions of morphisms.
The second one is a lax version of the first one and is a useful enlargement for representing the curved twisting morphisms by the bar and the cobar constructions.
The lax version is very close to the original definition of morphism between curved associative algebras given in
[
Pos93
]
.

Definition 1.1
.

•

A
curved operad

(\mathcal{P},\gamma,\eta,d,\theta)
is a gr-dg operad
(\mathcal{P},\gamma,\eta,d)
in the monoidal category
\mathbb{S}\textrm{-}\mathsf{Mod}(\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{K}}^{\mathrm{gr}})
equipped with a map
\theta:I\to(F_{1}\mathcal{P}(1))_{-2}
such that

(1)

d^{2}=[\theta,-]
,

(2)

d(\theta)=0
(
\theta
is
closed
).

•

A
morphism
f:(\mathcal{P},d,\theta)\rightarrow(\mathcal{Q},d^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})
of curved operads
is a morphism of gr-dg operads which preserves the curvature, that is satisfying the equations

\displaystyle f\cdot d

\displaystyle=d^{\prime}\cdot f\quad\textrm{ and}

\displaystyle f(\theta)

\displaystyle=\theta^{\prime}.

•

A
lax morphism

(\mathcal{P},d,\theta)\rightarrow(\mathcal{Q},d^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})
of curved operads is a pair
(f,a)
where
f:\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{Q}
is a morphism of complete operads and
a:I\to F_{1}\mathcal{Q}
is an
\mathbb{S}
-module map of degree
-1
such that

(1.1)

\displaystyle d^{\prime}\cdot f+[a,f]

\displaystyle=f\cdot d\quad\textrm{ and}

(1.2)

\displaystyle f(\theta)

\displaystyle=\theta^{\prime}+d^{\prime}\cdot a+\frac{1}{2}[a,a].

•

The composition of two lax morphisms
(g,b)
and
(f,a)
of curved operads is given by:
(g,b)\cdot(f,a)\coloneqq(g\cdot f,g(a)+b)
and the unit for the composition is
(\operatorname{id},0)
.

We denote by
\mathsf{cOp}
the category of curved operads with morphisms of curved operads and by
\mathsf{cOp}^{\mathrm{lax}}
the category of curved operads with lax morphisms of curved operads.

Lax morphisms admit a description as
strict
morphisms by twisting the predifferential and the curvature on the codomain curved operad.

Lemma 1.2
.

Let
(\mathcal{Q},\gamma^{\prime},d^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})
be a curved operad and
a:I\to F_{1}\mathcal{Q}
.

(1)

The tuple
(\mathcal{Q},\gamma^{\prime},d^{\prime}_{a}\coloneqq d^{\prime}+[a,-],\theta^{\prime}_{a}\coloneqq\theta^{\prime}+d^{\prime}\cdot a+\frac{1}{2}[a,a])
is a curved operad that we denote
\mathcal{Q}_{a}
.
In particular,
d^{\prime}_{a}
is a derivation with respect to
\gamma^{\prime}
,
\theta^{\prime}_{a}\in F_{1}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}
is closed and it satisfies

{d^{\prime}_{a}}^{2}=\left[\theta^{\prime}_{a},-\right].

(2)

The data of a lax morphism
(f,a):(\mathcal{P},d,\theta)\rightarrow(\mathcal{Q},d^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})
of curved operads is equivalent to the data of a morphism
f:(\mathcal{P},d,\theta)\rightarrow(\mathcal{Q},d^{\prime}_{a},\theta^{\prime}_{a})
of curved operads.

Proof.

(1)

We first check that
d^{\prime}_{a}
is a derivation with respect to
\gamma^{\prime}
.
It is enough to show that
[a,-]
is a derivation with respect to
\gamma^{\prime}
.
We emphasize that this is true only with
a
in arity 1 (
a\in\mathcal{Q}(1)
).
The Lie bracket
[-,-]
on
\oplus_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{Q}(n)
used above is the anti-symmetrisation of a pre-Lie product that we denote
\{-,-\}
.
With the notations
\epsilon_{i}=(-1)^{|\mu|+|\nu_{1}|+\cdots+|\nu_{i-1}|}
, we have

\gamma^{\prime}\left([a,\mu];\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{k}\right)+\sum_{i}\epsilon_{i}\gamma^{\prime}\left(\mu;\nu_{1},\ldots,[a,\nu_{i}],\ldots\nu_{k}\right)\\
=\gamma^{\prime}\left(\{a,\mu\};\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{k}\right)+(-1)^{|\mu|}\gamma^{\prime}\left(\{\mu,a\};\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{k}\right)\\
+\sum_{i}\epsilon_{i}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\left(\mu;\nu_{1},\ldots,\{a,\nu_{i}\},\ldots\nu_{k}\right)+(-1)^{|\nu_{i}|}\gamma^{\prime}\left(\mu;\nu_{1},\ldots,\{\nu_{i},a\},\ldots\nu_{k}\right)\right)\\
=\gamma^{\prime}\left(\{a,\mu\};\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{k}\right)+\epsilon_{k}\{\gamma^{\prime}\left(\mu;\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{k}\right),a\}\\
=[a,\gamma^{\prime}\left(\mu;\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{k}\right)]

by the associativity of
\gamma^{\prime}
.

We have
\theta^{\prime}_{a}=\theta^{\prime}+d^{\prime}\cdot a+\frac{1}{2}[a,a]:I\to F_{1}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}
since
a:I\to F_{1}\mathcal{Q}_{-1}
and
\theta^{\prime}_{a}:I\to F_{1}\mathcal{Q}_{-2}
.
It is closed since

d^{\prime}_{a}(\theta^{\prime}+d^{\prime}a+\frac{1}{2}[a,a])=\\
d^{\prime}\theta^{\prime}+[a,\theta^{\prime}]+{d^{\prime}}^{2}a+[a,d^{\prime}a]+\frac{1}{2}(d^{\prime}[a,a]+[a,[a,a]])=0

since
\theta^{\prime}
is
d^{\prime}
-closed,
{d^{\prime}}^{2}a=[\theta^{\prime},a]
,
d^{\prime}
is a derivation with respect to
[-,-]
, and
[-,-]
satisfies the Jacobi relation.

Then we compute

\displaystyle{d^{\prime}_{a}}^{2}

\displaystyle=d^{\prime}_{a}(d^{\prime}+[a,-])={d^{\prime}}^{2}+d^{\prime}[a,-]+[a,d^{\prime}-]+[a,[a,-]]

\displaystyle=[\theta^{\prime},-]+[d^{\prime}(a),-]+\left[\frac{1}{2}[a,a],-\right]\quad\text{($d^{\prime}$ is a derivation for $[,]$)}

\displaystyle=\left[\theta^{\prime}+d^{\prime}(a)+\frac{1}{2}[a,a],-\right]=[\theta^{\prime}_{a},-].

This proves that
\mathcal{Q}_{a}
is a curved operad.

(2)

A lax morphism of curved operads
(f,a):(\mathcal{P},d,\theta)\rightarrow(\mathcal{Q},d^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})
is the data of a morphism of the underlying graded operads
f:\mathcal{P}\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}
and a map
a:I\to F_{1}\mathcal{Q}
such that
d^{\prime}_{a}f=d^{\prime}\cdot f+[a,f]=f\cdot d
and
f(\theta)=\theta^{\prime}+d^{\prime}\cdot a+\frac{1}{2}[a,a]=\theta^{\prime}_{a}
.
It is therefore equivalently the data of a morphism of curved operads
f:(\mathcal{P},d,\theta)\rightarrow(\mathcal{Q},d^{\prime}_{a},\theta^{\prime}_{a})
.

∎

Example 1.3
.

A first example of curved operad is given by the
endomorphism operad
of a complete gr-dg
\mathbf{R}
-module
(M,d)

\mathrm{End}_{M}\coloneqq(\{\mathrm{Hom}(M^{\hat{\otimes}n},M)\}_{n\geq 0},\gamma,\partial,\theta)

where
\mathrm{Hom}(M^{\hat{\otimes}n},M)
is seen as a
\mathbf{K}
-module, the composition map
\gamma
is given by the composition of functions and

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lclcr}\partial(f)&\coloneqq&[d,f]&=&d\cdot f-(-1)^{|f|}\sum_{i}f\cdot(\operatorname{id}_{M},\ldots,d,\ldots,\operatorname{id}_{M}),\\
&&&&\textrm{for }f\in\mathrm{Hom}(M^{\hat{\otimes}n},M),\\
\theta&\coloneqq&d^{2}.\end{array}\right.

Definition 1.4
.

Let
(\mathcal{P},\gamma,\eta,d,\theta)
be a curved operad.
A structure of
(\mathcal{P},\gamma,\eta,d,\theta)
-algebra (
\mathcal{P}
-algebra for short when there is no ambiguity) on the gr-dg module
(A,d_{A})
is a curved operad morphism

\gamma_{A}:(\mathcal{P},\gamma,\eta,d,\theta)\to\mathrm{End}_{(A,d_{A})}.

Explicitly
A
is endowed with a
(\mathcal{P},\gamma,\eta,d)
-algebra structure and the gr-dg endomorphism
d_{A}:A\to A
of degree
-1
is a predifferential (with respect to the
\mathcal{P}
-algebra structure) such that

{d_{A}}^{2}=\gamma_{A}(\theta\circ\operatorname{id}_{A}).

(The notion of
predifferential of a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
is recalled in Definition
3.4
.)

Example 1.5
.

Given a gr-dg module
(A,d_{A})
, the identity map
\operatorname{id}_{\mathrm{End}_{(A,d_{A})}}
provides a structure of
\mathrm{End}_{(A,d_{A})}
-algebra on
(A,d_{A})
.

We define dually the notion of curved cooperad.

Definition 1.6
.

•

A
curved cooperad

(\mathcal{C},\Delta,\varepsilon,d,\theta^{c})
is a pg cooperad
(\mathcal{C},\Delta,\varepsilon,d)
in the monoidal category
\mathbb{S}\textrm{-}\mathsf{Mod}(\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{K}}^{\mathrm{pg}})
endowed with a
curvature

\theta^{c}
, that is a map
\theta^{c}:\mathcal{C}\to I
of degree
-2
such that

(1)

d^{2}=(\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{C}}\otimes\theta^{c}-\theta^{c}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{C}})\circ\Delta_{(1)}
,

(2)

\theta^{c}\cdot d=0
,

where
\Delta_{(1)}
is the infinitesimal decomposition map defined in
[
LV12
, section 6.1.4]
.
The map
\Delta:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}\hat{\circ}\mathcal{C}
is called the
decomposition map
, the map
\varepsilon:\mathcal{C}\to I
is the
counit map
.

•

A
morphism
(\mathcal{C},d_{\mathcal{C}},\theta^{c})\rightarrow(\mathcal{C}^{\prime},d_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}},{\theta^{c}}^{\prime})
of curved cooperads
are morphisms of pg cooperads which preserve the curvature, that is satisfying the equations

\displaystyle d^{\prime}\cdot f

\displaystyle=f\cdot d\quad\textrm{ and}

\displaystyle{\theta^{c}}^{\prime}\cdot f

\displaystyle=\theta^{c}.

•

A
lax morphism

(\mathcal{C},d_{\mathcal{C}},\theta^{c})\rightarrow(\mathcal{C}^{\prime},d_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}},{\theta^{c}}^{\prime})
of curved cooperads is a pair
(f,a)
where
f:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is a morphism of complete cooperads and
a^{c}:\mathcal{C}\to I
is a degree
-1

\mathbb{S}
-module map such that

(1.3)

\displaystyle d_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}\cdot f

\displaystyle=f\cdot d_{\mathcal{C}}+(f\otimes a^{c}-a^{c}\otimes f)\cdot\Delta_{(1)}\quad\textrm{ and}

(1.4)

\displaystyle{\theta^{c}}^{\prime}\cdot f

\displaystyle=\theta^{c}+a^{c}\cdot d+a^{c}\star a^{c},

with
a^{c}\star a^{c}:=\gamma_{I}\cdot(a^{c}\otimes a^{c})\cdot\Delta_{(1)}
.

•

The composition of two lax morphisms
(g,b^{c})
and
(f,a^{c})
of curved operads is given by:
(g,b^{c})\cdot(f,a^{c})\coloneqq(g\cdot f,a^{c}+b^{c}\cdot f)
and the unit for the composition is
(\operatorname{id},0)
.

Remark 1.7
.

We emphasize that there is no filtration assumption on the curvature
\theta^{c}
of a curved cooperad.

We recall from
[
HM12
]
(up to the sign) the definition of
(\mathcal{C},d_{\mathcal{C}},\theta_{\mathcal{C}})
-coalgebra over a curved cooperad.

Definition 1.8
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},\Delta,\varepsilon,d,\theta^{c})
be a curved cooperad. A
(\mathcal{C},\Delta,\varepsilon,d,\theta^{c})
-coalgebra
(
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra for short) is a triple
(C,\Delta_{C},d_{C})
where
(C,\Delta_{C},d_{C})
is a
(\mathcal{C},\Delta,\varepsilon,d)
-coalgebra in
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{pg}}
and the predifferential
d_{C}:C\to C
(of degree
-1
) satisfies:

{d_{C}}^{2}=-(\theta^{c}\circ id_{C})\cdot\Delta_{C}.

(The notion of
predifferential of a
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra
is recalled in Definition
3.4
.)

1.1.2.
Semi-augmentation

Following
[
HM12
]
, we define the notion of curved semi-augmented operad.

Definition 1.9
.

•

A
curved semi-augmented operad
, or
curved sa operad
for short,
(\mathcal{P},\gamma,\eta,d,\theta,\varepsilon)
is a curved operad endowed with an augmentation of (complete)
\mathbb{S}
-modules
\varepsilon:\mathcal{P}\to I
.

•

A
morphism between two curved sa operads

(\mathcal{P},\varepsilon)\xrightarrow{f}(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime})
is a morphism of the underlying curved operads.

1.1.3.
Altipotence

We now recall the notion of altipotence from
[
BMDC20
]
.
The
gr-flat
condition required is automatically satisfied since our base field is of characteristic 0.
The definitions, given for dg complete cooperads in the above reference, only depend on the underlying complete cooperad. The definitions apply therefore to curved (complete) cooperads without modifications.

Definition 1.10
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},\Delta,\varepsilon,d,\theta^{c})
be a curved cooperad.

•

A
gr-coaugmentation

\eta:I\to\mathcal{C}
is a map of complete
\mathbb{S}
-modules, such that applying the graded functor
\mathrm{Gr}
to
(\mathcal{C},\Delta,\varepsilon,d,\theta^{c},\eta)
provides a coaugmented curved cooperad.

•

In the context of complete gr-coaugmented curved cooperads, we call
morphism of complete gr-coaugmented curved cooperads
a morphism of curved cooperads which commutes with the gr-coaugmentations after applying the graded functor
\mathrm{Gr}
.

The
infinitesimal coideal
of
\eta
, notated
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}
, is the complete
\mathbb{S}
-module which is the pushout (in complete objects)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{I[-1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

We denote by
\widetilde{\eta}
the map
I[-1]\to\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}
in the pushout.

Remark 1.11
.

Since the filtration on
I
is the trivial filtration

F_{0}I=I\supset F_{1}I=0\supset\cdots

we obtain that a gr-coaugmentation is in fact a coaugmentation on the level of complete
\mathbb{S}
-modules.
Following the terminology of the previous section, it is a
semi-coaugmentation
.

Definition 1.12
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},\Delta,\varepsilon,d,\theta^{c},\eta)
be a gr-coaugmented curved cooperad.

•

We define the
reduced decomposition map

\bar{\Delta}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}\hat{\circ}\mathcal{C}
by

\bar{\Delta}\coloneqq\Delta-((\eta\cdot\varepsilon)\circ\operatorname{id})\cdot\Delta-(\operatorname{id}\circ(\eta\cdot\varepsilon))\cdot\Delta+((\eta\cdot\varepsilon)\circ(\eta\cdot\varepsilon))\cdot\Delta.

•

We define a bigraded collection
\mathfrak{cr}^{p,n}\mathcal{C}
of subcollections of
\mathcal{C}
recursively as follows.
For
p,n\in\mathbb{Z}
, we fix
\mathfrak{cr}^{p,0}\mathcal{C}\coloneqq F_{p}\mathcal{C}
and
\mathfrak{cr}^{0,n}\mathcal{C}\coloneqq F_{0}\mathcal{C}
.
Next, supposing that
\mathfrak{cr}^{p^{\prime},n^{\prime}}\mathcal{C}
is defined for
0\leq n^{\prime}<n
and
0\leq p^{\prime}\leq p
, we first define a subobject
\widetilde{\mathfrak{cr}}^{p,n}(\mathcal{C}\hat{\circ}\mathcal{C})
of
\mathcal{C}\hat{\circ}\mathcal{C}
. We define it as the sub-object in
\mathcal{C}\hat{\circ}\mathcal{C}

\hat{\bigoplus}_{m\geq 0}\hat{\sum}_{p_{0}+\cdots+p_{m}+\delta=p}F_{p_{0}}\mathcal{C}(m)\hat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{S}_{m}}\hat{\bigotimes}_{i=1}^{m}F_{p_{i}}\mathfrak{cr}^{p_{i}+\delta,n-1}\mathcal{C},

where the integers
p_{i}
are non negative.
Then define
\mathfrak{cr}^{p,n}\mathcal{C}
as the following pullback:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{cr}^{p,n}\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{cr}}^{p,n}(\mathcal{C}\hat{\circ}\mathcal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hat{\circ}\mathcal{C}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\Delta}}

Recursively, there are natural inclusions
\mathfrak{cr}^{p,n}\mathcal{C}\to\mathfrak{cr}^{p-1,n}\mathcal{C}
and
\mathfrak{cr}^{p,n}\mathcal{C}\to\mathfrak{cr}^{p,n+1}\mathcal{C}
.

Definition 1.13
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a gr-coaugmented curved cooperad.

•

For
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, we define the
k
-primitives
of
\mathcal{C}
to be the
\mathbb{S}
-module:

\mathrm{Prim}_{k}\mathcal{C}\coloneqq\bigcap_{p\geq 0}\mathfrak{cr}^{p+k,p}\mathcal{C},

where we fix
\mathfrak{cr}^{p+k,p}\mathcal{C}=\mathfrak{cr}^{0,p}\mathcal{C}=F_{0}\mathcal{C}
when
p+k\leq 0
.

•

We call
\mathcal{C}

altipotent
when the following conditions are satisfied

(1)

the map

\colim_{n}\mathfrak{cr}^{p,n}\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}

is an isomorphism for all
p
,

(2)

the coaugmentation
\eta(I)
lies in
\mathrm{Prim}_{0}\mathcal{C}
, and

(3)

for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}
we have

(
\ast
)

\displaystyle\bar{\Delta}(\mathrm{Prim}_{k}\mathcal{C})\subset\hat{\bigoplus}_{m}\hat{\sum}_{k_{0}+\cdots+k_{m}=k+1}F_{k_{0}}\mathcal{C}(m)\hat{\otimes}\left(\mathrm{Prim}_{k_{1}}\mathcal{C}\hat{\otimes}\cdots\hat{\otimes}\mathrm{Prim}_{k_{m}}\mathcal{C}\right)

where we set
F_{k_{0}}\mathcal{C}=F_{0}\mathcal{C}
when
k_{0}\leq 0
,

We recall that the infinitesimal coideal is left adjoint to the tree cooperad.

Proposition 1.14

(Corollary 3.34 in
[
BMDC20
]
)
.

The tree cooperad, viewed as a functor from
\mathbb{S}
-modules to altipotent cooperads, is right adjoint to the infinitesimal coideal.
That is, the tree cooperad is cofree in the category of altipotent complete cooperads and in particular, there are bijections, natural in
\mathcal{C}
and
M

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{S}\textsf{-}\mathsf{mod}^{\mathrm{pg}}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}},M)\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{alt.\,coop.}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{T}^{c}(M)).

1.2.
Bar and cobar constructions in the unital and curved context

We describe a bar construction in the context of curved semi-augmented operads.
Similarly we provide a cobar construction associated with curved altipotent cooperads.
Then we obtain a bar-cobar adjunction in this context.
These constructions are similar to the ones given by Lyubashenko in
[
Lyu14
, Sections 5.3 and 5.4]
but slightly different since he doesn’t have an adjunction.

We use the free and cofree constructions described in
[
BMDC20
]
.

1.2.1.
Bar construction of a curved sa operad

Let
(\mathcal{P},\gamma,\eta,d,\theta,\varepsilon)
be a curved sa operad with
\overline{\mathcal{P}}\coloneqq\ker\varepsilon
.
Its
bar construction

\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}
is given by the curved altipotent cooperad

\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}\coloneqq\left(\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}}),\Delta_{\beta},\varepsilon_{\beta},d_{\beta}\coloneqq d_{0}+d_{1}+d_{2},\theta^{c}_{\beta},\eta_{\beta}\right),

where
\Delta_{\beta}
is the cofree decomposition map,
\varepsilon_{\beta}
is the usual counit,
\eta_{\beta}
is the usual injection (
I\rightarrowtail\mathcal{T}^{c}M
, it will be a gr-coaugmentation only), the curvature
\theta^{c}_{\beta}
is the composition

\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})\twoheadrightarrow s\overline{\mathcal{P}}\oplus s^{2}(\overline{\mathcal{P}}\hat{\circ}_{(1)}\overline{\mathcal{P}})\xrightarrow{s^{-1}\otimes d+s^{-2}\otimes\gamma_{(1)}}\mathcal{P}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}I

and the different coderivations
d_{0}
,
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
are respectively the unique coderivations which extend the degree
-1
maps:

•

\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})\twoheadrightarrow I\xrightarrow{-s\theta}s\overline{\mathcal{P}}
,

•

\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})\twoheadrightarrow s\overline{\mathcal{P}}\xrightarrow{\bar{d}}s\overline{\mathcal{P}}
,

•

\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})\twoheadrightarrow s\overline{\mathcal{P}}\hat{\circ}_{(1)}s\overline{\mathcal{P}}\xrightarrow{s^{-1}\bar{\gamma}_{(1)}}s\overline{\mathcal{P}}
.

Proposition 1.15
.

The curvature
\theta^{c}_{\beta}
has degree
-2
and is closed with respect to the coderivation
d_{\beta}
. The coderivation satisfies

(1.5)

{d_{\beta}}^{2}=(\operatorname{id}\otimes\theta^{c}_{\beta}-\theta^{c}_{\beta}\otimes\operatorname{id})\cdot{\Delta_{\beta}}_{(1)}.

Moreover the bar construction induces a functor

\hat{\mathrm{B}}:\mathsf{Curved\ sa\ op.}^{\mathrm{lax}}\rightarrow\mathsf{Curved.\ altip.\ coop.}^{\mathrm{lax}}.

Proof.

To prove Equation (
1.5
), we first adapt the proof of Lemma 3.3.3 in
[
HM12
]
in order to take the curvature into account.
The map
\theta^{c}_{\beta}
has degree
-2
by definition.
Because
\theta^{c}_{\beta}
is zero on
I\oplus\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(>2)}
, to get the equality
\theta^{c}_{\beta}\cdot d_{\beta}=0
, it is enough to notice the following equalities:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}\theta^{c}_{\beta}\cdot{d_{0}}_{|I}&=&\varepsilon\cdot d\cdot\theta=0\quad(\text{since $\theta$ is closed}),\\
\theta^{c}_{\beta}\cdot(d_{0}+d_{1})_{|s\overline{\mathcal{P}}}&=&\varepsilon\cdot(-[\theta,s^{-1}-]+d^{2}(s^{-1}-))=0,\\
\theta^{c}_{\beta}\cdot(d_{1}+d_{2})_{|\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(2)}}&=&\varepsilon\cdot\gamma_{(1)}\cdot\big((\bar{d}-d)\otimes\operatorname{id}+\operatorname{id}\otimes(\bar{d}-d)\big)(s^{-2}-)=0,\\
\theta^{c}_{\beta}\cdot{d_{2}}_{|\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(3)}}&=&\varepsilon\cdot\big(\gamma_{(1)}(\bar{\gamma}_{(1)}(-,-),-)-\gamma_{(1)}(-,\bar{\gamma}_{(1)}(-,-))\big)(s^{-3}-)\\
&=&0.\end{array}\right.

The maps
{d_{\beta}}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}[d_{\beta},d_{\beta}]
and
(\operatorname{id}\otimes\theta^{c}_{\beta}-\theta^{c}_{\beta}\otimes\operatorname{id})\cdot{\Delta_{\beta}}_{(1)}
are coderivations (see
[
HM12
, Lemma 3.2.2]
for the second statement), so it is enough to prove that the projection to the cogenerators
s\overline{\mathcal{P}}
of Equation (
1.5
) is satisfied.
We have

(d_{0}+d_{1}+d_{2})^{2}=\underbrace{{d_{0}}^{2}}+\underbrace{d_{0}d_{1}+d_{1}d_{0}}+\underbrace{{d_{1}}^{2}+d_{0}d_{2}+d_{2}d_{0}}+\underbrace{d_{1}d_{2}+d_{2}d_{1}}+\underbrace{{d_{2}}^{2}}.

The term
({d_{0}}^{2})^{|s\overline{\mathcal{P}}}
is zero since
\im({d_{0}}^{2})\subset\mathcal{T}^{(\geq 2)}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})
.
We have
(d_{0}d_{1}+d_{1}d_{0})^{|s\overline{\mathcal{P}}}=(d_{1}d_{0})^{|s\overline{\mathcal{P}}}=d\cdot\theta=0
since
\theta
is closed.
The term
({d_{1}}^{2})^{|s\overline{\mathcal{P}}}
corresponds to
{\overline{d}}^{2}
and the term
(d_{0}d_{2}+d_{2}d_{0})^{|s\overline{\mathcal{P}}}=(d_{2}d_{0})^{|s\overline{\mathcal{P}}}
is equal to
-\overline{[\theta,-]}=-\overline{d^{2}}
on
s\overline{\mathcal{P}}
so
({d_{1}}^{2}+d_{0}d_{2}+d_{2}d_{0})^{|s\overline{\mathcal{P}}}=0
.
Then we have

\displaystyle(d_{1}d_{2}+d_{2}d_{1})^{|s\overline{\mathcal{P}}}=-s(\bar{\gamma}_{(1)})((d-\bar{d})\otimes\operatorname{id}+\operatorname{id}\otimes(d-\bar{d}))(s^{-2}-)^{|s\overline{\mathcal{P}}}_{|\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(2)}}

\displaystyle=-s(\bar{\gamma}_{(1)})((\varepsilon\cdot d)\otimes\operatorname{id}+\operatorname{id}\otimes(\varepsilon\cdot d))(s^{-2}-)^{|s\overline{\mathcal{P}}}_{|\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(2)}}

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{id}\otimes\theta^{c}_{\beta}-\theta^{c}_{\beta}\otimes\operatorname{id})\cdot{\Delta_{\beta}}_{(1)|\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(2)}}.

Finally

\displaystyle({d_{2}}^{2})^{|s\overline{\mathcal{P}}}=

\displaystyle s\left(\bar{\gamma}_{(1)}(\gamma_{(1)}(-,-),-)-\bar{\gamma}_{(1)}(-,\gamma_{(1)}(-,-))\right)(s^{-3}-)^{|s\overline{\mathcal{P}}}_{|\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(3)}}

\displaystyle-(\theta^{c}_{\beta}\otimes\operatorname{id}-\operatorname{id}\otimes\theta^{c}_{\beta})\cdot{\Delta_{\beta}}_{(1)|\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(3)}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\operatorname{id}\otimes\theta^{c}_{\beta}-\theta^{c}_{\beta}\otimes\operatorname{id}\right)\cdot{\Delta_{\beta}}_{(1)|\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(3)}}

since
\gamma
is associative.
It follows that Equation (
1.5
) is satisfied.

The bar construction is gr-coaugmented since
d_{\beta}(1)=d_{0}(1)=-s\theta\in F_{1}s\overline{\mathcal{P}}\subset F_{1}\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}
and it is altipotent since it is built by means of the cofree altipotent construction.

It remains to prove that
\hat{\mathrm{B}}
sends lax morphisms of curved operads to lax morphisms of curved cooperads and that it preserves composition of lax morphisms.
Let
(f,a):(\mathcal{P},d,\varepsilon,\theta)\rightarrow(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},d^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})
be a lax morphism of sa curved operads. The map of
\mathbb{S}
-modules underlying
f
is characterized by the morphism of
\mathbb{S}
-modules
\bar{f}:\overline{\mathcal{P}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}
and the morphism
\varepsilon^{\prime}\cdot(s^{-1}f):s\overline{\mathcal{P}}\to I
.
Because the tree cooperad is cofree in the category of altipotent cooperads (Proposition
1.14
), the map

\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{I}\oplus s\overline{\mathcal{P}}\xrightarrow{a+\bar{f}}s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}

induces a cooperad morphism
\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a):\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})\to\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}})
.
Moreover
\varepsilon^{\prime}\cdot(s^{-1}f)
induces a map
a^{c}(f):\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})\to I
by precomposition by the projection to
s\overline{\mathcal{P}}
.
We now show that the couple
(\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a),a^{c}(f))
is a lax morphism of curved cooperads.
We abbreviate the map
a^{c}(f)
by
a^{c}
when there is no ambiguity.

First, we prove Equation (
1.3
).
The cooperad
\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})
can be seen as an infinitesimal
\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}})
-comodule by means of the cooperad morphism
\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a)
.
Noting
d_{\beta}
, resp.
d^{\prime}_{\beta}
, the coderivation on
\mathcal{T}^{c}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{P}})
, resp. on
\mathcal{T}^{c}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}})
, the map
d_{\beta}^{\prime}\cdot\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a)-\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a)\cdot d_{\beta}
is a coderivation
\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})\to\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}})
. (This is a direct computation for two coderivations and a cooperad morphism.)
Similarly, the map
(\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a)\otimes a^{c}-a^{c}\otimes\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a))\cdot\Delta_{(1)}
is a coderivation
\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})\to\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}})
by Lemma 3.2.2 in
[
HM12
]
.
It follows that it is enough to prove the corestriction of Equation (
1.3
) to the generators of the cooperad in the codomain (by means of Lemma 15 in
[
MV09
]
).
The morphism
f
satisfies Equations (
1.1
) and (
1.2
) so by considering the projection to
\overline{\mathcal{P}}
, we get

\bar{d}^{\prime}\bar{f}+\overline{[a,\bar{f}]}=\bar{f}\bar{d}\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\bar{f}(\theta)=\theta^{\prime}+\bar{d}^{\prime}a+\frac{1}{2}\overline{[a,a]},

with
\overline{[a,f]}=\overline{[a,\bar{f}]}
since
\eta^{\prime}(1)
is central.
We compute the contributory terms as in Lemma 4.4 in
[
BMDC20
]
, using Construction 3.10.
The image in
I\oplus s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}\oplus\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}})^{(2)}\oplus\dots
of the morphism
\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a)
is equal to

\displaystyle\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a)(1)=1+sa+sa\otimes sa+\dots,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a)(s\mu)=s\bar{f}(\mu)+\sum_{i}s\bar{f}(\mu)\circ_{i}sa+sa\otimes s\bar{f}(\mu)+\dots,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a)(s\mu\circ_{j}s\nu)=s\bar{f}(\mu)\circ_{j}s\bar{f}(\nu)+\dots,

for any
\mu,\nu\in\overline{\mathcal{P}}
.
We therefore obtain

\displaystyle\left(d_{\beta}^{\prime}\cdot\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a)-\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a)\cdot d_{\beta}\right)_{|I}^{|s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}}=s\left(\bar{f}(\theta)-\theta^{\prime}-\bar{d}^{\prime}a-\frac{1}{2}\overline{[a,a]}\right)=0,

\displaystyle\left(d_{\beta}^{\prime}\cdot\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a)-\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a)\cdot d_{\beta}\right)_{|s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}}^{|s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}}=-s\left(\bar{d}^{\prime}\bar{f}(-)+\overline{[a,\bar{f}(-)]}-\bar{f}\bar{d}(-)\right)=0,

\displaystyle\left(d_{\beta}^{\prime}\cdot\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a)-\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a)\cdot d_{\beta}\right)_{|\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}})^{(2)}}^{|s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}}=\left(\bar{f}\otimes a^{c}-a^{c}\otimes\bar{f}\right)\cdot\overline{\Delta}_{(1)}.

We conclude that
\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a)
satisfies Equation (
1.3
), that is

(1.6)

d_{\beta}^{\prime}\cdot\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a)-\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a)\cdot d_{\beta}=(\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a)\otimes a^{c}-a^{c}\otimes\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a))\cdot\Delta_{(1)}.

For the second equation (that is (
1.4
)), it is enough to check it on
I\oplus s\overline{\mathcal{P}}\oplus\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(2)}
because of the definition of the maps involved.
On
I
, we have:

\left({\theta^{\prime}_{\beta}}^{c}\cdot\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a)-\theta_{\beta}^{c}-a^{c}d_{\beta}-a^{c}\star a^{c}\right)(1)\\
={\theta^{\prime}_{\beta}}^{c}(1+sa+sa\otimes sa)-0-a^{c}(-s\theta)-0\\
=\varepsilon^{\prime}\left(-d^{\prime}a-\frac{1}{2}[a,a]+f(\theta)\right)=\varepsilon^{\prime}(\theta^{\prime})=0.

On
s\mu\in s\overline{\mathcal{P}}
, we compute:

\left({\theta^{\prime}_{\beta}}^{c}\cdot\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a)-\theta_{\beta}^{c}-a^{c}d_{\beta}-a^{c}\star a^{c}\right)(s\mu)\\
={\theta^{\prime}_{\beta}}^{c}\left(s\bar{f}(\mu)+sa\otimes s\bar{f}(\mu)+\sum_{j}s\bar{f}(\mu)\circ_{j}sa\right)+\varepsilon d\mu-a^{c}(-s\bar{d}\mu)-0\\
=\varepsilon^{\prime}\left(-d^{\prime}\bar{f}(\mu)-[a,\bar{f}(\mu)]+f(\bar{d}\mu)\right)+\varepsilon d\mu\\
=-\varepsilon^{\prime}(f\eta\varepsilon d(\mu))+\varepsilon d\mu=-\varepsilon^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}\varepsilon d(\mu)+\varepsilon d\mu=0.

Finally, on
s\mu\circ_{j}s\nu\in\mathcal{T}^{c}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(2)}
, we have

\left({\theta^{\prime}_{\beta}}^{c}\cdot\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a)-\theta_{\beta}^{c}-a^{c}d_{\beta}-a^{c}\star a^{c}\right)(s\mu\circ_{j}s\nu)\\
={\theta^{\prime}_{\beta}}^{c}\left(s\bar{f}(\mu)\circ_{j}s\bar{f}(\nu)\right)-(-1)^{|\mu|}\left(\varepsilon\gamma(\mu\circ_{j}\nu)+a^{c}(s\bar{\gamma}(\mu\circ_{j}\nu))-\varepsilon^{\prime}f(\mu)\varepsilon^{\prime}f(\nu)\right)\\
=(-1)^{|\mu|}\left(\varepsilon^{\prime}f(\gamma(\mu\circ_{j}\nu))-\varepsilon\gamma(\mu\circ_{j}\nu)-\varepsilon^{\prime}f\bar{\gamma}(\mu\circ_{j}\nu)\right)=0.

It follows that Equation (
1.4
) is satisfied and the bar construction sends lax morphisms to lax morphisms.

We now prove that the bar construction preserves the composition of lax morphisms.
Let

(f,a):(\mathcal{P},d,\varepsilon,\theta)\rightarrow(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},d^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\text{ and }(g,b):(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},d^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\rightarrow(\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime},d^{\prime\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\theta^{\prime\prime})

be two lax morphisms of sa curved operads.
We denote by
a^{c}=a^{c}(f)
and
b^{c}=a^{c}(g)
the maps appearing in the definition of the bar construction.
We have

(\hat{\mathrm{B}}(g,b),b^{c})\cdot(\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a),a^{c})=(\hat{\mathrm{B}}(g,b)\cdot\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a),a^{c}+b^{c}\cdot\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a))

that we want to compare to

(\hat{\mathrm{B}}(g\cdot f,b+g(a)),a^{c}(g\cdot f)).

The morphism
\hat{\mathrm{B}}(g,b)\cdot\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a)
is characterised by its projection to the cogenerators
s\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}
which is given by
(b+\bar{g}(a))+\bar{g}\cdot\bar{f}=b+g(a)+\overline{g\cdot f}
(since
a\in F_{1}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
).
On the other side, the morphism
\hat{\mathrm{B}}(g\cdot f,g(a)+b)
is characterised by
g(a)+b+\overline{g\cdot f}
.
Thus they coincide.
Similarly,
a^{c}+b^{c}\cdot\hat{\mathrm{B}}(f,a)
is non zero only on
I\oplus s\overline{\mathcal{P}}
where it is equal to
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}(g(a)+g(\bar{f}))+\varepsilon^{\prime}(f)=\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\cdot g\cdot\bar{f}+\varepsilon^{\prime}\cdot f=\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\cdot g\cdot f
since
a\in F_{1}\mathcal{P}
.
This coincides with
a^{c}(g\cdot f)
.
This proves that the bar construction is a functor and this concludes the proof.
∎

1.2.2.
Cobar construction of a curved altipotent cooperad

We consider now a curved altipotent cooperad
(\mathcal{C},\Delta,\varepsilon,d,\theta^{c},\eta)
with
\overline{\mathcal{C}}\coloneqq\ker\varepsilon
.
Using the gr-coaugmentation
\eta
, we obtain a projection map
\mathcal{C}\twoheadrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{C}}
that we use to define the maps
\bar{d}:\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{C}\twoheadrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{C}}
and
\bar{\Delta}_{(1)}
(defined similarly).

The
cobar construction

\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{C}
is given by the curved semi-augmented operad

\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{C}\coloneqq\left(\mathcal{T}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}),\gamma_{\omega},\eta_{\omega},d_{\omega}\coloneqq D_{0}+D_{1}+D_{2},\theta_{\omega},\varepsilon_{\omega}\right),

where
\gamma_{\omega}
is the free composition map,
\eta_{\omega}
is the usual unit,
\varepsilon_{\omega}
is the usual projection (
\mathcal{T}M\twoheadrightarrow I
), the curvature
\theta_{\omega}
is the composite

I\xrightarrow{\eta}\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{-s^{-1}\bar{d}-s^{-2}\bar{\Delta}_{(1)}}s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}\oplus s^{-2}\overline{\mathcal{C}}\hat{\circ}_{(1)}\overline{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrowtail\mathcal{T}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}})

and the different derivations
D_{0}
,
D_{1}
and
D_{2}
are respectively the unique derivations which extend the degree
-1
maps:

•

s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}\xrightarrow{-s\theta^{c}}I\rightarrowtail\mathcal{T}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}})
,

•

s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}\xrightarrow{\bar{d}}s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrowtail\mathcal{T}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}})
,

•

s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}\xrightarrow{\Delta_{s^{-1}}\cdot\bar{\Delta}_{(1)}}s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}\hat{\circ}_{(1)}s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrowtail\mathcal{T}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}})
, where
\Delta_{s^{-1}}(s^{-1})=-s^{-2}
.

Proposition 1.16
.

The curvature
\theta_{\omega}
has degree
-2
and is closed with respect to the coderivation
d_{\omega}
. The derivation satisfies

(1.7)

{d_{\omega}}^{2}=[\theta_{\omega},-].

Moreover the cobar construction induces a functor

\hat{\Omega}:\mathsf{Curved\ altip.\ coop.}^{\mathrm{lax}}\rightarrow\mathsf{Curved.\ sa\ op.}^{\mathrm{lax}}.

Proof.

The proof that
d_{\omega}\theta_{\omega}=0
and
{d_{\omega}}^{2}=[\theta_{\omega},-]
is dual to the beginning of the proof of Proposition
1.15
.
Similarly, we can prove that
\hat{\Omega}
sends lax morphisms of curved cooperads to lax morphisms of curved operads.
Let
(f,a^{c}):(\mathcal{C},d,\eta,\theta^{c})\to(\mathcal{C}^{\prime},d^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\,{\theta^{\prime}}^{c})
be a lax morphism of curved cooperads.
The map of
\mathbb{S}
-modules underlying
f
is characterized by the morphism of
\mathbb{S}
-modules
\bar{f}:\overline{\mathcal{C}}\to\overline{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}
and the morphism
s^{-1}\overline{f\cdot\eta}:I\to F_{1}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}})
(
f
is gr-coaugmented) which induces a map
a:I\to F_{1}\mathcal{T}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}})
.
We write
\hat{\Omega}(f,a^{c}):\mathcal{T}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}})\to\mathcal{T}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}})
the operad morphism induced by

s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}\xrightarrow{-sa^{c}+\bar{f}}I\oplus s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}\rightarrowtail\mathcal{T}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}).

The proof that
(\hat{\Omega}(f,a^{c}),a)
is a lax morphism of curved operads is dual to the second part of the proof of Proposition
1.15
.
We prove similarly as in Proposition
1.15
that it preserves the composition of lax morphisms.
∎

Example 1.17
.

We denote by
\mathrm{Lie}
the operad encoding Lie algebras and by
\mathrm{cLie}
the operad encoding curved Lie algebras.
It is defined by

\mathrm{cLie}\coloneqq\left(\mathcal{T}\left(\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern
