(This filtration is in fact a graduation.)
It is a curved operad whose curvature belongs to
F_{1}\mathrm{cLie}
.
Indeed,
0=d^{2}=[\theta,-]
because it is a suboperad of the curved operad
\mathrm{cAs}
for which it is proved in
[
BMDC20
, Proposition 6.2]
.

We also fix
\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{Lie}\coloneqq\mathrm{End}_{s\mathbb{R}}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{Lie}
the operad encoding
shifted Lie algebras
and
\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}\coloneqq\mathrm{End}_{s\mathbb{R}}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{cLie}
the operad encoding
shifted curved Lie algebras
.

The operads encoding homotopy version of these algebras are built by means of the cobar construction and the Koszul duality theory of curved operad
[
BMDC20
]
and are denoted by
\mathrm{L}_{\infty}\coloneqq\hat{\Omega}\mathrm{Lie}^{\text{!`}}
,
\mathrm{cL_{\infty}}\coloneqq\hat{\Omega}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}}
,
\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{L_{\infty}}\coloneqq\hat{\Omega}\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{Lie}^{\text{!`}}
and
\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cL_{\infty}}\coloneqq\hat{\Omega}\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}}
.

The coproduct
\Delta
and the partial coproduct
\Delta_{(1)}
of the cooperad
\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{Lie}^{\text{!`}}
are computed in
[
BM18
, Appendix A]
.
The case of the cooperad
\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}}
, Koszul dual cooperad of the curved operad
\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}
, can be done similarly as Theorem 6.9 in
[
BMDC20
]
which concerns the operad encoding curved associative algebras.

We obtain that the Koszul dual cooperad
\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}}
is 1-dimensional in each arity,
\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}}(n)\cong\mathbb{K}\cdot{l}_{n}^{c}
, where
{l}_{n}^{c}
is an element of degree
0
on which
\mathbb{S}_{n}
acts trivially. The infinitesimal decomposition map on
\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}}
is given by

(1.8)

\displaystyle\Delta_{(1)}({l}_{n}^{c})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p+q=n+1\\
p\geq 1,q\geq 0\end{subarray}}\sum_{\sigma\in Sh_{q,\,p-1}^{-1}}({l}_{p}^{c}\circ_{1}{l}_{q}^{c})^{\sigma},

where
Sh_{q,\,p-1}^{-1}
is the set of
(q,\,p-1)
-unshuffles, that is, inverses of
(q,\,p-1)
-shuffles. The formula for the full decomposition map is given by

\Delta_{\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}}}({l}_{n}^{c})=\sum_{q_{1}+\cdots+q_{p}=n}\frac{1}{p!}\sum_{\sigma\in Sh_{q_{1},\,\ldots,\,q_{p}}^{-1}}\left({l}_{p}^{c}\,;\,{l}_{q_{1}}^{c},\,\ldots,\,{l}_{q_{p}}^{c}\right)^{\sigma},

where
Sh_{q_{1},\,\ldots,\,q_{p}}^{-1}
is the set of
(q_{1},\,\ldots,\,q_{p})
-unshuffles
\sigma
.

1.2.3.
Convolution curved operad

We aim to extend the
\mathrm{L}_{\infty}
-algebra structures on
the convolution algebras
\hom(C,A)
of
\mathbf{R}
-linear maps from a
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra
C
to a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
A
given in
[
Wie19
]
to the curved setting.
We first describe the convolution curved operad
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
and see how it is related to the bar and cobar constructions.

Let
(\mathcal{C},\Delta,\varepsilon_{\mathcal{C}},d_{\mathcal{C}},\theta^{c},\eta_{\mathcal{C}})
be a curved altipotent cooperad and
(\mathcal{P},\gamma,\eta_{\mathcal{P}},d_{\mathcal{P}},\theta,\varepsilon_{\mathcal{P}})
be a curved sa operad.

We consider the graded collection of modules

\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\coloneqq\{\mathrm{Hom}_{\textrm{filt.}\ \mathbf{K}\text{-mod}}(\mathcal{C}(n),\mathcal{P}(n))\}_{n\geq 0}.

In order to get an
\mathbb{S}
-module, we endow each module with the following action of the symmetric group: for
f\in\mathcal{C}(n)\to\mathcal{P}(n)
,
\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{n}
and
\nu\in\mathcal{C}(n)
, we have

(f^{\sigma})(\nu)\coloneqq(f(\nu^{\sigma^{-1}}))^{\sigma}.

Let
f:\mathcal{C}(k)\to\mathcal{P}(k)
and let, for
1\leq j\leq k
,
g_{j}:\mathcal{C}(i_{j})\to\mathcal{P}(i_{j})
be a collection of filtered
maps.
We define a composition map
\gamma_{H}(f;g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})
by the formula

\mathcal{C}(n)\xrightarrow{\Delta}(\mathcal{C}\hat{\circ}\mathcal{C})(n)\twoheadrightarrow\\
\mathcal{C}(k)\hat{\otimes}_{\mathbf{K}[\mathbb{S}_{k}]}\left(\left(\mathcal{C}(i_{\sigma(1)})\hat{\otimes}\cdots\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{C}(i_{\sigma(k)})\right)\hat{\otimes}_{\mathbf{K}[\mathbb{S}_{i_{\sigma(1)}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{S}_{i_{\sigma(k)}}]}\mathbf{K}[\mathbb{S}_{n}]\right)\\
\xrightarrow{\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{k}}f^{\sigma}\otimes g_{\sigma(1)}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{\sigma(k)}\otimes\operatorname{id}}\\
\mathcal{P}(k)\hat{\otimes}_{\mathbf{K}[\mathbb{S}_{k}]}\left(\left(\mathcal{P}(i_{\sigma(1)})\hat{\otimes}\cdots\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{P}(i_{\sigma(k)})\right)\hat{\otimes}_{\mathbf{K}[\mathbb{S}_{i_{\sigma(1)}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{S}_{i_{\sigma(k)}}]}\mathbf{K}[\mathbb{S}_{n}]\right)\\
\hookrightarrow(\mathcal{P}\hat{\circ}\mathcal{P})(n)\xrightarrow{\gamma}\mathcal{P}(n).

It is well defined and it is filtered since the maps
f
,
g_{1}
, …,
g_{k}
are, and
\Delta
and
\gamma
are compatible with the filtrations.

Let
\eta_{H}:I\to\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
the composite
\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathcal{C}}
and
\varepsilon_{H}:\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\to\mathrm{End}(I)\cong I
be given by

(f:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P})\mapsto(\varepsilon_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot f\cdot\eta_{\mathcal{C}}:I\to I),

so that
\varepsilon_{H}\cdot\eta_{H}=\operatorname{id}_{I}
.
We note also
\partial(f)\coloneqq d_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot f-(-1)^{|f|}f\cdot d_{\mathcal{C}}
.

We consider on
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
several filtrations. First we denote by
F_{\bullet}
the filtration induced by the filtrations on
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{P}
, that is for all
p\geq 0

F_{p}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\coloneqq\{f\in\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\ |\ \forall q\geq 0,\ f(F_{q}\mathcal{C})\subset F_{q+p}\mathcal{P}\}.

The maps
\gamma_{H}
and
\partial
are compatible with this filtration since the maps involved in their definitions are filtered. The filtration is complete since the filtration on
\mathcal{P}
is.

Secondly, we consider on
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
a coarser filtration
\Gamma_{\bullet}
that is still complete and such that our intended curvature for
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
is in filtration degree 1.
We consider all the filtrations
G_{\bullet}
compatible with
\gamma_{H}
and
\partial
such that

(1.9)

\left\{\begin{aligned} G_{0}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})&=F_{0}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})=\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\\
G_{1}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})&\supset F_{1}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})+\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})(0)+\mathrm{Hom}(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}},\mathcal{P})(1)\\
G_{p}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})&\supset F_{p}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\text{ for all }p\geq 2.\end{aligned}\right.

The constant filtration given by
G_{p}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})=\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
, for
p\geq 0
, is a filtration satisfying the above conditions (
1.9
).
Therefore there exists a smallest such filtration, namely the intersection of all filtrations satisfying (
1.9
). We denote it by

\Gamma_{0}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})=\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\supseteq\Gamma_{1}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\supseteq\cdots

and we call it the
lower central filtration

\Gamma_{\bullet}
of
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
.

Remark 1.18
.

We prove in Lemma
1.20
that
\Gamma_{\bullet}
is complete.

Before describing slightly the lower central filtration, we prove several properties on a (curved) altipotent cooperad. First of all, by the freeness property of the tree cooperad (see Corollary 3.35 in
[
BMDC20
]
), there exists a cooperad morphism
\widetilde{\Delta}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{T}^{c}(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}})
corresponding to the identity map
\operatorname{id}:\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}
.
Because
\mathcal{C}
is gr-coaugmented, we have on the level of
\mathbb{S}
-modules the isomorphism
\mathcal{C}\cong I\oplus\overline{\mathcal{C}}
and we deduce a map

\Delta^{\pi}:\mathcal{C}\to\prod_{l\geq 0}\mathcal{T}^{c}(\overline{\mathcal{C}},\widetilde{I})_{l}[l]\cong\prod_{l\geq 0}\mathcal{T}^{c}(\overline{\mathcal{C}},I)_{l},

where
\mathcal{T}^{c}(\overline{\mathcal{C}},I)_{l}
stands for the
\mathbb{S}
-module of trees labelled by elements in
\overline{\mathcal{C}}
or in
I
with exactly
l
elements in
I
.

Lemma 1.19
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a curved altipotent cooperad.
Let
\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{f}}\subset\mathcal{C}
be a finitely generated sub-
\mathbb{S}
-module.
Fix an integer
p
.
Then there exists an integer
s(\mathfrak{f},p)\geq 0
such that for each
l_{0}\geq 0
, the image
I_{\Delta}^{l_{0},p}
of the composite

\mathcal{C}^{f}\xrightarrow{\Delta^{\pi}}\prod_{l\geq 0}\mathcal{T}^{c}(\overline{\mathcal{C}},I)_{l}\twoheadrightarrow[\mathcal{T}^{c}(\overline{\mathcal{C}},I)_{l_{0}}]_{p}\coloneqq\mathcal{T}^{c}(\overline{\mathcal{C}},I)_{l_{0}}/F_{p}\mathcal{T}^{c}(\overline{\mathcal{C}},I)_{l_{0}}

factorises through

\mathcal{T}^{c}(\overline{\mathcal{C}},I)^{(\leq s(\mathfrak{f},p)+l_{0})}_{l_{0}}/F_{p}\mathcal{T}^{c}(\overline{\mathcal{C}},I)^{(\leq s(\mathfrak{f},p)+l_{0})}_{l_{0}},

where
\mathcal{T}^{c}(\overline{\mathcal{C}},I)^{(\leq s(\mathfrak{f},p)+l_{0})}_{l_{0}}\subset\mathcal{T}^{c}(I\oplus\overline{\mathcal{C}})^{(\leq s(\mathfrak{f},p)+l_{0})}
stands for the trees with at most
s(\mathfrak{f},p)+l_{0}
vertices and exactly
l_{0}
vertices labelled by an element in
I
.

Proof.

The size of a tree appearing in the image under consideration is bounded in width by the fact that
\Delta:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}\hat{\circ}\mathcal{C}
is well-defined and in depth (or height) by the fact that
\mathcal{C}
is altipotent.
∎

Lemma 1.20
.

The collection
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
is complete for the lower central filtration
\Gamma_{\bullet}
.

Proof.

The
\mathbb{S}
-module
\Gamma_{v}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
is the
\mathbb{S}
-module of multiple compositions of
\gamma_{H}
applied to elements
f_{1}
, …,
f_{m}
such that there exists a partition
\{1,\ldots,m\}=E_{1}\sqcup E_{2}
with

(1)

f_{j}\in F_{p_{j}}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
for all
j\in E_{1}
,

(2)

f_{j}\in\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})(0)\times\mathrm{Hom}(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}},\mathcal{P})(1)
for all
j\in E_{2}
,

and
(\sum_{j\in E_{1}}p_{j})+|E_{2}|\geq v
.

For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we fix a direct sum decomposition by finite-dimensional
\mathbb{S}_{n}
-submodules
\mathcal{C}(n)=\hat{\oplus}_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}_{n}}\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{f}}
of the
\mathbb{S}_{n}
-module
\mathcal{C}(n)
(which exists since
\mathbf{K}
is field and therefore
\mathbf{K}[\mathbb{S}_{n}]
is semi-simple) so that

(1.10)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})

\displaystyle=\{\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C}(n),\mathcal{P}(n))\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}=\{\mathrm{Hom}\left(\hat{\oplus}_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}_{n}}\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{f}},\mathcal{P}(n)\right)\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}

\displaystyle\cong\left\{\prod_{\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}_{n}}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{f}},\mathcal{P}(n))\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}.

The filtration
F_{p}
on
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
restricts to
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{f}},\mathcal{P}(n))
and we keep the same notation for it.

For all
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}_{n}
, because
\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{f}}
is finite-dimensional and the filtration
F_{\bullet}
is complete, there exists a smallest integer, denoted
q(\mathfrak{f})
, such that
\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{f}}\cap F_{q(\mathfrak{f})+1}\mathcal{C}=\{0\}
.
Fix an integer
p
.
Because
\mathcal{C}
is altipotent and by Lemma
1.19
, we get that
for all
l_{0}\geq 0
the image
I_{\Delta}^{l_{0},q(\mathfrak{f})+p}

lands in
\mathcal{T}^{c}(\overline{\mathcal{C}},I)^{(\leq s(\mathfrak{f},p)+l_{0})}_{l_{0}}/F_{q(\mathfrak{f})+p}\mathcal{T}^{c}(\overline{\mathcal{C}},I)^{(\leq s(\mathfrak{f},p)+l_{0})}_{l_{0}}
, where
s(\mathfrak{f},p)
depends only on
\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{f}}
and
p
.

We now prove that for any

(1.11)

v\geq 2p+s(\mathfrak{f},p)

we have

\forall\varphi\in\Gamma_{v}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P}),\ \varphi_{|\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{f}}}\in F_{p}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{f}},\mathcal{P}).

An element
\varphi\in\Gamma_{v}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
is a sum
\sum_{t_{v}}\tilde{\gamma}_{H}\cdot(f_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{m(t_{v})})\cdot t_{v}
, where
t_{v}\in\mathcal{T}(\mathcal{C})
is a tree with
m(t_{v})
vertices,
\tilde{\gamma}_{H}
is induced by
\gamma_{H}
and the
f_{j}
’s are elements in
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
such that there exists a partition
\{1,\ldots,m(t_{v})\}=E_{1}\sqcup E_{2}
with
f_{j}\in F_{p_{j}}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
for all
j\in E_{1}
and
f_{j}\in\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})(0)\times\mathrm{Hom}(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}},\mathcal{P})(1)
for all
j\in E_{2}
, and
(\sum_{j\in E_{1}}p_{j})+|E_{2}|\geq v
.
Due to the isomorphism
\mathcal{C}\cong I\oplus\overline{\mathcal{C}}
, we can assume without loss of generality that the vertices of
t_{v}
are either in
I
or in
\overline{\mathcal{C}}
.
When
\sum_{j\in E_{1}}p_{j}\geq p
, we automatically have the desired conclusion.
Otherwise by Condition (
1.11
), we have
|E_{2}|>p+s(\mathfrak{f},p)
.
This implies that there are at least
p
maps
f_{j}
, for
j\in E_{2}
, that are applied to
I
(thus each of them increases the filtration degree by at least 1) and therefore the term
\tilde{\gamma}_{H}\cdot(f_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{m(t_{v})})\cdot t_{v}
increases the filtration degree by at least
p
.
It follows that
\varphi_{|\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{f}}}\in F_{p}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{f}},\mathcal{P})
as expected.

Let us now prove that the filtration is complete.
For
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
, we note
H_{\mathfrak{f}}\coloneqq\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{f}},\mathcal{P})
.
We write
i_{\mathfrak{f}}:\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{f}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}
for the inclusion and
i_{\mathfrak{f}}^{*}:\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\to H^{\mathfrak{f}}
for the restriction.
We have just seen the existence for all
p\in\mathbb{N}
of some integer
v_{p}
such that
i_{\mathfrak{f}}^{*}\Gamma_{v_{p}}H\subset F_{p}i_{\mathfrak{f}}^{*}H=i_{\mathfrak{f}}^{*}F_{p}H
, where the last equality follows from the decomposition (
1.10
).
Using moreover that
H\coloneqq\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
is complete for the filtration
F_{\bullet}
and the interchange property of limits, we obtain

H_{\mathfrak{f}}=i_{\mathfrak{f}}^{*}H\cong\lim_{p\in\mathbb{N}}H_{\mathfrak{f}}/(i_{\mathfrak{f}}^{*}F_{p}H)=\lim_{p\in\mathbb{N}}H_{\mathfrak{f}}/(F_{p}H_{\mathfrak{f}})\\
\cong\lim_{p\in\mathbb{N}}\lim_{q\in\mathbb{N}}H_{\mathfrak{f}}/(F_{p}H_{\mathfrak{f}}+i_{\mathfrak{f}}^{*}\Gamma_{q}H)\cong\lim_{q\in\mathbb{N}}\lim_{p\in\mathbb{N}}i_{\mathfrak{f}}^{*}H/(i_{\mathfrak{f}}^{*}F_{p}H+i_{\mathfrak{f}}^{*}\Gamma_{q}H)\\
\cong i_{\mathfrak{f}}^{*}\lim_{q\in\mathbb{N}}H/\Gamma_{q}H.

This isomorphism being valid for all
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathfrak{F}
, we get by the decomposition (
1.10
) the identification
H\cong\lim_{q}H/\Gamma_{q}H
.
This concludes the proof.
∎

We define the degree
-2
element
\Theta_{H}\coloneqq\theta\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathcal{C}}+\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot\theta^{c}
. Because
\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot\theta^{c}\cdot\eta_{\mathcal{C}}=0
and by definition of the central lower filtration
\Gamma_{\bullet}
, we have

\Theta_{H}\in\mathrm{Hom}(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}},\mathcal{P})(1)\subset\Gamma_{1}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})(1).

Remark 1.21
.

We use the filtration
\Gamma_{\bullet}
instead of the filtration
F_{\bullet}
on
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
so that the element
\Theta_{H}
belong to
\Gamma_{1}\mathrm{Hom}_{(}\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})(1)
.
Since
\theta^{c}
cannot have filtration degree
\geq 1
(for the filtration
F_{\bullet}
) when it is non zero, the element
\Theta_{H}
doesn’t belong to
F_{1}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
in general.

Proposition 1.22
.

The collection
(\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P}),\Gamma_{\bullet},\gamma_{H},\eta_{H},\partial,\Theta_{H},\varepsilon_{H})
is a curved sa operad.
It is called the
convolution curved operad
.

Proof.

The operad structure
\gamma_{H}
is associative since
\Delta
is coassociative and
\gamma
is associative. The map
\eta_{H}
is a unit for the composition map since
\varepsilon_{\mathcal{C}}
is a counit for
\mathcal{C}
and
\eta_{\mathcal{P}}
is a unit for
\mathcal{P}
.
The map
\varepsilon_{H}
is a semi-augmentation since
\varepsilon_{H}\cdot\eta_{H}=\operatorname{id}_{I}
.

Finally, the element
\Theta_{H}\in\Gamma_{1}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
is closed since
\theta^{c}
and
\theta
are, and a direct computation shows that for a linear map
f:\mathcal{C}(k)\to\mathcal{P}(k)
, we have

\displaystyle\partial^{2}(f)

\displaystyle={d_{\mathcal{P}}}^{2}\cdot f-f\cdot{d_{\mathcal{C}}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\gamma(\theta\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathcal{C}};f)-\sum_{i}\gamma(f;\operatorname{id}^{\otimes(i-1)}\otimes\theta\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathcal{C}}\otimes\operatorname{id}^{\otimes(k-i-1)})

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt-\sum_{i}\gamma(f;\operatorname{id}^{\otimes(i-1)}\otimes\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot\theta^{c}\otimes\operatorname{id}^{\otimes(k-i-1)})+\gamma(\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot\theta^{c};f)

\displaystyle=\gamma(\Theta_{H};f)-\sum_{i}\gamma(f;\operatorname{id}^{\otimes(i-1)}\otimes\Theta_{H}\otimes\operatorname{id}^{\otimes(k-i-1)})=[\Theta_{H},f].

∎

Remark 1.23
.

In the sequel, we are mainly interested in the central lower filtration on the convolution curved operad (rather than the filtration
F_{\bullet}
).

To the symmetric operad
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
, we can associate a pre-Lie product
\star
on
\hom(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\coloneqq\prod_{n\geq 0}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})(n)
as in Section 5.4.3 in
[
LV12
]
.
The product
f\star g
is given by a symmetrical version of the formula

\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{\Delta_{(1)}}\mathcal{C}\hat{\circ}_{(1)}\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{f\circ_{(1)}g}\mathcal{P}\hat{\circ}_{(1)}\mathcal{P}\xrightarrow{\gamma_{(1)}}\mathcal{P}.

This pre-Lie product restricts to invariant elements under the conjugation action to endow the gr-dg module
\hom_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\coloneqq\left(\prod_{n\geq 0}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})(n),\Gamma_{\bullet}\right)
with the pre-Lie product
\star
.
The anti-symmetrisation of
\star
provides a Lie bracket
[f,g]=f\star g-(-1)^{|f||g|}g\star f
. The computation seen at the end of the proof of Proposition
1.22
shows that
(\hom_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P}),[-,-],\partial,\Theta_{H})
is a curved Lie algebra.

Definition 1.24
.

An
operadic curved twisting morphism
is a degree
-1
element
\alpha\in\hom_{\mathbb{S}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}},\mathcal{P})
(that is
\alpha\cdot\eta_{\mathcal{C}}:I\to F_{1}\mathcal{P}
) solution to the curved Maurer–Cartan equation

\Theta_{H}+\partial(\alpha)+\frac{1}{2}[\alpha,\alpha]=0.

We denote by
\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
the set of operadic curved twisting morphisms.

Lemma 1.25
.

Operadic curved twisting morphisms form a bifunctor

\mathrm{Tw}(-,-):\left(\mathsf{curved\ alt.\ coop.}^{\mathrm{lax}}\right)^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathsf{curved\ sa\ op.}^{\mathrm{lax}}\to\mathsf{Set}.

Proof.

We first prove the functoriality in the left variable.
Let
(f,a^{c}):\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
be a lax morphism between curved altipotent cooperads. To a curved twisting morphism
\alpha:\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{P}
, we associate the map
F(f,a^{c})(\alpha)\coloneqq\alpha\cdot f+\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot a^{c}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}
.
We first compute

\partial(\alpha\cdot f+\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot a^{c})=(\partial\alpha)\cdot f-\alpha\cdot(f\otimes a^{c}-a^{c}\otimes f)\cdot\Delta_{(1)}+\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot a^{c}\cdot d_{\mathcal{C}}.

Then

(\alpha\cdot f+\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot a^{c})\star(\alpha\cdot f+\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot a^{c})=\\
(\alpha\star\alpha)\cdot f+\alpha\cdot(f\otimes a^{c}-a^{c}\otimes f)\cdot\Delta_{(1)}+\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot(a^{c}\otimes a^{c})\cdot\Delta_{(1)}

It follows that

\displaystyle\partial(F(f,a^{c}))+F(f,a^{c})\star F(f,a^{c})

\displaystyle=-\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot\theta\cdot f+\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot\left(a^{c}\cdot d_{\mathcal{C}}+(a^{c}\otimes a^{c})\cdot\Delta_{(1)}\right)

\displaystyle=-\Theta_{H}^{\prime}\cdot f+\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot({\theta^{c}}^{\prime}\cdot f-\theta^{c})=-\Theta_{H}.

Therefore
F(f,a^{c})
is a curved twisting morphism (we have
F(f,a^{c})\in\hom_{\mathbb{S}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}},\mathcal{P})
since
a^{c}
has degree
-1
).
It is direct to check that
F(\operatorname{id},0)=\operatorname{id}
and
F
preserves the composition.

Similarly we prove the functoriality in the right variable. Let
(f,a):\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
be a lax morphism between curved operads. To a curved twisting morphism
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
, we associate the map
G(f,a)(\alpha)\coloneqq f\cdot\alpha+a\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
.
We first compute

\partial(f\cdot\alpha+a\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathcal{C}})=-[a,f]\cdot\alpha+f\cdot(\partial\alpha)+d_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}\cdot a\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathcal{C}}.

Then

\displaystyle(f\cdot\alpha+a\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathcal{C}})\star(f\cdot\alpha+a\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathcal{C}})=f\cdot(\alpha\star\alpha)+[a,f]\cdot\alpha+a^{2}\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathcal{C}}

It follows that

\displaystyle\partial(G(f,a))+G(f,a)\star G(f,a)

\displaystyle=f\cdot(\partial\alpha+\alpha\star\alpha)+(d_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}a+a^{2})\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathcal{C}}

\displaystyle=-f\cdot\Theta_{H}+\left(f(\theta_{\mathcal{P}})-\theta_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}\right)\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathcal{C}}=-\Theta_{H}^{\prime}.

Therefore
G(f,a):\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
is a curved twisting morphism (we have
G(f,a)\in\hom_{\mathbb{S}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}},\mathcal{P})
since
a:I\to F_{1}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
).
It is direct to check that
G(\operatorname{id},0)=\operatorname{id}
and
G
preserves the composition.
This concludes the proof.
∎

Theorem 1.26
.

For any curved altipotent cooperad
\mathcal{C}
and any curved sa operad
\mathcal{P}
, there are natural bijections

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{curv.\,sa\,op.}^{\mathrm{lax}}}(\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\cong\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{curv.\,alti.\,coop.}^{\mathrm{lax}}}(\mathcal{C},\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}).

Proof.

We make the first bijection explicit.
A lax morphism of curved sa operads
(f_{\alpha},a_{\alpha}):\mathcal{T}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}})\to\mathcal{P}
is uniquely determined by a map
-s\bar{\alpha}:s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}\to\mathcal{P}
of degree
0
and an
\mathbb{S}
-module map
a_{\alpha}:I\to F_{1}\mathcal{P}
of degree
-1
.
This is equivalent to the data of a map
\alpha\coloneqq a_{\alpha}\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathcal{C}}+\bar{\alpha}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}
of degree
-1
such that
\alpha\cdot\eta_{\mathcal{C}}:I\to F_{1}\mathcal{P}
, or equivalently to
\alpha\in\hom_{\mathbb{S}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}},\mathcal{P})
.
Moreover,
f_{\alpha}
commutes with the predifferential up to the term
[a_{\alpha},f_{\alpha}]
if and only if the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{-s\bar{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{D_{0}+D_{1}+D_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d_{\mathcal{P}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}}
\textstyle{I\oplus s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}\oplus(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}\hat{\circ}_{(1)}s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 21.33955pt\eta_{\mathcal{P}}+(-s\bar{\alpha})+(-s\bar{\alpha})\hat{\circ}_{(1)}(-s\bar{\alpha})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}\oplus\mathcal{P}\circ_{(1)}\mathcal{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}+\gamma_{(1)}}

up to the term
[a_{\alpha},-s\bar{\alpha}]
.
This corresponds to the equality
\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot\theta^{c}+\partial(\bar{\alpha})+[a_{\alpha},\bar{\alpha}]+\bar{\alpha}\star\bar{\alpha}=0
on
\overline{\mathcal{C}}
, or equivalently to the restriction of the equality
\Theta_{H}+\partial(\alpha)+\alpha\star\alpha=0
on
\overline{\mathcal{C}}
.
The fact that the morphism
f_{\alpha}
sends the curvature
\theta_{\omega}
to
\theta_{\mathcal{P}}+d_{\mathcal{P}}a_{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}[a_{\alpha},a_{\alpha}]
coincides with the restriction of the equation
\Theta_{H}+\partial(\alpha)+\alpha\star\alpha=0
to
\im\eta_{\mathcal{C}}
since
\alpha\cdot\eta_{\mathcal{C}}=a_{\alpha}
.

We now make the second bijection explicit.
A lax morphism of curved altipotent cooperads
(g_{\alpha},a_{\alpha}^{c}):\mathcal{C}\rightarrow{\mathcal{T}^{c}}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})
is uniquely determined by a map
s\bar{\alpha}:\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow s\overline{\mathcal{P}}
(by Proposition
1.14
) and an
\mathbb{S}
-module map
a_{\alpha}^{c}:\mathcal{C}\to I
of degree
-1
.
This is equivalent to a map
\alpha\coloneqq\bar{\alpha}+\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot a_{\alpha}^{c}:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}
of degree
-1
satisfying
\alpha\cdot\eta_{\mathcal{P}}:I\to F_{1}\mathcal{P}
, or equivalently to
\alpha\in\hom_{\mathbb{S}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}},\mathcal{P})
.
Moreover, because
g_{\alpha}(\mu)=\varepsilon_{\mathcal{C}}(\mu)+s\bar{\alpha}(\mu)+(s\bar{\alpha}\otimes s\bar{\alpha})\Delta_{(1)}+\dots\in I\oplus s\overline{\mathcal{P}}\oplus s\overline{\mathcal{P}}\hat{\circ}_{(1)}s\overline{\mathcal{P}}\oplus\dots
(by Construction 3.10 in
[
BMDC20
]
), the morphism
g_{\alpha}
satisfies Equation (
1.4
) if and only if
\varepsilon_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot(-d_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot\bar{\alpha}-\gamma_{(1)}(\bar{\alpha}\otimes\bar{\alpha})\cdot\Delta_{(1)})=\theta^{c}+a_{\alpha}^{c}d_{\mathcal{C}}+a_{\alpha}^{c}\star a_{\alpha}^{c}
, that is to say
\varepsilon_{\mathcal{P}}(\Theta_{H}+\partial\alpha+\alpha\star\alpha)=0
.
Also
g_{\alpha}
commutes with the predifferentials up to
(g_{\alpha}\otimes a_{\alpha}^{c}-a_{\alpha}^{c}\otimes g_{\alpha})\cdot\Delta_{(1)}
(see Equation (
1.3
)) if and only if the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon_{\mathcal{C}}+s\bar{\alpha}+((s\bar{\alpha})\otimes(s\bar{\alpha}))\cdot\Delta_{(1)}\hskip 28.45274pt}
\scriptstyle{d_{\mathcal{C}}}
\textstyle{I\oplus s\overline{\mathcal{P}}\oplus s\overline{\mathcal{P}}\hat{\circ}_{(1)}s\overline{\mathcal{P}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d_{\beta}^{|s\overline{\mathcal{P}}}=(d_{0}+d_{1}+d_{2})^{|s\overline{\mathcal{P}}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s\bar{\alpha}}
\textstyle{s\overline{\mathcal{P}}}

up to the term
(s\bar{\alpha}\otimes a_{\alpha}^{c}-a_{\alpha}^{c}\otimes s\bar{\alpha})\cdot\Delta_{(1)}
.
Similarly as before, this is equivalent to the projection of the equality
\Theta_{H}+\partial(\alpha)+\alpha\star\alpha=0
on to
s\overline{\mathcal{P}}
.
This concludes the proof.
∎

Example 1.27
.

•

Through the adjunction the identity morphism
\operatorname{id}:\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}\to\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}
corresponds to the operadic curved twisting morphism
\pi:\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}\twoheadrightarrow s\overline{\mathcal{P}}\cong\overline{\mathcal{P}}\rightarrowtail\mathcal{P}
.

•

Through the adjunction the identity morphism
\operatorname{id}:\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{C}\to\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{C}
corresponds to the operadic curved twisting morphism
\iota:\mathcal{C}\twoheadrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{C}}\cong s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrowtail\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{C}
.

In Section
1.2.4
, we study the counit of the bar-cobar adjunction above and we prove that it provides a cofibrant resolution in the model category of curved operads.

Before that we use the bar-cobar adjunction in order to describe operadic curved twisting morphisms as lax morphisms of curved sa operads from
\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cL_{\infty}}
to the convolution curved operad
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
.
This extends a result given in
[
Wie19
, Section 7]
which applies to dg operads and cooperads which are connected (that is satisfy
\mathcal{P}(0)=0=\mathcal{C}(0)
and
\mathcal{P}(1)=\mathbf{K}=\mathcal{C}(1)
).
Then, in Section
3.3
, we use this lax morphism in order to describe an
\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cL_{\infty}}
structure on the convolution algebra
\hom(C,A)
for a
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra
C
and a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
A
(on which
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
acts).

Let
a\in\Gamma_{1}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})(1)=\hom_{\mathbb{S}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}},\mathcal{P})(1)
be an operadic curved twisting morphism.
For
f\in\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
, we fix
\partial_{a}(f)\coloneqq\partial(f)+[a,f]
and
\Theta_{a}\coloneqq\Theta_{H}+\partial a+\frac{1}{2}[a,a]
.
By Lemma
1.2
, the collection
(\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P}),\gamma_{H},\eta_{H},\partial_{a},\Theta_{a},\varepsilon_{H})
is curved sa operad, that we call a
twisted convolution curved operad
and denote
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})_{a}
.

Proposition 1.28
.

For any curved altipotent cooperad
\mathcal{C}
and any curved sa operad
\mathcal{P}
, there is a bijection

\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{curv.\,sa\,op.}^{\mathrm{lax}}}(\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cL_{\infty}},\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})).

Moreover, given an operadic curved twisting morphism
\alpha\in\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
, we get a morphism of curved operads
f_{\alpha}:\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cL_{\infty}}\to\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})_{a_{\alpha}}=(\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P}),\partial_{a_{\alpha}},\Theta_{a_{\alpha}})
, where
a_{\alpha}:I\to\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
is defined by
a_{\alpha}(1)=\alpha(1):\mathcal{C}(1)\to\mathcal{P}(1)
.

Proof.

By Theorem
1.26
, we have

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{curv.\,sa\,op.}^{\mathrm{lax}}}(\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cL_{\infty}},\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P}))\cong\mathrm{Tw}(\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}},\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})).

Given a curved sa operad
(\mathcal{Q},F_{\bullet})
, there is an isomorphism

\hom_{\mathbb{S}}\left(\widetilde{\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}}},\mathcal{Q}\right)\cong F_{1}\mathcal{Q}(0)^{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\times F_{1}\mathcal{Q}(1)^{\mathbb{S}_{1}}\times\prod_{n\geq 2}\mathcal{Q}(n)^{\mathbb{S}_{n}}

since the
\mathbb{S}_{n}
action on
\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}}
is trivial. When applied to
(\mathcal{Q},F_{\bullet})=(\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P}),\Gamma_{\bullet})
(on which the
\mathbb{S}
-action is given by conjugation), this implies the isomorphisms

\hom_{\mathbb{S}}\left(\widetilde{\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}}},\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\right)\cong\hom_{\mathbb{S}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}},\mathcal{P}).

Then, denoting by
\Upsilon_{\alpha}\in\hom_{\mathbb{S}}\left(\widetilde{\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}}},\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\right)
the element associated with
\alpha\in\hom_{\mathbb{S}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}},\mathcal{P})
through the bijection, a direct computation using that:

•

the curvature on
\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}}
is zero, and that

•

the infinitesimal decomposition map of
\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}}
is given in Equation (
1.8
),

shows that
\Upsilon_{\alpha}
is a solution of the curved Maurer–Cartan equation if and only if
\alpha
is a solution of the curved Maurer–Cartan equation.

Then, given an operadic curved twisting morphism
\alpha\in\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
, the previous bijection provides a corresponding lax morphism
(f_{\alpha},a_{\alpha}):\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cL_{\infty}}\to\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
, where
a_{\alpha}(1)=\alpha(1):\mathcal{C}(1)\to\mathcal{P}(1)
.
By Lemma
1.2
, this is equivalent to a morphism of curved operads
f_{\alpha}:\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cL_{\infty}}\to\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})_{a_{\alpha}}
.
This finishes the proof.
∎

1.2.4.
Bar-cobar resolution

In order to make use of the known bar-cobar resolution in the dg context, we put some weight condition on our curved operad.
The weight filtration should not be confused with the complete filtration
F_{\bullet}
of the objects of the base category.

We call an
\mathbb{S}
-module
M

weight filtered
if it is endowed with an
\mathbb{N}
-filtration

M=M^{(\leq 0)}\subset M^{(\leq 1)}\subset\cdots\subset M^{(\leq w)}\subset\cdots.

We assume moreover that the structural maps of
M
(the maps defining the actions of the symmetric groups and the predifferential) and the maps of weight filtered
\mathbb{S}
-modules are compatible with the weight filtration.
Because we are working over a field of characteristic 0, we can find (by Maschke’s Theorem) a splitting on the level of
\mathbb{S}
-modules of the filtration.
We denote by
M^{(w)}
, for
w\geq 0
, the elements of weight
w
when such a splitting is fixed.

We call a dg operad
\mathcal{Q}

weight filtered
if its underlying
\mathbb{S}
-module is weight filtered and if its structural maps (the composition map, the differential, …) are weight filtered.
We say that the weight filtration is
connected
if
\mathcal{Q}^{(0)}=\mathcal{Q}^{(\leq 0)}=I
.
Moreover, we call
\mathcal{Q}

bounded below
when for each fixed
w\geq 0
, the chain complex underlying
\mathcal{Q}^{(\leq w)}
is bounded below.

Remark 1.29
.

In the next proposition, we consider a curved operad such that
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}
is weight filtered. Be aware of the fact that the filtration
F_{p}\mathcal{P}
differs in general from the weight filtration
(\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P})^{(\leq w)}
.

We work here the model category structure on curved operads described in
[
BMDC20
, Appendix C]
and recalled in Appendix
A
.
In that article, the notation
\mathcal{T}(\vartheta I)
stands for the initial object of the category of curved operads with non-zero curvature (that is
\vartheta
is a degree
-2
element in filtration degree 1).
We say that a morphism of curved operads
f:\mathcal{R}\to\mathcal{P}
is an
\mathbb{S}
-cofibrant resolution
when it is a graded quasi-isomorphism and a strict surjection (that is
f
a surjection such that for all
p\geq 0
,
f(F_{p}\mathcal{R})=f(\mathcal{R})\cap F_{p}\mathcal{P}
) and when moreover the gr-dg
\mathbb{S}
-module map
\mathcal{T}(\vartheta I)\to\mathcal{R}
is cofibrant.
Furthermore, we say that the filtration
F_{\bullet}
on an object
M
comes from the graduation
\mathrm{Gr}_{q}M
when
F_{p}M=\oplus_{q\geq p}\mathrm{Gr}_{q}M
.

Theorem 1.30
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a curved operad such that
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}
is a connected bounded below weight filtered operad. The counit of the adjunction

\hat{\Omega}\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{P}

obtained from Theorem
1.26
is a graded quasi-isomorphism and a strict surjection.

Assuming moreover that the filtration
F_{\bullet}
on the curved operad
\mathcal{P}
comes from a graduation
\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}
(on the level of
\mathbb{S}
-modules) and that the curvature of
\mathcal{P}
is concentrated in
\mathrm{Gr}_{1}\mathcal{P}
, then the map
\mathsf{c}\mathcal{T}(0)=\mathcal{T}(\vartheta I)\to\hat{\Omega}\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}
is cofibrant in the model category structure on curved operad described in
[
BMDC20
, Appendix C]
.

Proof.

We denote by
\Omega
the cobar construction of a coaugmented curved cooperad and by
\mathrm{B}
the bar construction of an sa dg operad. These constructions appear
[
HM12
]
(see also the corrigendum
[
HM23
]
).
The functor
\mathrm{Gr}
is strong monoidal since
\mathbf{K}
is a field so, by a careful inspection of the differentials,
\mathrm{Gr}\hat{\Omega}\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}\cong\Omega\mathrm{B}\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}
.
By Theorem 3.4.4 in
[
HM12
]
, we obtain that
\hat{\Omega}\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{P}
is a graded quasi-isomorphism.
It is a strict surjection since it is the composite of the two strict surjections
\hat{\Omega}\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{T}(\overline{\mathcal{P}})\xrightarrow{\bar{\gamma}}\mathcal{P}
.

When the curvature
\theta_{\mathcal{P}}
is non zero, the curvature
\theta_{\omega}=\theta_{\mathcal{P}}
of
\hat{\Omega}\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}
is also non zero and we have
\ker([\theta_{\mathcal{P}},-])=\mathcal{T}(\theta_{\mathcal{P}})\cong\mathcal{T}(\vartheta I)
by the fact that the graded operad underlying the cobar construction is free.
Assume now that the filtration
F_{\bullet}
on
\mathcal{P}
comes from a graduation
\mathrm{Gr}_{q}\mathcal{P}
.
We now show that the map
\mathcal{T}(\vartheta I)\to\hat{\Omega}\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}
, sending
\vartheta
to
\theta_{\omega}=\theta_{\mathcal{P}}
, is a cofibration.
We recall the following notations from the definitions of the bar and cobar constructions. The gr-dg differential on the cobar construction is
d_{\omega}=D_{0}+D_{1}+D_{2}
, where
D_{1}
is induced by the pre-differential on the bar construction
d_{0}+d_{1}+d_{2}
.

We first define the map
h:\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}\to\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}
by

\displaystyle h(t)

\displaystyle=-t^{\prime}\text{ when }t=\vbox{{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 2.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-2.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 2.0pt\raise-1.7014pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 7.49307pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 2.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-2.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 2.0pt\raise-1.7014pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 2.0pt\raise-1.7014pt\hbox{$\textstyle{d_{0}(t_{i})}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 29.88934pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 2.0pt\raise-1.7014pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-2.0pt\raise-7.71129pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 2.0pt\raise-1.7014pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 11.41052pt\raise-7.71129pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 2.0pt\raise-1.7014pt\hbox{$\textstyle{t_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.88934pt\raise-7.71129pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 2.0pt\raise-1.7014pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-2.0pt\raise-13.97293pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 2.0pt\raise-1.7014pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 13.94467pt\raise-13.97293pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 2.0pt\raise-1.7014pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 29.88934pt\raise-13.97293pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 2.0pt\raise-1.7014pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}}\right)=t

because
d_{0}
is a coderivation.
As a consequence, we have
\ker d_{0}=\im d_{0}
on
\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}
.
We decompose
\overline{\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}}
by the number of vertices
\overline{\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}}=\oplus_{l\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}\mathcal{T}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(l)}
.
We denote by
V_{1}=V
the direct complement of
\mathbf{K}\theta_{\mathcal{P}}
in
s^{-1}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})=\overline{\mathcal{P}}
as above.
We fix

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcll}S_{0}&\coloneqq&\{0\},\\
S_{1}&\coloneqq&\ker({\mathrm{Gr}d_{\omega}}_{|V_{1}})\oplus D_{1}\ker({\mathrm{Gr}d_{\omega}}_{|V_{1}})&\subset s^{-1}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}}\oplus\mathcal{T}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(2)}),\\
S_{2}&\coloneqq&S_{1}\oplus W_{1}\oplus D_{1}W_{1}&\subset s^{-1}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}}\oplus\mathcal{T}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(2)}),\end{array}\right.

where
W_{1}
is a direct complement of
\ker({\mathrm{Gr}d_{\omega}}_{|V_{1}})
into
V_{1}
(such a direct complement exists since
\mathbf{K}
is a field of characteristic 0 and
\mathbf{K}[\mathbb{S}_{n}]
is semi-simple for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
) and the inclusions hold because the filtration
F_{\bullet}
comes from a graduation.
A direct sum appears in the definition of
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
since
V_{1}\cap\ker d_{0}=V_{1}\cap\im{d_{0}}_{|I}=V_{1}\cap\mathbf{K}\theta_{\mathcal{P}}=\{0\}
.
For
l\geq 2
, we fix by induction a direct complement
V_{l}
of
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{T}}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(\leq l-1)}\oplus D_{1}V_{l-1}
in
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{T}}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(\leq l)}
, that is

s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{T}}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(\leq l)}=s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{T}}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(\leq l-1)}\oplus D_{1}V_{l-1}\oplus V_{l}.

Moreover let
W_{l}
be a direct complement of
\ker{\mathrm{Gr}d_{\omega}}_{|V_{l}}
into
V_{l}
.
Then we define for each
l\geq 2

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}S_{2l-1}&\coloneqq&S_{2(l-1)}\oplus\ker({\mathrm{Gr}d_{\omega}}_{|V_{l}})\oplus D_{1}\ker({\mathrm{Gr}d_{\omega}}_{|V_{l}}),\\
S_{2l}&\coloneqq&S_{2l-1}\oplus W_{l}\oplus D_{1}W_{l}.\end{array}\right.

As above, the second direct sum which appears in the definition of
S_{2l-1}
and
S_{2l}
follows from the following argument. For
v\in V_{l}
,
D_{1}v\in s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{T}}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(\leq l)}
implies
d_{0}v=0
so
v\in\ker{d_{0}}_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{T}}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(l)}}=\im{d_{0}}_{s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{T}}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(l-1)}}
where

s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{T}}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(l-1)}\subset s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{T}}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(\leq l-1)}=s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{T}}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(\leq l-2)}\oplus V_{l-1}\oplus D_{1}V_{l-2}

so there exists
v^{\prime}\in s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{T}}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(\leq l-2)}\oplus V_{l-1}\oplus D_{1}V_{l-2}
such that
v=D_{1}v^{\prime}-d_{1}v^{\prime}-d_{2}v^{\prime}
.
Because
D_{1}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{T}}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(\leq l-2)}\oplus D_{1}V_{l-2})\subset s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{T}}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(\leq l-1)}
, we get

v\in(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{T}}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})^{(\leq l-1)}\oplus D_{1}V_{l-1})\cap V_{l}=\{0\}.

By definition,
\left(S_{l}\right)_{l}
is an exhaustive increasing filtration of
s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{T}}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}})
and it satisfies
S_{l-1}\rightarrowtail S_{l}
are split monomorphisms of
\mathbb{S}
-modules with cokernels isomorphic to

S_{l}/S_{l-1}\cong\coprod_{\alpha}\left(\xi^{\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{K}[\mathbb{S}_{m_{\alpha}}]\oplus\zeta^{\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{K}[\mathbb{S}_{m_{\alpha}}]\right)

where
\xi^{\alpha}
is in homological degree
n_{\alpha}+1
and filtration degree
q_{\alpha}
and
\zeta^{\alpha}
is in homological degree
n_{\alpha}
and filtration degree
q_{\alpha}+1
. The predifferential
d_{\omega}
is such that
d_{\omega}(\xi^{\alpha})+\zeta^{\alpha}\in\left(\mathcal{T}_{+}(S_{l-1}),\,d_{\omega}\right)
since
d_{\omega}=D_{0}+D_{1}+D_{2}
with
(D_{0}+D_{2})(v)\in\mathcal{T}_{+}(S_{l-1})
for
v\in S_{l}
and because
d_{0}
increases exactly the filtration degree
F_{\bullet}
by 1 since
\theta_{\mathcal{P}}\in\mathrm{Gr}_{1}\mathcal{P}
. Moreover
d_{\omega}(\zeta^{\alpha})
is obtained by the fact that
d_{\omega}^{2}(\xi^{\alpha})=[\theta_{\mathcal{P}},\,\xi^{\alpha}]
.
This is precisely the description of cofibrant curved operads described in
[
BMDC20
, Proposition C.33]
hence
\hat{\Omega}\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}
is cofibrant.
∎

1.3.
Curved Koszul duality

In this section, we merge Section 4 in
[
HM12
]
and Section 5 in
[
BMDC20
]
in order to obtain a generalisation of Koszul duality to the context of curved inhomogeneous quadratic operads.

Definition 1.31
.

A curved operad
(\mathcal{P},d_{\mathcal{P}},\theta_{\mathcal{P}})
is called
inhomogeneous quadratic
if the following requirements are satisfied.

(1)

The curved operad
\mathcal{P}
admits a presentation of the form

\mathcal{P}=(\mathcal{T}(E)/(R),d_{\mathcal{P}}=0,\theta_{\mathcal{P}}),

where
E
is a
\mathbb{S}
-module and
(R)
is the ideal generated by a strict sub-
\mathbb{S}
-module
R\subset I\oplus E\oplus\mathcal{T}(E)^{(2)}
and the curvature
\theta_{\mathcal{P}}
is induced by a map
\tilde{\theta}_{\mathcal{P}}:I\to F_{1}\mathcal{T}(E)^{(2)}
.
The superscript
(2)
indicates the number of vertices and defines the weight degree.

(2)

The sub-
\mathbb{S}
-module
R
is a direct sum of (homological) degree homogeneous subspaces.

A first consequence is that the curved operad
\mathcal{P}
is degree graded and has a weight filtration
\mathcal{P}^{(\leq w)}
induced by the
\mathbb{S}
-module of generators
E
.
We denote the associated graded (curved) operad by
\mathcal{P}^{(\bullet)}
.
Let
\mathrm{q}:\mathcal{T}(E)\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{T}(E)^{(2)}
be the canonical projection and let
\mathrm{q}R\subset\mathcal{T}(E)^{(2)}
be the image under
\mathrm{q}
of
R
.
We assume further that the following conditions hold:

(I)

The space of generators is minimal, that is
R\cap\{I\oplus E\}=\{0\}
.

(II)

The space of relations is maximal, that is
(R)\cap\{I\oplus E\oplus\mathcal{T}(E)^{(2)}\}=R
.

(III)

The quadratic part of the relations satisfies
\mathrm{q}R\cap F_{1}\mathcal{T}(E)=\{0\}
.

We assume moreover that the curved operad
\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}:=\left(\mathcal{T}(E)/(\mathrm{q}R),0,\theta_{\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}}\coloneqq[\tilde{\theta}_{\mathcal{P}}]\right)
is homogeneous quadratic (in the sense of Definition 5.7 of
[
BMDC20
]
), that is

(3)

the counit
\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)\to I
does not factor through the coideal quotient
(S)
, where we fix

\mathcal{C}\coloneqq\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)\text{ and }S\coloneqq\left(I\oplus\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)^{(2)}\right)/\left(s^{2}\mathrm{q}R\oplus(\operatorname{id}+s^{2}\tilde{\theta}_{\mathcal{P}})(I)\right).

Since
R\cap\{I\oplus E\}=\{0\}
and
\mathrm{q}R\cap F_{1}\mathcal{T}(E)=\{0\}
, there exists an
\mathbf{K}
-linear map
\varphi:\mathrm{q}R\rightarrow I\oplus E
that we assume to be filtered such that
R
is the graph of
\varphi
:

\displaystyle R

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{X+\varphi(X),\,X\in\mathrm{q}R\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{X+\varphi_{1}(X)+\varphi_{0}(X),\,X\in\mathrm{q}R,\,\varphi_{1}(X)\in E,\,\varphi_{0}(X)\in I\}.

The relations
\mathrm{q}R
hold in
\mathcal{P}^{(\bullet)}
.
Therefore, there exists an epimorphism of graded (curved) operads

p:\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{P}^{(\bullet)}.

We recall from
[
BMDC20
, Section 5]
that the
Koszul dual cooperad
of the homogeneous quadratic curved operad
\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}
is the sub-cooperad of
\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)
generated by the strict epimorphism
\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)\twoheadrightarrow S
and we denote it by:

\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}:=\mathcal{C}\left(sE,\,s^{2}\mathrm{q}R\oplus(\operatorname{id}+s^{2}\tilde{\theta}_{\mathcal{P}})(I)\right).

It is a sub-cooperad of the cofree cooperad
\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)
on
sE
and its counit is given by the composite
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}:\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\rightarrowtail\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)\twoheadrightarrow I
.
By the definition of
\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}
, since
\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\to\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)\twoheadrightarrow S
is zero, we obtain that the projection
\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\twoheadrightarrow I\oplus\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)^{(2)}
factors through
s^{2}\mathrm{q}R\oplus(\operatorname{id}+s^{2}\tilde{\theta}_{\mathcal{P}})(I)
and we can define the projection map
p^{(2)}:\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\twoheadrightarrow s^{2}\mathrm{q}R
.
There exists a unique coderivation
d_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}:\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)
of degree
-1
which extends the map

\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\twoheadrightarrow s^{2}\mathrm{q}R\xrightarrow{-s^{-1}\otimes\varphi_{1}}sE.

Moreover, we denote by
\theta_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}^{c}:\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\rightarrow I
the map of degree
-2

\theta_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}^{c}:\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\twoheadrightarrow s^{2}\mathrm{q}R\xrightarrow{-s^{-2}\otimes\varphi_{0}}I.

Lemma 1.32
.

Let
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{T}(E)/(R)
be an inhomogeneous quadratic curved operad. Condition (II) implies that:

•

The image of
d_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}
lies in
\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}=\mathcal{C}\left(sE,\,s^{2}\mathrm{q}R\oplus(\operatorname{id}+s^{2}\tilde{\theta}_{\mathcal{P}})(I)\right)
;

•

The coderivation
d_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}
satisfies
{d_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}}^{2}=(\operatorname{id}_{\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}\otimes\theta_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}^{c}-\theta_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}^{c}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}})\cdot\Delta_{(1)}
;

•

The coderivation
d_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}
satisfies
\theta_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}^{c}\cdot d_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}=0
.

Proof.

Proposition 5.5 in
[
BMDC20
]
says that

\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}=\ker\left(\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{(\operatorname{id}\otimes\Delta)\cdot\Delta_{(1)}}\mathcal{C}\hat{\circ}_{(1)}\mathcal{C}^{\hat{\circ}2}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{C}}\circ_{(1)}\xi\circ\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{C}}}\mathcal{C}\hat{\circ}_{(1)}(S\hat{\circ}\mathcal{C})\right),

where
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)
and
S=\left(I\oplus\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)^{(2)}\right)/\left(s^{2}\mathrm{q}R\oplus(\operatorname{id}+s^{2}\tilde{\theta}_{\mathcal{P}})(I)\right)
.
Applying
d_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}
to an element in
\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}
, we obtain that the result lands in
\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}
whenever
\xi\cdot d_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}=0
(since
d_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}
is a coderivation and by the characterisation of
\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}
just recalled).
The cooperad
\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}
is a sub-cooperad of
\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)
so an element
c
in
\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}
can be written as a sum
c=\sum_{k\geq 0}c^{(k)}
of trees
c^{(k)}
in
\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)^{(k)}
.
By definition, the coderivation
d_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}
decreases on each component the number of vertices by one.
Since
\varepsilon\cdot d_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}=0
, we have
\xi\cdot d_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}(c)=\xi\cdot d_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}(c^{(3)})
.

Then the proof works similarly as
[
HM12
, Lemma 4.1.1]
.
Any kind of tree in
\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)^{(3)}
has one of the two forms

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 2.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-2.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 2.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}.\end{array}\right.

It follows by a careful computation of the signs that applying

\left(s^{-1}p^{(2)}\otimes p^{(1)}+p^{(1)}\otimes s^{-1}p^{(2)}\right)\cdot\Delta_{(1)}

(where
p^{(1)}
is the projection
\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\twoheadrightarrow sE
) to an element in
\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}
gives 0.
Therefore, we obtain

\Big(\big(s^{-1}\otimes(\operatorname{id}_{\mathrm{q}R}+\varphi_{1}+\varphi_{0})\cdot p^{(2)}\big)\otimes p^{(1)}+\\
p^{(1)}\otimes\big(s^{-1}\otimes(\operatorname{id}_{\mathrm{q}R}+\varphi_{1}+\varphi_{0})\cdot p^{(2)}\big)\Big)\cdot\Delta_{(1)}({\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}})\\
\subset\{sR\otimes_{(1)}sE+sE\otimes_{(1)}sR\}\cap s^{2}\{E\oplus\mathcal{T}(E)^{(2)}\}\subset s^{2}R

by Condition (II) for the last inclusion.
Denoting
s^{-1}\otimes\varphi_{1}\cdot p^{(2)}
by
\tilde{\varphi}_{1}
and
s^{-1}\otimes\varphi_{0}\cdot p^{(2)}
by
\tilde{\varphi}_{0}
, we get the following system of equations

(1)

(in
\mathcal{T}(E)^{(2)}
)
(\tilde{\varphi}_{1}\otimes p^{(1)}+p^{(1)}\otimes\tilde{\varphi}_{1})\cdot\Delta_{(1)}({\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}})\subset s^{2}\mathrm{q}R
;

(2)

(in
sE
)
\big(\tilde{\varphi}_{1}\cdot(\tilde{\varphi}_{1}\otimes p^{(1)}+p^{(1)}\otimes\tilde{\varphi}_{1})-s^{-1}(\tilde{\varphi}_{0}\otimes p^{(1)}+p^{(1)}\otimes\tilde{\varphi}_{0})\big)\cdot{\Delta_{(1)}}_{|{\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}}=0
;

(3)

(in
I
)
\tilde{\varphi}_{0}\cdot(\tilde{\varphi}_{1}\otimes p^{(1)}+p^{(1)}\otimes\tilde{\varphi}_{1})\cdot{\Delta_{(1)}}_{|{\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}}=0
.

Equation (1) is equivalent (up to sign) to
\xi\cdot d_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}=0
.
This ensures that the image of
d_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}
lies in
\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}
.
Equation
(2)
corresponds to the projection of the second point of the lemma to
sE
.
The equality extends to
\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}
since
{d_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}[d_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}},\,d_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}]
and
(\operatorname{id}_{\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}\otimes\theta_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}^{c}-\theta_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}^{c}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}})\cdot\Delta_{(1)}
are coderivations (see Lemma 3.2.2 in
[
HM12
]
).
Equation
(3)
corresponds to the third point of the lemma.
∎

Definition 1.33
.

Let
(\mathcal{P},\theta_{\mathcal{P}})
be an inhomogeneous quadratic curved operad with presentation
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{T}(E)/(R)
satisfying the conditions of Definition
1.31
.

•

The
Koszul dual curved cooperad
of
(\mathcal{P},\theta_{\mathcal{P}})
is the curved cooperad

\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\coloneqq(\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}},d_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}},\theta^{c}_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}).

It is altipotent since it is a (strict) sub-cooperad of an altipotent cooperad (the cofree one).

•

We define the application
\kappa:\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\to\mathcal{P}
of degree
-1
by

\kappa:\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\rightarrowtail\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)\twoheadrightarrow sE\cong E\to\mathcal{P}.

•

The curved operad
(\mathcal{P},\theta_{\mathcal{P}})
is called
Koszul
when

(1)

(\mathcal{P},\theta_{\mathcal{P}})\cong(\widehat{\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}},\bar{\theta}_{\mathcal{P}})
with
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}\cong\mathrm{Free}(E)/(R)
in
filtered
operads and
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{T}(E)/(R)
in
complete
operads is the presentation which makes the curved operad
(\mathcal{P},\theta_{\mathcal{P}})
inhomogeneous quadratic, and

(2)

(\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P},\theta_{\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}})\cong(\widehat{\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}},\bar{\theta}_{\mathcal{P}})
and
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}\cong\mathrm{q}\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}
as (curved) quadratic operads, and

(3)

the inhomogeneous quadratic operad
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}
is Koszul in the sense of
[
HM12
, 4.3]
or equivalently the curved quadratic operad
\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}
is Koszul in the sense of
[
BMDC20
, Definition 5.20]
, that is
\mathrm{q}\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}\cong\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}
is a Koszul (quadratic) operad.

Lemma 1.34
.

Let
(\mathcal{P},\theta_{\mathcal{P}})
be an inhomogeneous quadratic curved operad as in the previous definition.
The degree
-1
map
\kappa
is an operadic curved twisting morphism, that is

\Theta_{H}+\partial(\kappa)+\frac{1}{2}[\kappa,\kappa]=0,

where
\Theta_{H}=\theta_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}+\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot\theta_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}^{c}
and
\partial(\kappa)=d_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot\kappa+\kappa\cdot d_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}=\kappa\cdot d_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}
since
d_{\mathcal{P}}=0
.

Proof.

As in
[
BMDC20
, Section 5.17]
, we have

\eta_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}(1)=1+\left(s^{2}\tilde{\theta}_{\mathcal{P}}+\sum s^{2}qr\right)+\cdots\in I\oplus\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)^{(2)}\oplus\cdots

with
\sum s^{2}qr\in s^{2}\mathrm{q}R
.
On
\eta_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}(I)
, we therefore obtain

\left(\Theta_{H}\cdot\eta_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}+\partial(\kappa)\cdot\eta_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}+\frac{1}{2}[\kappa,\kappa]\cdot\eta_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}\right)(1)=\\
\theta_{\mathcal{P}}-\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot\sum\varphi_{0}(qr)-\sum\varphi_{1}(qr)-\theta_{\mathcal{P}}-\sum qr=0\in\mathcal{P}.

Then, on
\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}\subset sE\oplus\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)^{(2)}\oplus\cdots
, because the decomposition product appearing in the definition of
[-,-]
is the cofree one and by the definition of
\kappa
and
d_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}
, it is enough to consider elements in
\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}
whose projection on
\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)^{(2)}
is non zero.
Again by
[
BMDC20
, Section 5.17]
, these elements write
\sum s^{2}qr+\cdots\in\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)^{(2)}\oplus\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)^{(\geq 4)}
, with
\sum s^{2}qr\in s^{2}\mathrm{q}R
.
On such an element, we get

\left(\Theta_{H}+\partial(\kappa)+\frac{1}{2}[\kappa,\kappa]\right)\left(\sum s^{2}qr+\cdots\right)=\\
\left(\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot\theta^{c}_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}+\kappa\cdot(-s^{-1}\otimes\varphi_{1})+\frac{1}{2}[\kappa,\kappa]\right)\left(\sum s^{2}qr\right)=\\
-\sum\left(\varphi_{0}+\varphi_{1}+\operatorname{id}_{\mathrm{q}R}\right)\left(\sum qr\right)=0\in\mathcal{P}.

∎

As a consequence of this lemma and of Theorem
1.26
, we can associate to
\kappa
two maps:

•

f_{\kappa}:\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\to\mathcal{P}
is a morphism of curved sa operads,

•

g_{\kappa}:\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\to\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}
is a morphism of curved altipotent cooperads.

Theorem 1.35
.

Let
\left(\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{T}(E)/(R),\theta_{\mathcal{P}}\right)
be a (presented) Koszul inhomogeneous quadratic curved operad such that
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}
is connected bounded below weight graded.
The curved operad morphism

f_{\kappa}:\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\to\mathcal{P}

is a graded quasi-isomorphism and a strict surjection.

Assuming moreover that the filtration
F_{\bullet}
on
\mathcal{P}
comes from a graduation and that the curvature
\theta_{\mathcal{P}}
is non zero and is concentrated in
\mathrm{Gr}_{1}\mathcal{P}
, then the map
\mathsf{c}\mathcal{T}(0)=\mathcal{T}(\vartheta I)\to\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}
is an
\mathbb{S}
-cofibration, that is, a cofibration in the underlying category of
\mathbb{S}
-modules, in the model category structure on gr-dg
\mathbb{S}
-modules described in
[
BMDC20
, Appendix C]
.

Proof.

The proof follows the same lines as the proof of Theorem
1.30
.
The functor
\mathrm{Gr}
is strong monoidal since
\mathbf{K}
is a field of characteristic 0 so
\mathrm{Gr}\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\cong\Omega\mathrm{Gr}(\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}})
as dg operads (by a careful inspection of the differentials).
Moreover, on the level of
\mathbb{S}
-modules, we have the following isomorphisms

\mathrm{Gr}(\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}})=\mathrm{Gr}(\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}})\cong(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P})^{\text{!`}}\cong(\mathrm{q}\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P})^{\text{!`}}

where the first isomorphism follows from the Poincaré–Birkhoff–Witt type isomorphism in
[
BMDC20
, Theorem 5.21]
(since
\mathrm{q}\mathcal{P}
is a curved quadratic Koszul operad) and the second isomorphism is true since
\mathcal{P}
is Koszul.
Since
\mathcal{P}
is Koszul, the data
(E,R)
of generators and relations of
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}
are the same.
It follows that
\mathrm{Gr}(d_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}})=d_{(\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P})^{\text{!`}}}
and
\mathrm{Gr}(\theta^{c}_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}})=\theta^{c}_{(\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P})^{\text{!`}}}
.
Therefore, we obtain by
[
HM12
, Theorem 4.3.1]
the quasi-isomorphism

\mathrm{Gr}\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\cong\Omega((\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P})^{\text{!`}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}.

This says precisely that
f_{\kappa}
is a graded quasi-isomorphism.
The map
f_{\kappa}
is a strict surjection since it is the composite of the two strict surjections
\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{T}(E)\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{P}
.

When
\theta_{\mathcal{P}}\neq 0
, the curvature
\theta_{\omega}
of
\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}
is also non zero and we have
\ker([\theta_{\omega},-])=\mathcal{T}(\theta_{\omega})\cong\mathcal{T}(\vartheta I)
by the fact that the graded operad underlying the cobar construction is free.
Assume now that the filtration
F_{\bullet}
on
\mathcal{P}
comes from a graduation
\mathrm{Gr}_{p}\mathcal{P}
and that
\theta_{\mathcal{P}}
, hence
\theta_{\omega}
is concentrated in
\mathrm{Gr}_{1}\mathcal{P}
.
Then, as in
[
BMDC20
, Theorem 5.24]
, we get that the underlying gr-dg
\mathbb{S}
-module map
\mathcal{T}(\vartheta)\to\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}
is cofibrant, so
\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\to\mathcal{P}
is an
\mathbb{S}
-cofibration.
∎

Remark 1.36
.

(1)

A priori, we cannot hope in general for
\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}
to be cofibrant in the model category structure on curved operad (obtained by transfer) since its curvature is a linear combination of products.

(2)

Nevertheless, by Theorem C.45 in
[
BMDC20
]
, the model categories of
\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}
-algebras and of
\hat{\Omega}\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}
-algebras are both equivalent to the model category of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras (because all operads are
\mathbb{S}
-split in characteristic 0).
In particular, up to equivalence, the
\infty
-category of homotopy
\mathcal{P}
-algebras doesn’t depend of a choice of resolution.

2.
Examples of inhomogeneous quadratic curved operads

In this section, we make the case of the
\mathbf{K}
-linear operad encoding curved unital associative algebras and the
\mathbb{R}
-linear operad encoding curved Lie
\mathbb{C}
-algebras explicit.
We show that they form Koszul inhomogeneous quadratic curved operads.

To shorten the notations, we use in this section the convention
[n]\coloneqq\{1,\ldots,n\}
for an integer
n
.

2.1.
Curved unital associative algebras

We consider the curved operad presented as follows

are of degree 0 and the predifferential is zero.

\mathrm{cuAs}
by the number of

, say

(This filtration is in fact a graduation.)
It is a curved operad whose curvature belongs to

.
Indeed, we have already seen in

, Proposition 6.2]
that the bracket of the curvature with the elements in

is 0.

0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}]=0
, which is true because

\textstyle{{\scriptstyle\circ}}

is a unit for

.

Lemma 2.1
.

A
\mathrm{cuAs}
-algebra on a gr-dg module
A
is the same data as a curved unital associative algebra
(A,\mu,\eta_{A},d_{A},\theta_{A})
with curvature
\theta_{A}\in F_{1}A
.

Proof.

A map of curved operad
\mathrm{cuAs}\to\mathrm{End}_{A}
is characterized by the image of the generators

\textstyle{{\scriptstyle\circ}}

,

and

which give respectively three maps:
\eta_{A}:\mathbf{K}\to A
of degree 0,
\theta_{A}:\mathbf{K}\to F_{1}A
of degree
-2
and
\mu:A^{\otimes 2}\to A
of degree 0.
The relation defining
\mathrm{cuAs}
ensures that
\mu
is associative and that
\eta_{A}
is a unit for
\mu
.
The fact that the curvature is sent to the curvature says finally
{d_{A}}^{2}=[\theta_{A},\,-]
.
∎

We now describe our sign convention for a basis of the cooperad
\mathrm{As}^{\text{!`}}
and the consequences for the formula describing its decomposition map.
Following
[
LV12
, Section 9.1.5]
we note
\mu_{n}^{c}=-\sum_{t\in PBT_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tilde{t})t
for
n\geq 3
(where the sum runs over planar binary trees with
n
leaves identified with
\mathcal{T}(\mu^{c}_{2})(n)
) and we fix
\tilde{\mu}_{n}^{c}\coloneqq(-1)^{\frac{(n-1)(n+2)}{2}}\mu_{n}^{c}
for
n\geq 0
.
This change of sign gives for example
\tilde{\mu}_{0}^{c}=-\mu_{0}^{c}
,
\tilde{\mu}_{1}^{c}=\mu_{1}^{c}
and
\tilde{\mu}_{2}^{c}=\mu_{2}^{c}
.
With this basis, we obtain the following formula for the decomposition map.

Lemma 2.2
.

The decomposition map
\Delta
on
\mathrm{As}^{\text{!`}}=\{\tilde{\mu}_{n}^{c}\}_{n\geq 1}
is given by

\Delta(\tilde{\mu}_{n}^{c})=\sum_{i_{1}+\cdots+i_{m}=n}(-1)^{\sum_{j}(i_{j}-1)\sum_{l<j}i_{l}}(\tilde{\mu}_{m}^{c};\,\tilde{\mu}_{i_{1}}^{c},\,\cdots,\,\tilde{\mu}_{i_{m}}^{c}).

Proof.

In
[
LV12
, Lemma 9.1.2]
, the formula given for the decomposition map of
\mathrm{As}^{\text{!`}}=\{\mu_{n}^{c}\}_{n\geq 1}
is

\displaystyle\Delta(\mu_{n}^{c})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1}+\cdots+i_{m}=n}(-1)^{\sum(i_{j}-1)(m-j)}(\mu_{m}^{c};\,\mu_{i_{1}}^{c},\,\cdots,\,\mu_{i_{m}}^{c}).

We consider the isomorphism of
\mathrm{As}^{\text{!`}}
given by
\mu_{n}^{c}\mapsto\tilde{\mu}_{n}^{c}\coloneqq(-1)^{\frac{(n-1)(n+2)}{2}}\mu_{n}^{c}
.
We compute

\displaystyle\frac{(n-1)(n+2)}{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j}(i_{j}-1)(m-j)-\frac{(m-1)(m+2)}{2}-\sum_{j}\frac{(i_{j}-1)(i_{j}+2)}{2}=

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{j}(i_{j}-1)+(m-1)\right)(n+2)+\sum_{j}(i_{j}-1)(m-j)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{(m-1)(m+2)}{2}-\sum_{j}\frac{(i_{j}-1)(i_{j}+2)}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{j}(i_{j}-1)\left(i_{j}+2+\sum_{l\neq j}i_{l}\right)+(m-1)(m+2+n-m)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{j}(i_{j}-1)(m-j)-\frac{(m-1)(m+2)}{2}-\sum_{j}\frac{(i_{j}-1)(i_{j}+2)}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{j}(i_{j}-1)\left(\sum_{l\neq j}i_{l}+2(m-j)\right)+(m-1)(m-n)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j}\sum_{l\neq j}i_{j}i_{l}+\frac{n(m-1)}{2}+m(n-m)-\sum_{j}i_{j}j+\frac{m(m+1)}{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{(m-1)(m-n)}{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j}i_{j}\left(\sum_{l>j}i_{l}-j\right)+nm=\sum_{j}i_{j}\left(\sum_{l>j}(i_{l}-1)+m-2j\right)+nm

\displaystyle\equiv\sum_{j}\sum_{l>j}i_{j}(i_{l}-1)\mod 2\equiv\sum_{j}\sum_{l<j}(i_{j}-1)i_{l}\mod 2.

This proves the formula.
∎

Theorem 2.3
.

The inhomogeneous quadratic curved operad
\mathrm{cuAs}
is Koszul.
Its Koszul dual curved cooperad
\mathrm{cuAs}^{\text{!`}}
is isomorphic to

\mathrm{cuAs}^{\text{!`}}\cong\left(s\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\raise-3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-6.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-6.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-4.27777pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\lx@xy@point}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\raise-6.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-9.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-9.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\raise-9.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-12.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-12.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\raise-12.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}\oplus\mathrm{q}\mathrm{uAs}^{\text{!`}}=\left\{\nu_{n}^{T}\right\}_{n\geq 0,T\subseteq[n]},\Delta^{\theta},0,\theta^{c}\right),

where the terms
\nu_{n}^{T}
have degree
n-1+|T|
and for any
n\geq 0
and
T\subseteq[n]
, we have

(2.1)

\displaystyle\Delta^{\theta}(\nu_{n}^{T})\coloneqq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}+\dots+i_{m^{\prime}}=n-|T_{0}|\\
\{T_{0},\dots,T_{m^{\prime}}\}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\epsilon}(\nu_{m}^{T_{0}};\nu_{i_{1}}^{T_{1}},\ldots,\nu_{i_{m^{\prime}}}^{T_{m^{\prime}}})

where
m^{\prime}\coloneqq m-|T_{0}|
and
T_{0}=T_{0}^{0}\sqcup\dots\sqcup T_{0}^{m^{\prime}}
with for all
j
between 1 and
m^{\prime}
,
\max T_{0}^{j-1}<\min T_{0}^{j}
and

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle=T_{0}^{0}\sqcup(T_{1}+|T_{0}^{0}|)\sqcup(T_{0}^{1}+i_{1}-1)\sqcup\dots\sqcup

\displaystyle\qquad\left(T_{m^{\prime}}+|T_{0}^{1}|+\sum_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}-1}(|T_{0}^{j}|+i_{j})\right)\sqcup\left(T_{0}^{m^{\prime}}+\sum_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}(i_{j}-1)\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{T}_{0}\sqcup\dots\sqcup\tilde{T}_{m^{\prime}}

(where
\tilde{T}_{j}
corresponds to the elements of
T_{j}
reindexed) and where

\epsilon\coloneqq\sum_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}(i_{j}-1)\left(\sum_{l<j}i_{l}+|T_{0}^{0}|+|T_{1}|+|T_{0}^{1}|+\cdots+|T_{j-1}|+|T_{0}^{j-1}|\right)+\\
|T_{0}|\left(n-m\right)+\sum_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}|T_{0}^{j}|(|T_{1}|+\cdots+|T_{j}|).

The curvature
\theta^{c}:\mathrm{cuAs}^{\text{!`}}\to I
is equal to

\theta^{c}(\nu_{n}^{T})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{rl}|&\text{if }n=2\text{ and, }T=\{1\}\text{ or }T=\{2\}\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

Moreover, the natural map

f_{\kappa}:\mathrm{cuA}_{\infty}\coloneqq\hat{\Omega}\mathrm{cuAs}^{\text{!`}}\to\mathrm{cuAs}

is an
\mathbb{S}
-cofibrant resolution.

Remark 2.4
.

Even if it is only implicitly visible, the difference with the decomposition map formula given in
[
HM12
]
is (up to signs) that in the above theorem the sum runs over integers
m
,
i_{1}
, …,
i_{m^{\prime}}
which can be 0.

Proof.

The filtration
F_{\bullet}
on the curved operad
\mathrm{cuAs}
is a graduation and
\mathrm{cuAs}
is the completion of its associated graded.
The presentation of
\mathrm{cuAs}
given above is easily seen to be inhomogeneous quadratic, except for Condition (3) in Definition
1.31
that will be proven during the proof.
The presentation satisfies the fact that
\mathrm{q}\mathrm{cuAs}
is the completion of its associated graded.
It satisfies also that
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{q}\mathrm{cuAs}\cong\mathrm{q}\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{cuAs}
as quadratic operad.
In order to prove the Koszulness of
\mathrm{cuAs}
, it remains to show that
\mathrm{q}\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{cuAs}
is a quadratic Koszul operad (see Definition
1.33
).
Noting
\mathcal{P}\ast\mathcal{Q}
for the coproduct of two operads
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{Q}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{q}\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{cuAs}

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{Free}\left(\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern , the differential on the copies

.
Appart from this particular copy (which is clearly acyclic), each copy
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{cAs}\circ_{\kappa}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{cAs})^{\text{!`}}(n)
is acyclic (or quasi-isomorphic to
I
in the case
n=1
) because
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{cAs}
is Koszul (see
[
BMDC20
, Theorem 6.9]
) and we get that
\mathrm{q}\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{cuAs}\circ_{\kappa}(\mathrm{q}\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{cuAs})^{\text{!`}}\xrightarrow{\sim}I
.
It follows that
\mathrm{q}\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{cuAs}
is Koszul as a quadratic operad.
Thus
\mathrm{cuAs}
is a Koszul inhomogeneous quadratic curved operad (up to Condition (3) in Definition
1.31
).

To compute
\mathrm{cuAs}^{\text{!`}}
, we can proceed as in the proof of Theorem 6.9 in
[
BMDC20
]
.
We first prove that
\mathcal{C}=\left(\left\{\nu_{n}^{T}\right\}_{n\geq 0,T\subseteq[n]},\Delta^{\theta},0,\theta^{c}\right)
is a curved sub-cooperad of

Then we prove that this inclusion is an isomorphism.

We have

\Delta\left(\left(\mu_{n+k}^{c,S}\right)^{T}\right)=\\
\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}+\dots+i_{m^{\prime}}=n-|T_{0}|\\
\{T_{0},\dots,T_{m^{\prime}}\}\\
\{S_{0},\ldots,S_{m^{\prime}}\}\\
|S_{j}|=k_{j}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}}\left(\left(\mu_{m+k_{0}}^{c,S_{0}}\right)^{T_{0}};\left(\mu_{i_{1}+k_{1}}^{c,S_{1}}\right)^{T_{1}},\ldots,\left(\mu_{i_{m^{\prime}}+k_{m^{\prime}}}^{c,S_{m^{\prime}}}\right)^{T_{m^{\prime}}}\right)

where
m^{\prime}=m-|T_{0}|
and the sums run over the partitions
\{T_{0},\dots,T_{m^{\prime}}\}
and
\{S_{0},\ldots,S_{m^{\prime}}\}
such that

•

T_{0}=T_{0}^{0}\sqcup\dots\sqcup T_{0}^{m^{\prime}}
with for all
j
between 1 and
m^{\prime}
,
\max T_{0}^{j-1}<\min T_{0}^{j}
and

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle=T_{0}^{0}\sqcup(T_{1}+|T_{0}^{0}|)\sqcup(T_{0}^{1}+i_{1}-1)\sqcup\dots\sqcup

\displaystyle\qquad\left(T_{m^{\prime}}+|T_{0}^{1}|+\sum_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}-1}(|T_{0}^{j}|+i_{j})\right)\sqcup\left(T_{0}^{m^{\prime}}+\sum_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}(i_{j}-1)\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{T}_{0}\sqcup\dots\sqcup\tilde{T}_{m^{\prime}}

(where
\tilde{T}_{j}
corresponds to the elements of
T_{j}
reindexed)

•

S_{0}=S_{0}^{0}\sqcup\dots\sqcup S_{0}^{m^{\prime}}
and for all
j
between 1 and
m^{\prime}
,
\max S_{0}^{j-1}<\min S_{0}^{j}
and

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle=S_{0}^{0}\sqcup(S_{1}+|S_{0}^{0}|+|T_{0}^{0}|)\sqcup(S_{0}^{1}+i_{1}+k_{1}-1)\sqcup\dots\sqcup

\displaystyle\qquad\left(S_{m^{\prime}}+|S_{0}^{0}|+|T_{0}^{0}|+\sum_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}-1}(|S_{0}^{j}|+|T_{0}^{j}|+i_{j}+k_{j})\right)\sqcup\left(S_{0}^{m^{\prime}}+\sum_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}(i_{j}+k_{j}-1)\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{S}_{0}\sqcup\dots\sqcup\tilde{S}_{m^{\prime}}

(where
\tilde{S}_{j}
corresponds to the elements of
S_{j}
reindexed)

and

•

\epsilon_{1}=\sum(i_{j}+k_{j}-1)(\sum_{l<j}(i_{l}+k_{l})+\sum_{l<j}(|S_{0}^{l}|+|T_{0}^{l}|))+k_{0}(n+k-m-k_{0})+L_{0}+K
, with

, and

is computed as in Formula (9) in
[
BMDC20
]
and modified by the change of convention explained in Lemma
2.2
,

•

whereas

\displaystyle\epsilon_{2}

\displaystyle=|T_{0}|\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}(i_{j}+k_{j}-1-k_{j})\right)+\sum_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}|T_{0}^{j}|\sum_{l\leq j}|T_{l}|+\sum_{j}|T_{j}|\sum_{l>j}(i_{l}+2k_{l}-1)

\displaystyle\equiv|T_{0}|\left(n-m\right)+\sum_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}|T_{0}^{j}|\sum_{l\leq j}|T_{l}|+\sum_{j}|T_{j}|\sum_{l>j}(i_{l}-1)\mod 2

is computed as in Corollary 6.1.5 in
[
HM12
]
using Proposition 6.1.4 and where we haven’t considered the sign coming from the decomposition of

already considered in the previous point.

is zero.
As a byproduct, we get that
\upsilon(\nu_{1}^{\emptyset})\in\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)
is sent to 0 in the coideal quotient
(S)
(whose definition is given in
[
BMDC20
, Definition 5.3]
) so the counit of
\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)
cannot factor through the coideal quotient
(S)
.
By the universal property of the cooperad
(\mathrm{q}\mathrm{cuAs})^{\text{!`}}
, it follows that there exists a unique (injective) morphism
\mathcal{C}\rightarrowtail(\mathrm{q}\mathrm{cuAs})^{\text{!`}}
such that the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE).}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{q}\mathrm{cuAs})^{\text{!`}}}

The injective map
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{C}\rightarrowtail\mathrm{coFree}(sE)
can be factored as
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{C}\rightarrowtail\mathrm{Gr}((\mathrm{q}\mathrm{cuAs})^{\text{!`}})\cong(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{q}\mathrm{cuAs})^{\text{!`}}\rightarrowtail\mathrm{coFree}(sE)
, where the second isomorphism is the Poincaré–Birkhoff–Witt type isomorphism which appears in the proof of Theorem
1.35
.
Its image is precisely
(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{q}\mathrm{cuAs})^{\text{!`}}\cong s\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern
