so
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{C}\cong\mathrm{Gr}((\mathrm{q}\mathrm{cuAs})^{\text{!`}})\cong(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{q}\mathrm{cuAs})^{\text{!`}}
.
Since the previous inclusions are strict and we are working with complete objects, this proves that
\mathcal{C}\cong(\mathrm{q}\mathrm{cuAs})^{\text{!`}}
.

The curvature is computed directly from its definition given in Section
1.3
.

In order to have an
\mathbb{S}
-cofibrant resolution, it is enough to remark that the curvature is non-zero, that for the weight filtration given by its generators,
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{cuAs}
is connected bounded below weight graded and that the filtration
F_{\bullet}
on
\mathrm{cuAs}
is induced by the graduation given by the number of

.
We can therefore apply Theorem
1.35
.
∎

We make the algebras over the curved operad
\mathrm{cuA}_{\infty}
explicit and we compare them with the literature.

Proposition 2.5
.

A
\mathrm{cuA}_{\infty}
-algebra
\gamma_{A}:\mathrm{cuA}_{\infty}\to\mathrm{End}_{(A,d_{A})}
is equivalent to a (complete) graded vector space
(A,\,F_{\bullet})
equipped with an operation
m_{0}:\mathbf{K}\to F_{1}A
of degree
-2
and for all
n\geq 1
and
T\subseteq[n]
, with filtered operations

m_{n}^{T}:A^{\otimes(n-|T|)}\to A\text{ of degree }n+|T|-2,

where
m_{1}=d_{A}
, which together satisfy the following identities:

(2.3)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}\partial(m_{2}^{\{1\}})&=&m_{2}\circ(m_{1}^{\{1\}},-)-\operatorname{id}_{A},\\
\partial(m_{2}^{\{2\}})&=&m_{2}\circ(-,m_{1}^{\{1\}})-\operatorname{id}_{A},\end{array}\right.

and for
(n,\,T)\neq(2,\,\{1\})
and
(n,\,T)\neq(2,\,\{2\})

(2.4)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p+q+r=n\\
T=T_{0}^{\prime}\sqcup(T_{1}+p)\sqcup(T_{0}^{\prime\prime}+q)\\
T_{0}=T_{0}^{\prime}\sqcup T^{\prime\prime}_{0}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{(p+|T_{0}^{\prime\prime}|)q+r+|T_{0}^{\prime}|(|T_{1}|+1)}m_{p+1+r}^{T_{0}}\circ_{(p+1-|T_{0}^{\prime}|)}m_{q}^{T_{1}}=0,

where
\max T_{0}^{\prime}<p\leq p+q<\min T_{0}^{\prime\prime}
and where we have identified the maps
m_{n}
and
m_{n}^{\emptyset}
.

Up to the sign convention, this notion of algebras coincides with the notions of homotopy-unital filtered
\mathrm{A}_{\infty}
-algebras used in
[
FOOO07
, Section 3.3.1]
.

Proof.

An operad morphism
\gamma_{A}:\hat{\Omega}\mathrm{cuAs}^{\text{!`}}\to\mathrm{End}_{(A,\,d_{A})}
is characterized by
(A,d_{A})
and a degree
0
map of
\mathbb{S}
-modules
s^{-1}\overline{\mathrm{cuAs}^{\text{!`}}}\to\mathrm{End}_{A}
. Therefore it corresponds to a (complete) graded vector space
(A,F_{\bullet})
and a collection of filtered applications

m_{n}^{T}:A^{\otimes(n-|T|)}\to A,\text{ of degree }n+|T|-2\text{ for all }n\geq 0\text{ and }T\subseteq[n],

such that
m_{0}:\mathbf{K}\to F_{1}A
and
m_{1}=d_{A}
, where each
m_{n}^{T}
corresponds to the image of
\nu_{n}^{T}
through
\gamma_{A}
.
The partial decomposition map on
\mathrm{cuAs}^{\text{!`}}
is given by

\Delta_{(1)}^{\theta}(\nu_{n}^{T})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p+q+r=n\\
T=T_{0}^{\prime}\sqcup(T_{1}+p)\sqcup(T_{0}^{\prime\prime}+q)\\
T_{0}=T_{0}^{\prime}\sqcup T^{\prime\prime}_{0}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{(q-1)(p+|T_{0}^{\prime\prime}|)+|T_{0}^{\prime}||T_{1}|}\nu_{p+1+r}^{T_{0}}\circ_{p+1-|T_{0}^{\prime}|}\nu_{q}^{T_{1}}.

The fact that
\gamma_{A}
commutes with the predifferentials and the fact that
\gamma_{A}
sends the curvature to the curvature ensure that Equations (
2.3
) and (
2.4
) are satisfied in accordance with the conditions
n\geq 0
and
T\subseteq[n]
.

The correspondence with the notion of homotopy unit given in
[
FOOO07
, Definition 3.3.2]
is given as follows: the homotopy unit
\mathbf{e}
in this reference coincides with the image
m_{1}^{\{1\}}(1_{\mathbf{K}})=\gamma_{A}\left(\nu_{1}^{\{1\}}\right)(1_{\mathbf{K}})
.
The maps
\mathfrak{h}_{k}
coincide with

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\geq 1\\
T\subseteq[n+k-1]\\
|T|=k-1\end{subarray}}m_{n+k-1}^{T}=\gamma_{A}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\geq 1\\
T\subseteq[n+k-1]\\
|T|=k-1\end{subarray}}\nu_{n+k-1}^{T}\right).

∎

2.2.
Complex curved Lie algebras

In
[
BM18
]
, the author has defined the operads
\mathrm{Cx}
and
\mathrm{Cx}_{\infty}
and he has used them to propose a new description of the notion of complexe structure.
In order to define the notion of
\mathrm{Cx}_{\infty}
-space, we add a
0
-ary generator to these operads so that our operads encode (homotopy) complex curved Lie algebras instead of (homotopy) complex Lie algebras.

We recall some notations and results from
[
BM18
]
.

Its differential is zero and there is an isomorphism of

-modules

.

\mathrm{Cx}^{\text{!`}}=\left(\mathrm{Lie}_{1}^{\text{!`}}\hat{\circ}\mathbb{R}\left[\tiny\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern \overline{\jmath}^{c}_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}:=(\overline{l}_{n}^{c};\overline{\imath}_{k_{1}}^{c},\ldots,\overline{\imath}^{c}_{k_{n}})
, where

and the elements

, the generators of
\mathrm{Cx}^{\text{!`}}
. The decomposition map is given on generators by

(2.5)

\Delta_{\Lambda^{c}}\left(\overline{\jmath}^{c}_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}\right)=\\
\sum\frac{1}{p!}\beta^{\sigma}_{k^{\prime}_{j},k^{\prime\prime}_{j}}\times\left(\overline{\jmath}_{l^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,l^{\prime}_{p}}^{c};\overline{\jmath}_{k^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,k^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma(q_{1})}}^{c},\overline{\jmath}_{k^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma(q_{1}+1)},\ldots,k^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma(q_{1}+q_{2})}}^{c},\ldots\right)^{\sigma^{-1}},

where the sum
\sum
is given by
\sum_{q_{1}+\cdots+q_{p}=n}\sum_{\sigma\in Sh_{q_{1},\ldots,q_{p}}}\sum_{k^{\prime}_{j}+k^{\prime\prime}_{j}=k_{j}}
(where
Sh_{q_{1},\ldots,q_{p}}
is the set of
(q_{1},\ldots,q_{p})
-shuffles), the integers
l^{\prime}_{i}
are defined by
l^{\prime}_{i}:=k^{\prime}_{\sigma(q_{1}+\cdots+q_{i-1}+1)}+\cdots+k^{\prime}_{\sigma(q_{1}+\cdots+q_{i})}
and the number
\beta^{\sigma}_{k^{\prime}_{j},k^{\prime\prime}_{j}}
is a sign defined in
[
BM18
, Proposition 3.6]
.
Finally, the curvature on
\mathrm{Cx}^{\text{!`}}
is defined by

\theta^{c}\left(\overline{\jmath}^{c}_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}-{\tiny\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern Proof.

\mathrm{cCx}
is zero so the only thing to prove is that the map
[\theta,-]
is zero.

We put a
weight
filtration
\mathrm{cCx}^{(\bullet)}
on
\mathrm{cCx}
by counting the number of the generators.

Lemma 2.8
.

A
\mathrm{cCx}
-algebra on a complete gr-dg
\mathbb{R}
-vector space
A
is the same data as a complete curved Lie
\mathbb{C}
-algebra
(A,[-,-],d_{A},\theta_{A})
with curvature
\theta_{A}\in F_{1}A
.

Proof.

A map of complete curved operad
\mathrm{cCx}\to\mathrm{End}_{A}
is characterized by the images of the generators

\textstyle{\bullet}

and

\textstyle{1}

\textstyle{s^{-1}}

which give respectively a degree

map
\theta_{A}:\mathbb{K}\to A
, a degree

map

and a degree

anti-symmetric map

. The relations defining
\mathrm{cCx}
ensure that
J
provides a
\mathbb{C}
-action, that
[-,-]
is a Lie bracket of degree
1
which is
\mathbb{C}
-linear.
The fact that the curvature of
\mathrm{cCx}
is sent to the curvature of
\mathrm{End}_{A}
says that
{d_{A}}^{2}=[\theta_{A},-]
.
∎

2.3.
The Koszul dual curved cooperad associated with
\mathrm{cCx}

We show now that the curved operad
\mathrm{cCx}
is Koszul and we describe its Koszul dual curved cooperad
\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}
.

Theorem 2.9
.

The presentation of the curved operad
\mathrm{cCx}
given above is inhomogeneous quadratic (we could even say more precisely
constant-quadratic
).
It is moreover a Koszul curved operad.

\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}

0.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-9.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\raise-9.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-12.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-12.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\raise-12.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}
.
The curvature
\theta^{c}
is defined by

\theta^{c}\left(\jmath_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}{\tiny\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.27777pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 2.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.27777pt\hbox{$\textstyle{1}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 7.40283pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.27777pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.97221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.27777pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 3.70142pt\raise-3.97221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.27777pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 7.40283pt\raise-3.97221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.27777pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-6.97221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.27777pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 3.70142pt\raise-6.97221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.27777pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 7.40283pt\raise-6.97221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.27777pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-9.97221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.27777pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 3.70142pt\raise-9.97221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.27777pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 7.40283pt\raise-9.97221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.27777pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}}&\textrm{if }n=1\textrm{ and }k_{1}=2,\\
0&\textrm{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

The decomposition map
\Delta^{\theta}
is given by the formula

\Delta^{\theta}\left(\jmath_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}^{c}\right)=\\
\sum\frac{1}{p!}\beta^{\sigma}_{k^{\prime}_{j},k^{\prime\prime}_{j}}\times\left(\jmath_{l^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,l^{\prime}_{p}}^{c};\jmath_{k^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,k^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma(q_{1})}}^{c},\jmath_{k^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma(q_{1}+1)},\ldots,k^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma(q_{1}+q_{2})}}^{c},\ldots\right)^{\sigma^{-1}}

where the sum
\sum
is given by
\sum_{q_{1}+\cdots+q_{p}=n}\sum_{\sigma\in Sh_{q_{1},\ldots,q_{p}}}\sum_{k^{\prime}_{j}+k^{\prime\prime}_{j}=k_{j}}
, the integers
l^{\prime}_{i}
are defined by
l^{\prime}_{i}\coloneqq k^{\prime}_{\sigma(q_{1}+\cdots+q_{i-1}+1)}+\cdots+k^{\prime}_{\sigma(q_{1}+\cdots+q_{i})}
and the number
\beta^{\sigma}_{k^{\prime}_{j},k^{\prime\prime}_{j}}
is a number defined in
[
BM18
, Proposition 3.6]
and is recalled in the remark below.
Moreover, the natural map

f_{\kappa}:\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}\coloneqq\hat{\Omega}\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}\to\mathrm{cCx}

is an
\mathbb{S}
-cofibrant resolution.

Remark 2.10
.

•

The difference between the formula given in Proposition 3.6 in
[
BM18
]
and the formula here is that in the Theorem above the integers
p
and
q_{i}
’s can be
0
. In this case, we denote by
\jmath^{c}_{\emptyset}
the corresponding generator.

•

Be aware of the fact that the curvature
\theta:I\to\mathrm{cCx}
and
\theta^{c}:\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}\to I
are unrelated. The curvature
\theta^{c}
encodes that
\mathbb{C}
isn’t augmented.

•

The numbers
\beta^{\sigma}_{k^{\prime}_{j},k^{\prime\prime}_{j}}
are equal to

\beta^{\sigma}_{k^{\prime}_{j},k^{\prime\prime}_{j}}:=\operatorname{sgn}_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}\sigma\times\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}_{j},k^{\prime\prime}_{j}}^{\sigma}\times\prod_{i=1}^{p}\alpha_{k^{\prime}_{\sigma(q_{1}+\cdots+q_{i-1}+1)},\,\ldots,k^{\prime}_{\sigma(q_{1}+\cdots+q_{i})}}

where
\operatorname{sgn}_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}\sigma
is the signature of the restriction of
\sigma
to the indices
j
such that
k_{j}
is odd (after relabeling the remaining
\sigma(j)
in a way that the order of the
\sigma(j)
does not change) and where

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}_{j},\,k^{\prime\prime}_{j}}^{\sigma}&\coloneqq&(-1)^{\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{\sigma(i)}^{\prime\prime}(k_{\sigma(i+1)}^{\prime}+\cdots+k_{\sigma(n)}^{\prime})}\\
\alpha_{k^{\prime}_{1},\,\ldots,\,k^{\prime}_{q}}&\coloneqq&\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\in Sh_{k^{\prime}_{1},\,\cdots,\,k^{\prime}_{q}}}\operatorname{sgn}\sigma^{\prime},\,\textrm{with convention }\alpha_{0,\,\ldots,\,0}:=1.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

The proof follows the same flow as the proof of Theorem
2.3
.
The presentation of
\mathrm{cCx}
given in Lemma
2.7
is easily seen to be inhomogeneous quadratic, except for Condition (3) in Definition
1.31
that will be proven during the proof.

To prove the Koszulness of
\mathrm{cCx}
(see Definition
1.33
), we first remark that
\mathrm{cCx}
is the completion of its associated graded (for the filtration
F_{\bullet}
) and that the presentation given above behaves correctly with the filtration.
Then the associated quadratic curved operad
\mathrm{q}\mathrm{cCx}
is also the completion of its associated graded and as quadratic operads
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{q}\mathrm{cCx}\cong\mathrm{q}\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{cCx}
.
Finally we prove that
\mathrm{q}\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{cCx}
is a quadratic Koszul operad.
We have

(\mathrm{q}\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{cCx})^{\text{!`}}\circ_{\kappa}\mathrm{q}\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{cCx}\cong\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 2}.

It is proved in
[
BM18
]
that the operad
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{q}\mathrm{Cx}
is Koszul (it is simply called
\mathrm{q}\mathrm{Cx}
there) therefore we get that the quasi-isomorphism
(\mathrm{q}\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{cCx})^{\text{!`}}\circ_{\kappa}\mathrm{q}\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{cCx}\xrightarrow{\sim}I
and the fact that
\mathrm{q}\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{cCx}
is a quadratic Koszul operad.

We now compute the Koszul dual curved cooperad
\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}
.
We first prove that

\mathcal{C}=\left(\left\{\jmath_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}^{c}\right\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\geq 0\\
k_{j}\geq 0\end{subarray}},\Delta^{\theta},0,\theta^{c}\right)

is a sub-cooperad of
\mathcal{T}^{c}\left(sE\right)
, for

, satisfying the property for which
(\mathrm{q}\mathrm{cCx})^{\text{!`}}
is universal (so that
\mathcal{C}\hookrightarrow(\mathrm{q}\mathrm{cCx})^{\text{!`}}
).
We use the (cohomological degree 0) inclusion

(2.6)

\upsilon:\jmath_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}^{c}\mapsto\sum_{k\geq 0}\frac{1}{k!}\jmath_{\underbrace{{\scriptstyle 0},\dots,{\scriptstyle 0}}_{k\textrm{ times}},k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}^{c,[k]}\ ,

where
\jmath_{0,\dots,0,k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}^{c,[k]}
is the element
\jmath_{0,\dots,0,k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}^{c}
on which we have grafted the element

in the
k
first positions.
The equality

(\upsilon\circ\upsilon)(\Delta^{\theta}(\jmath_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}^{c}))=\Delta(\upsilon(\jmath_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}^{c}))

results from two reasons. First, the coefficients
\operatorname{sgn}_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}\sigma
,
\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}_{j},k^{\prime\prime}_{j}}^{\sigma}
and
\alpha_{k^{\prime}_{1},\,\ldots,k^{\prime}_{q}}
are the same for
\jmath_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}^{c}
and
\jmath_{0,\dots,0,k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}^{c,[k]}
.
Secondly the computation in the cofree cooperad of
\Delta\Big(\jmath_{0,\dots,0,k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}^{c,[k]}\Big)
provides an element

\Big(\jmath_{0,\dots,0,l^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,l^{\prime}_{p}}^{c,[l_{0}]}\ ;\ \jmath_{0,\dots,0,k^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,k^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma(q_{1})}}^{c,[l_{1}]},\ \jmath_{0,\dots,0,k^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma(q_{1}+1)},\ldots,k^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma(q_{1}+q_{2})}}^{c,[l_{2}]},\ \ldots\Big)

with an extra factor
\frac{k!}{l_{0}!l_{1}!l_{2}!\dots}=\mathrm{Card}(Sh_{l_{0},l_{1},l_{2},\ldots})
.
It remains to show that the composite

\mathcal{C}\rightarrowtail\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)\twoheadrightarrow S\coloneqq\left.\left(I\oplus\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)^{(2)}\right)\middle/\left({\scriptstyle s^{2}}\left(\mathrm{q}R\right)\oplus(\operatorname{id}+{\scriptstyle s^{2}}\tilde{\theta})(I)\right)\right.

where

0.0pt\raise-1.7014pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}}\right)\right\rangle,

is zero.
A direct computation of the image of the different elements
\jmath_{\emptyset}^{c}
,
\jmath_{0}^{c}
,
\jmath_{1}^{c}
,
\jmath_{2}^{c}
,
\jmath_{0,0}^{c}
,
\jmath_{0,1}^{c}
and
\jmath_{0,0,0}^{c}
through
\upsilon
shows the result.
By the universal property of the cooperad
(\mathrm{q}\mathrm{cCx})^{\text{!`}}
, we get the (unique) factorisation
\mathcal{C}\rightarrowtail(\mathrm{q}\mathrm{cCx})^{\text{!`}}\rightarrowtail\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)
.

As a byproduct of the above computations, we get that
\upsilon(\jmath_{0}^{c})\in\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)
is sent to 0 in the coideal quotient
(S)
(whose definition is given in
[
BMDC20
, Definition 5.3]
) so the counit of
\mathcal{T}^{c}(sE)
cannot factor through the coideal quotient
(S)
.
This finishes to prove that
\mathrm{cCx}
is inhomogeneous quadratic.

The injective map
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{C}\rightarrowtail\mathrm{coFree}(sE)
can be factored as
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{C}\rightarrowtail\mathrm{Gr}((\mathrm{q}\mathrm{cCx})^{\text{!`}})\cong(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{q}\mathrm{cCx})^{\text{!`}}\rightarrowtail\mathrm{coFree}(sE)
, where the second isomorphism is the Poincaré–Birkhoff–Witt type isomorphism which appears in the proof of Theorem
1.35
.
Its image is precisely
(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{q}\mathrm{cCx})^{\text{!`}}\cong\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\raise-3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-6.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-6.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-4.25pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\lx@xy@point}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\raise-6.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-9.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\raise-9.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-12.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-12.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\raise-12.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}\oplus\mathrm{q}\mathrm{Cx}^{\text{!`}}
so
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{C}\cong\mathrm{Gr}((\mathrm{q}\mathrm{cCx})^{\text{!`}})\cong(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{q}\mathrm{cCx})^{\text{!`}}
.
Since the previous inclusions are strict and we are working with complete objects, this proves that
\mathcal{C}\cong(\mathrm{q}\mathrm{cCx})^{\text{!`}}
.

In order to have an
\mathbb{S}
-cofibrant resolution, it is enough to remark that the curvature is non-zero, that for the weight filtration given by its generators,
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{cCx}
is connected bounded below weight graded and that the filtration
F_{\bullet}
on
\mathrm{cCx}
is induced by the graduation given by the number of
{\scriptstyle s^{-1}}\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise .
We can therefore apply Theorem
1.35
.
∎

Corollary 2.11
.

The infinitesimal decomposition map on
\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}
is given by

\Delta_{(1)}(\jmath_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}^{c})=\\
\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p+q=n+1\\
p,q\geq 0\end{subarray}}\sum_{\sigma\in Sh_{q,p-1}}\sum_{K}\alpha^{\sigma}_{k^{\prime}_{j},k^{\prime\prime}_{j}}\times\left(\jmath_{k^{\prime},k_{\sigma(q+1)},\ldots,k_{\sigma(n)}}^{c}\circ_{1}\jmath_{k^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,k^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma(q)}}^{c}\right)^{\sigma^{-1}},

where

K\coloneqq\{k^{\prime}_{\sigma(j)}+k^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma(j)}=k_{\sigma(j)},\ k^{\prime}_{\sigma(j)}=k_{\sigma(j)}\textrm{ for }j>q\textrm{ and }k^{\prime}=k^{\prime}_{\sigma(1)}+\cdots+k^{\prime}_{\sigma(q)}\},

and where
\alpha^{\sigma}_{k^{\prime}_{j},k^{\prime\prime}_{j}}:=\operatorname{sgn}_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}\sigma\times\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}_{j},k^{\prime\prime}_{j}}^{\sigma}\times\alpha_{k^{\prime}_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,k^{\prime}_{\sigma(q)}}
.

Proof.

The formula is the same as the formula given in Proposition 3.6 in
[
BM18
]
except that
p,q\geq 0
instead of
p,q\geq 1
(the case
p=0
appears when
n=0
).
∎

3.
Algebraic curved twisting morphisms

We now consider algebras and coalgebras in
\mathbf{R}
-modules where
\mathbf{R}
is a unital
\mathbf{K}
-cdga.

3.1.
Formal constructions in the curved context

3.1.1.
Free curved algebra

We first recall from
[
BMDC20
, Proposition C.35]
the free-fogetful adjunction for curved algebras over a curved operad
(\mathcal{P},d,\theta,\eta)
.

Proposition 3.1
.

The forgetful functor
U:\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}\to\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{gr}}
admits a left adjoint, called the
free
\mathcal{P}
-algebra functor
,
F_{\mathcal{P}}:\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{gr}}\to\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}
given by

(V,d_{V})\mapsto F_{\mathcal{P}}(V,d_{V})\coloneqq\left(\mathcal{P}(V)/\left(\im\left({d_{\mathcal{P}(V)}}^{2}-\gamma(\theta\otimes\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}})\otimes\operatorname{id}_{V}\right)\right),d_{\overline{\mathcal{P}(V)}}\right).

Remark 3.2
.

We remark that the ideal
\left(\im\left({d_{\mathcal{P}(V)}}^{2}-\gamma(\theta\otimes\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}})\otimes\operatorname{id}_{V}\right)\right)
coincides with the ideal
\left(\im\left(\eta\otimes({d_{V}}^{2})-\theta\otimes\operatorname{id}_{V}\right)\right)
appearing in
[
BMDC20
]
.

Definition 3.3
.

We call a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
A

quasi-free
if there exists a gr-dg module
V
and a predifferential (see Definition
3.4
)
d:\mathcal{P}(V)\to\mathcal{P}(V)
such that

A=\mathcal{P}(V)/\left(\im\left(d^{2}-\gamma_{\mathcal{P}(V)}(\theta\otimes\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}(V)})\right)\right).

This notion is different from that of a free
\mathcal{P}
-algebra because in the quasi-free case the predifferential
d
is not a priori induced by a map
d_{V}:V\to V
(that is it might be different from any map
d_{\mathcal{P}}\circ\operatorname{id}_{V}+\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}\circ^{\prime}d_{V}
).

Similarly, there exists a cofree-forgetful adjunction for curved coalgebras over a curved altipotent cooperad
(\mathcal{C},d,\theta^{c},\varepsilon)
that we present in Section
3.2.2
.

3.1.2.
(Co)Derivations and predifferentials

In the context of a gr-dg operad
(\mathcal{Q},d_{\mathcal{Q}})
and a gr-dg cooperad
(\mathcal{D},d_{\mathcal{D}})
, the notions of
\mathcal{Q}
-derivation and of
\mathcal{D}
-coderivation are similar to the ones present in the classical literature.
We recall some definitions that aren’t always clear in the literature for us and propositions for the sake of completeness.

Definition 3.4
.

(1)

A
derivation
on a
\mathcal{Q}
-algebra
B
is a linear map
d:B\to B
satisfying the equation
d\cdot\gamma_{B}=\gamma_{B}\cdot(\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Q}}\circ^{\prime}d)
.

(2)

A
predifferential
on a
\mathcal{Q}
-algebra
B
is a linear map
d:B\to B
of degree
-1
satisfying the equation
d\cdot\gamma_{B}=\gamma_{B}\cdot(d_{\mathcal{Q}}\circ\operatorname{id}_{A}+\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Q}}\circ^{\prime}d)
.

(3)

A
coderivation
on a
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebra
D
is a linear map
d^{c}:D\to D
satisfying the equation
\Delta_{D}\cdot d^{c}=(\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{D}}\circ^{\prime}d^{c})\cdot\Delta_{D}
.

(4)

A
predifferential
on a
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebra
D
is a linear map
d^{c}:D\to D
of degree
-1
satisfying the equation
\Delta_{D}\cdot d^{c}=(d_{\mathcal{D}}\circ\operatorname{id}_{D}+\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{D}}\circ^{\prime}d^{c})\cdot\Delta_{D}
.

It follows from the above definitions that when
d_{A}
is a predifferential of a
\mathcal{Q}
-algebra
A
, then a degree
-1
gr-dg linear map
d
is a derivation if and only if
d_{A}+d
is a predifferential of
B
.
A similar result holds for
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebras (without the gr-dg assumption).

More specifically, when a degree
-1
linear map
d
is derivation of the free
\mathcal{Q}
-algebra
\mathcal{Q}(V)
, then
d_{\mathcal{Q}}\circ\operatorname{id}_{V}+d
is a predifferential of
\mathcal{Q}(V)
.
Again a similar result holds for coderivations and predifferentials of cofree
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebras.

Proposition 3.5

(Propositions 6.3.6 and 6.3.8 in
[
LV12
]
)
.

Let
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{g}}
the category of graded
\mathbf{R}
-modules.

(1)

Any derivation on a free
\mathcal{Q}
-algebra
\mathcal{Q}(V)
is completely characterised by its restriction on the graded module of generators

\mathrm{Der}(\mathcal{Q}(V))\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{g}}}(V,\mathcal{Q}(V)).

(2)

Any coderivation on a cofree
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebra
\mathcal{D}(V)
is completely characterised by its projection onto the graded module of generators

\mathrm{coDer}(\mathcal{D}(V))\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{g}}}(\mathcal{D}(V),V).

3.2.
Bar (and cobar) constructions for curved (co)algebras

In this section,
(\mathcal{P},d,\theta,\eta)
refers to a curved operad and
(\mathcal{C},d,\theta^{c},\varepsilon)
refers to a curved altipotent cooperad.
To any curved twisting morphism
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}
, we associate two functors

\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}:\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}\rightleftharpoons\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{coalg}:\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}

forming an adjunction and representing a notion of algebraic curved twisting morphisms.

3.2.1.
Bar construction of a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra

Let us denote by
\mathcal{C}^{pg}
the pg cooperad underlying the curved altipotent cooperad
\mathcal{C}
.
Let
(A,d_{A},\gamma_{A})
be a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra.

We denote by
d^{r}_{\alpha}
the coderivation (of the cofree
\mathcal{C}^{pg}
-coalgebra
\mathcal{C}(A)
) which extends the composite
\mathcal{C}(A)\xrightarrow{\alpha\circ\operatorname{id}_{A}}\mathcal{P}(A)\xrightarrow{\gamma_{A}}A
.
We also write
d_{\mathcal{C}(A)}=d_{\mathcal{C}}\circ\operatorname{id}_{A}+\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{C}}\circ^{\prime}d_{A}
which is a predifferential of the
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra
\mathcal{C}(A)
.

Lemma 3.6
.

Because
\alpha
is a curved twisting morphism, the predifferential
d_{\mathcal{C}(A)}+d_{\alpha}^{r}
satisfies

(d_{\mathcal{C}(A)}+d_{\alpha}^{r})^{2}=-(\theta^{c}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{C}(A)})\cdot\Delta_{\mathcal{C}(A)}.

Proof.

A direct computation gives

\displaystyle(d_{\mathcal{C}(A)}+d^{r}_{\alpha})^{2}

\displaystyle={d_{\mathcal{C}}}^{2}\circ\operatorname{id}_{A}+\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{C}}\circ^{\prime}{d_{A}}^{2}+d^{r}_{\partial\alpha+\frac{1}{2}[\alpha,\alpha]}

\displaystyle=((\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{C}}\otimes\theta^{c}-\theta^{c}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{C}})\cdot\Delta_{(1)})\circ\operatorname{id}_{A}+\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{C}}\circ^{\prime}(\gamma_{A}(\theta\circ\operatorname{id}_{A}))+d^{r}_{-\Theta}

\displaystyle=-(\theta^{c}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{C}(A)})\cdot\Delta_{\mathcal{C}(A)},

where we have used the fact that
\alpha
is a curved twisting morphism for the second equality.
∎

Definition 3.7
.

The
bar construction of a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
(A,d_{A},\gamma_{A})
with respect to
\alpha
is

\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A\coloneqq(\mathcal{C}(A),d_{b}\coloneqq d_{\mathcal{C}(A)}+d^{r}_{\alpha}).

It satisfies
{d_{b}}^{2}=-(\theta^{c}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{C}(A)})\cdot\Delta_{\mathcal{C}(A)}
by the previous lemma and this therefore defines a functor
\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}:\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}\to\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{coalg}
.

Remark 3.8
.

For the interested reader, we remark that the bar construction is quasi-cofree in the sense described in next section (Section
3.2.2
) since it is equal to the sub-
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra of
\mathcal{C}(A)
with relations given by

S=\mathcal{C}(A)/\ker\left((d_{\mathcal{C}(A)}+d_{\alpha})^{2}+(\theta^{c}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{C}(A)})\cdot\Delta_{\mathcal{C}(A)}\right)=\{0\}.

In the following proposition, we propose several equivalent definitions of the notion of
\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cL_{\infty}}
-algebras.

Proposition 3.9
.

A
\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cL_{\infty}}
-algebra structure on a gr-dg module
(A,d_{A})
is the data of a morphism of curved operad
\gamma_{A}:\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cL_{\infty}}\to\mathrm{End}_{(A,d_{A})}
. It is equivalent to each of the following data:

(1)

an operadic curved twisting morphism
\alpha_{A}
in
\mathrm{Tw}(\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}},\mathrm{End}_{(A,d_{A})})
such that
(\alpha_{A})_{|I}=0
,

(2)

a predifferential
D_{A}:\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}}(A)\to\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}}(A)
(of degree
-1
) such that
{D_{A}}_{|A}=d_{A}
and which satisfies
{D_{A}}^{2}=0
,

(3)

a family of skew-symmetric maps
l_{n}:A^{\otimes n}\to A
of degree
|l_{n}|=-1
, for all
n\geq 0
with
l_{1}=d_{A}
which satisfy the relations

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p+q=n+1\\
p\geq 1,q\geq 0\end{subarray}}\sum_{\sigma\in Sh_{q,\,p-1}^{-1}}({l}_{p}\circ_{1}{l}_{q})^{\sigma}=0.

Proof.

The equivalence between the definition of
\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cL_{\infty}}
-algebra structure on a gr-dg module
(A,d_{A})
and the data of a twisting morphism
\alpha_{A}
which cancels on
I
is Theorem
1.26
restricted to morphisms instead of lax morphisms.
Indeed, assuming
(1)
, we get a predifferential
d_{b}=d_{\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}}(A)}+d_{\alpha_{A}}^{r}=\operatorname{id}_{\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}}}\circ^{\prime}d_{A}+d_{\alpha_{A}}^{r}
, where
d_{\alpha_{A}}^{r}
is defined in Section
3.2.1
with the operadic curved twisting morphism
\alpha_{A}
and the fact that
A
can be seen as an
\mathrm{End}_{(A,d_{A})}
-algebra (by the identity map
\operatorname{id}_{\mathrm{End}_{(A,d_{A})}}
).
Assuming
(2)
,

D_{A}-\operatorname{id}_{\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}}}\circ^{\prime}d_{A}
is a coderivation characterised by a linear map
\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}}(A)\to A
whose component
A\to A
is
0
, therefore by an
\mathbb{S}
-module morphism
\alpha_{A}:\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}}\to\mathrm{End}_{(A,d_{A})}
which cancels on
I
.
It follows that
D_{A}-\operatorname{id}_{\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}}}\circ^{\prime}d_{A}=d_{\alpha_{A}}^{r}
and the computation made in the proof of Lemma
3.6
shows that
d^{r}_{\Theta_{H}+\partial\alpha_{A}+\frac{1}{2}[\alpha_{A},\alpha_{A}]}=0
, with
\Theta_{H}=\theta_{\mathrm{End}_{(A,d_{A})}}\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}}}+\eta_{\mathrm{End}_{(A,d_{A})}}\cdot\theta^{c}_{\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}}}={d_{A}}^{2}\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cLie}^{\text{!`}}}
, so
\alpha_{A}
is an operadic curved twisting morphism.

The equivalence between
(2)
and
(3)
is obtained by denoting
\{l_{n}\}_{n\geq 0}
the maps which characterise the coderivation
D_{A}
.
The desired equation in
(3)
is the projection of the equality
{D_{A}}^{2}=0
to
A
, which characterises the whole equality since
{D_{A}}^{2}
is also a coderivation.
∎

3.2.2.
Cofree curved coalgebra

Let
(\mathcal{D},d,\varepsilon)
be a pg cooperad (no curvature a priori).
In order to describe the notion of sub-
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra generated by a pg module in
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{pg}}
, we first describe the notion of sub-
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebra of a
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebra
D
with relations given by
S
.

We follow and adapt the definitions given in
[
BMDC20
, Section 5.1]
and based on Appendix B in
[
Val08
]
.
As in
[
BMDC20
]
, we consider strict morphisms (that is morphisms
f:C\to D
such that for all
p\geq 0
,
f(F_{p}C)=f(C)\cap F_{p}D
) in order to identify certain coimages with cokernels (and in the dual case certain images with kernels).

Definition 3.10
.

In this definition, we consider a pg cooperad
(\mathcal{D},d,\varepsilon)
(no curvature a priori).

•

Let
J\rightarrowtail D\twoheadrightarrow Q
be an exact sequence in
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{pg}}
, where
D
is a
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebra. The epimorphism
C\twoheadrightarrow Q
in
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{pg}}
is a
coideal epimorphism
if
J\rightarrowtail D
is a monomorphism of
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebras in
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{pg}}
.

•

Let
\xi:D\twoheadrightarrow S
be an epimorphism in
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{pg}}
, where
D
is a
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebra. We consider the category
\mathcal{S}_{\xi}
of exact sequences
(\textbf{S}):J\rightarrowtail D\twoheadrightarrow Q
such that
J\rightarrowtail D
is a monomorphism of
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebras and such that the composite
J\rightarrowtail D\twoheadrightarrow S
is equal to
0
. A morphism between
(\textbf{S})
and
(\textbf{S}^{\prime}):J^{\prime}\rightarrowtail D\twoheadrightarrow Q^{\prime}
is given by a pair
(i:J\to J^{\prime},\,p:Q\to Q^{\prime})
such that
i
is a morphism of
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebras and
p
is a morphism in
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{pg}}
, and such that the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q^{\prime}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

•

We aim to consider the largest sub-
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebra of
D
such that the post-composition with
D\twoheadrightarrow S
is zero. This notion is given by the terminal object
(\overline{\textbf{S}}):C(S)\rightarrowtail D\twoheadrightarrow(S)^{c}
in
\mathcal{S}_{\xi}
, when the latter admits one.
When it exists, the sub-
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebra
C(S)
is called the
sub-
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebra of
D
with relations given by
S
.

We now make the coideal quotient
(S)^{c}
and the sub-
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebra
C(S)
explicit.

Definition 3.11
.

We denote by
i_{D}^{S}:D\hookrightarrow S\oplus D
the inclusion and by
\pi_{D}^{S}:S\oplus D\twoheadrightarrow D
the projection.

•

The
multilinear part
in
S
of
\mathcal{D}(S\oplus D)
is given either by the cokernel

\coker\left(\mathcal{D}(D)\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{D}}\circ i_{D}^{S}}\mathcal{D}(S\oplus D)\right),

or equivalently, by the kernel

\ker\left(\mathcal{D}(S\oplus D)\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{D}}\circ\pi_{D}^{S}}\mathcal{D}(D)\right),

by means of the fact that the monoidal structure commutes with colimits and
i_{D}^{S}
is a section of
\pi_{D}^{S}
.
It is denoted by
\mathcal{D}(\underline{S}\oplus D)
.

•

The
coideal quotient
(S)^{c}
of
D
generated by
\xi:D\twoheadrightarrow S
is given by the coimage

(S)^{c}:=\coim\left(D\xrightarrow{\Delta_{D}}\mathcal{D}(D)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{proj}\cdot(\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{D}}\circ(\xi\oplus\operatorname{id}_{D}))}\mathcal{D}(\underline{S}\oplus D)\right),

where the map
\mathrm{proj}
is the projection
\mathcal{D}(S\oplus D)\to\mathcal{D}(\underline{S}\oplus D)
.

We define in the next proposition the sub-
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebra
C(S)
as the kernel of
D\twoheadrightarrow(S)^{c}
.
Because
(S)^{c}
is a coimage, the map
D\twoheadrightarrow(S)^{c}
is a strict epimorphism (that is the filtration on
(S)^{c}
is the quotient filtration).
It follows that we have
(S)^{c}\cong\coker(C(S)\to D)
.

Proposition 3.12
.

The
sub-
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebra of
D
with relations given by
S
is

C(S)\coloneqq\ker(D\to(S)^{c}),

that is

C(S)=\ker\left(D\xrightarrow{\Delta_{D}}\mathcal{D}(D)\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{D}}\circ(\operatorname{id}_{D},\xi)}\mathcal{D}\hat{\circ}(D,S)\right),

where
\mathcal{D}\hat{\circ}(D,S)
is the part of
\mathcal{D}(D\oplus S)
linear in
S
(see the remark below).

Remark 3.13
.

The pg module
\mathcal{D}\hat{\circ}(D,S)
is defined by

\mathcal{D}\hat{\circ}(D,S)\coloneqq\hat{\bigoplus}_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{D}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\left(\bigoplus_{1\leq i\leq n}D\hat{\otimes}\cdots\hat{\otimes}D\hat{\otimes}\underbrace{S}_{i\text{th position}}\hat{\otimes}D\hat{\otimes}\cdots\hat{\otimes}D\right).

Proof.

To verify that
\Delta_{D}
induces (by restriction) a comultiplication
\Delta_{C(S)}:C(S)\to\mathcal{D}(C(S))
, we first remark that the composite
\flat

C(S)\xrightarrow{\Delta_{D}}\mathcal{D}(D)\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{D}}\circ\Delta_{D}}(\mathcal{D}\hat{\circ}\mathcal{D})(D)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{proj}\cdot(\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{D}\hat{\circ}\mathcal{D}}\circ(\xi\oplus\operatorname{id}_{D}))}(\mathcal{D}\hat{\circ}\mathcal{D})(\underline{S}\oplus D)

is zero since it is equal, by coassociativity of
\Delta_{D}
, to

(\mathrm{proj}\cdot(\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{D}\hat{\circ}\mathcal{D}}\circ(\xi\oplus\operatorname{id}_{D})))\cdot(\Delta_{\mathcal{D}}\circ\operatorname{id}_{D})\cdot\Delta_{D}=(\Delta_{\mathcal{D}}\circ\operatorname{id}_{D})\cdot(\mathrm{proj}\cdot(\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{D}}\circ(\xi\oplus\operatorname{id}_{D})))\cdot\Delta_{D}

which is zero on
C(S)=\ker(D\twoheadrightarrow(S)^{c})
.
Moreover, the projection of the map
\flat
to each

\mathcal{D}(n)\hat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}(\mathcal{D}(C)^{\hat{\otimes}k}\hat{\otimes}C(S)\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{D}(C)^{\hat{\otimes}(n-1-k)})

is given by applying the map
\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{D}}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{D}^{\otimes k}\otimes(\mathrm{proj}\cdot(\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{D}}\circ(\xi\oplus\operatorname{id}_{D})))\otimes\operatorname{id}_{D}^{\otimes(n-1-k)}
to the correct component of the image of
\Delta_{D}
.
This ensures that we can corestrict
\Delta_{C(S)}=\Delta_{D}:C(S)\to\mathcal{D}(C(S))
.

Then, by definition,
C(S)=\ker\left((\mathrm{proj}\cdot(\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{D}}\circ(\xi\oplus\operatorname{id}_{D})))\cdot\Delta_{D}\right)
so

C(S)\subseteq C(S)^{\prime}\coloneqq\ker\left(D\xrightarrow{\Delta_{D}}\mathcal{D}(D)\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{D}}\circ(\operatorname{id}_{D},\xi)}\mathcal{D}\hat{\circ}(D,S)\right).

For the same reason as before, the comultiplication
\Delta_{D}
restricts (and corestricts properly) to
C(S)^{\prime}
and by an iterated application of the comultiplication, we see that an element in
C(S)^{\prime}
is in fact in
C(S)
, so the wanted equality.
∎

We now use this notion of sub-
\mathcal{D}
-coalgebra with generators given by a pg module
S\in\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{pg}}
to build a cofree
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra functor, where
\mathcal{C}
is a curved altipotent cooperad.

Proposition 3.14
.

The forgetful functor
U^{c}:\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{coalg}\to\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{pg}}
admits a left adjoint, called the
cofree
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra functor
,
F_{\mathcal{C}}^{c}:\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{pg}}\to\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{coalg}
given by

(V,d_{V})\mapsto F_{\mathcal{C}}^{c}(V,d_{V})\coloneqq C(S)=\ker\left(\mathcal{C}(V)\twoheadrightarrow(S)^{c}\right)\subset\left(\mathcal{C}(V),d_{\mathcal{C}(V)}\right),

where
\mathcal{C}(V)
is seen as a coalgebra over the pg cooperad underlying
\mathcal{C}
and for

S\coloneqq\mathcal{C}(V)/\ker\left({d_{\mathcal{C}(V)}}^{2}+\left(\theta^{c}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{C}(V)}\right)\cdot\Delta_{\mathcal{C}(V)}\right).

We therefore have for all
(V,d_{V})\in\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{pg}}
and
(C,d_{C})\in\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{coalg}
the bijections

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{coalg}}\left((C,d_{C}),F_{\mathcal{C}}^{c}(V,d_{V})\right)\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{pg}}}\left(U^{c}(C,d_{C}),(V,d_{V})\right).

Proof.

The construction
F_{\mathcal{C}}^{c}
is functorial in
(V,d_{V})
since the map
\mathcal{C}(V)\to S
is functorial in
(V,d_{V})
.
By definition of
C(S)
, the composition
C(S)\to S
is zero, that is

C(S)\subseteq\ker\left({d_{\mathcal{C}(V)}}^{2}+\left(\theta^{c}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{C}(V)}\right)\cdot\Delta_{\mathcal{C}(V)}\right).

It follows that
C(S)
, which is a priori a coalgebra over the pg cooperad underlying
\mathcal{C}
, is in fact a coalgebra over the curved altipotent cooperad
\mathcal{C}
.

The universal property that
C(S)
satisfies ensures that the
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra morphisms
(C,d_{C})\to(C(S),d_{C(S)})
, that is also a morphisms of the coalgebras over the pg cooperad underlying
\mathcal{C}
, coincide with morphisms
(C,d_{C})\to(\mathcal{C}(V),d_{\mathcal{C}(V)})
of coalgebras over the pg cooperad underlying
\mathcal{C}
such that the composite
C\to\mathcal{C}(V)\twoheadrightarrow S
is zero.
This last condition is automatically satisfied because of the fact that the relation
{d_{C}}^{2}+\left(\theta^{c}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{C}\right)\cdot\Delta_{C}
is satisfied on
C
and that the morphisms of coalgebras in the pg context commute with the predifferentials and the comultiplication.
∎

Remark 3.15
.

For the curious reader, it is certainly a good exercise to understand the cofree cooperad recalled in Proposition
1.14
as a particular case of a cofree coalgebra over a colored cooperad for which altipotent cooperads are example of coalgebras. Hint: considering the infinitesimal coideal
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}
is a way to describe the cooperad structure of
\mathcal{C}
as a coalgebra over a certain colored cooperad.

Definition 3.16
.

We call a
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra
C

quasi-cofree
if there exists a graded module
V
and a predifferential (see Definition
3.4
)
d:\mathcal{C}(V)\to\mathcal{C}(V)
such that
C=C(S)
for

S\coloneqq\mathcal{C}(V)/\ker\left(d^{2}+\left(\theta^{c}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{C}(V)}\right)\cdot\Delta_{\mathcal{C}(V)}\right).

This notion is different from that of a cofree
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra because in the quasi-cofree case the predifferential
d
is not a priori induced by a map
d_{V}:V\to V
(that is it might be different from any map
d_{\mathcal{C}}\circ\operatorname{id}_{V}+\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{C}}\circ^{\prime}d_{V}
).

3.2.3.
Cobar construction of a
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra

Let
(C,d_{C},\Delta_{C})
be a
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra.
As in the previous section, we denote by
d_{\alpha}^{l}
the derivation of the free
\mathcal{P}
-algebra which extends the composite
C\xrightarrow{\Delta_{C}}\mathcal{C}(C)\xrightarrow{\alpha\circ\operatorname{id}_{C}}\mathcal{P}(C)
.
We also write
d_{\mathcal{P}(C)}=d_{\mathcal{P}}\circ\operatorname{id}_{C}+\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{C}}\circ^{\prime}d_{C}
which is a predifferential of the
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
\mathcal{P}(C)
.

Lemma 3.17
.

Because
\alpha
is a curved twisting morphism, the predifferential
d_{\mathcal{P}(C)}+d_{\alpha}^{l}
satisfies

(d_{\mathcal{P}(C)}-d_{\alpha}^{l})^{2}=\gamma_{\mathcal{P}(C)}(\theta\circ\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}(C)}).

Proof.

The proof is similar to the one of Lemma
3.6
.
We compute

\displaystyle(d_{\mathcal{P}(C)}-d^{l}_{\alpha})^{2}

\displaystyle={d_{\mathcal{P}}}^{2}\circ\operatorname{id}_{C}+\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}\circ^{\prime}{d_{C}}^{2}+d^{l}_{-\partial\alpha-\frac{1}{2}[\alpha,\alpha]}

\displaystyle=[\theta,\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}]\circ\operatorname{id}_{C}-\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}\circ^{\prime}((\theta^{c}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{C})\cdot\Delta_{C})+d^{l}_{\Theta}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{\mathcal{P}(C)}(\theta\circ\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}(C)}).

∎

Definition 3.18
.

The
cobar construction of a
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra
(C,d_{C},\Delta_{C})
with respect to
\alpha
is

\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}C\coloneqq(\mathcal{P}(C),d_{o}\coloneqq d_{\mathcal{P}(C)}-d^{l}_{\alpha}).

It satisfies
{d_{o}}^{2}=\gamma_{\mathcal{P}(C)}(\theta\circ\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}(C)})
by the previous lemma and this therefore defines a functor
\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}:\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{coalg}\to\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}
.

Remark 3.19
.

The cobar construction is quasi-free since it is equal to the quotient
\mathcal{P}
-algebra

\mathcal{P}(C)/\im\left({d_{o}}^{2}-\gamma_{\mathcal{P}(C)}(\theta\circ\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}(C)})\right)=\mathcal{P}(C).

3.3.
Convolution algebra

Let
(\mathcal{P},\gamma_{\mathcal{P}},d_{\mathcal{P}},\theta_{\mathcal{P}})
be a curved sa operad and let
(\mathcal{C},d_{\mathcal{C}},\Delta_{\mathcal{C}},\theta_{\mathcal{C}}^{c})
be a curved altipotent cooperad.

Let
C
be a
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra and
A
be a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra. We consider the graded module
\hom(C,A)
of filtered
\mathbf{R}
-linear maps from
C
to
A
.
As for the convolution operad, there is a natural filtration on
\hom(C,A)
given by

F_{p}\hom(C,A)\coloneqq\{\varphi\in\hom(C,A)\textrm{ s.t. }\forall q\geq 0,\ \varphi(F_{q}C)\subset F_{p+q}A\}.

For
f\in\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})(k)
and
\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{k}\in\hom(C,A)
, we define a
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
-algebra structure
\gamma_{h}
by the composite

\gamma_{h}(f;\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{k}):C\xrightarrow{\Delta_{C}}\mathcal{C}(C)\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{C}(k)\hat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{S}_{k}}C^{\hat{\otimes}k}\\
\xrightarrow{\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{k}}f^{\sigma}\otimes\varphi_{\sigma(1)}\otimes\cdots\otimes\varphi_{\sigma(k)}}\mathcal{P}(k)\hat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{S}_{k}}A^{\hat{\otimes}k}\xrightarrow{\gamma_{A}}A,

which is well defined and which is a filtered map (when we consider the filtration
F_{\bullet}
on
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
) since all the involved maps are.
For
\varphi\in\hom(C,A)
, we define the degree
-1
map

\partial(\varphi)\coloneqq d_{A}\cdot\varphi-(-1)^{|\varphi|}\varphi\cdot d_{C}.

For our filtration on
\hom(C,A)
to be compatible with the filtration
\Gamma_{\bullet}
on
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
, we need a second filtration.
We consider on
\hom(C,A)
all the filtrations
G_{\bullet}
compatible with the filtration
\Gamma_{\bullet}
on
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
, with
\gamma_{h}
, and with
\partial
such that

(3.1)

\left\{\begin{aligned} G_{0}\hom(C,A)&=\hom(C,A)\\
G_{1}\hom(C,A)&\supset F_{1}\hom(C,A)+\gamma_{h}\left(\Gamma_{1}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})(\hom(C,A))\right)\\
G_{p}\hom(C,A)&\supset F_{p}\hom(C,A)\text{ for all }p\geq 2.\end{aligned}\right.

The constant filtration given by
G_{p}\hom(C,A)=\hom(C,A)
, for
p\geq 0
, is a filtration satisfying the above condition (
3.1
).
Therefore there exists a smallest such filtration, namely the intersection of all filtrations satisfying (
3.1
).
We call it the
lower central series
of
\hom(C,A)
and we denote it by
\varGamma_{\bullet}
.

For
a:I\to\Gamma_{1}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})(1)
, we fix
\partial_{a}\coloneqq\partial+\gamma_{h}(a;-)
on
\hom(C,A)
.

Lemma 3.20
.

(1)

The gr-dg module
(\hom(C,A),\partial)
is complete for the filtration
\varGamma_{\bullet}
.

(2)

Similarly the gr-dg module
(\hom(C,A),\partial_{a})
is complete for the filtration
\varGamma_{\bullet}
.

Proof.

(1)

The square of
\partial
increases the filtration degree because
\theta_{\mathcal{P}}\in F_{1}\mathcal{P}
and
\theta_{\mathcal{C}}^{c}\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathcal{P}}\in\Gamma_{1}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
.
The fact the filtration is complete follows from the fact that the lower central filtration
\Gamma_{\bullet}
on
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
is complete and that the maps
\Delta_{C}
and
\gamma_{A}
and the applications that we consider in
\hom(C,A)
are filtered.

(2)

We have

\displaystyle{\partial_{a}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\partial_{a}(\partial+\gamma_{h}(a;-))=\partial^{2}+\partial\gamma_{h}(a;-)+\gamma_{h}(a;\partial-)+\gamma_{h}(a;\gamma_{h}(a;-))

\displaystyle=\partial^{2}+\gamma_{h}(\partial a+\frac{1}{2}[a,a];-)

so
\partial_{a}
is also a gr-differential by the definition of
\varGamma_{\bullet}
and the fact that
a\in\Gamma_{1}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})(1)
.

∎

Proposition 3.21
.

(1)

The gr-dg module
(\hom(C,A),\varGamma_{\bullet},\gamma_{h},\partial)
is an algebra over the convolution curved sa operad
(\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P}),\Gamma_{\bullet},\gamma_{H},\partial,\Theta_{H})
.

(2)

Similarly, given a map

a:I\to\Gamma_{1}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})(1)=\mathrm{Hom}(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}},\mathcal{P})(1),

the gr-dg module
(\hom(C,A),\varGamma_{\bullet},\gamma_{h},\partial_{a})
is an algebra over the convolution curved sa operad
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})_{a}=(\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P}),\Gamma_{\bullet},\gamma_{H},\partial_{a},\Theta_{a})
.

Proof.

(1)

The algebra structure
\gamma_{h}
is associative since
\Delta_{C}
is coassociative and
\gamma_{A}
is associative.
The composition
\gamma_{h}
commutes with the predifferentials.
Finally we compute

\displaystyle\partial^{2}(\varphi)

\displaystyle={d_{A}}^{2}\cdot\varphi-\varphi\cdot{d_{C}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{A}\cdot(\theta;\varphi)+(\theta^{c}\otimes\varphi)\cdot\Delta_{C}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{h}(\Theta_{H};\varphi).

This shows the first result.

(2)

Similarly,
\gamma_{h}
commutes with the predifferentials
\partial_{a}
since its commutes with the predifferentials
\partial
and since
\Delta_{C}
is coassociative and
\gamma_{A}
is associative (the proof is similar to the proof given in Lemma
1.2
).
Moreover by the computation made in the proof of Lemma
3.20
, we have on
\hom(C,A)

{\partial_{a}}^{2}=\partial^{2}+\gamma_{h}(\partial a+\frac{1}{2}[a,a];-)=\gamma_{h}(\Theta_{a};-).

∎

Definition 3.22
.

Let
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}
be an operadic curved twisting morphism.

(1)

We denote by
\{l_{n}^{\alpha}\}_{n\geq 0}
the
\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{cL_{\infty}}
-algebra structure on
(\hom(C,A),\partial_{a_{\alpha}})
associated with
\alpha
by means of Propositions
1.28
,
3.21
and
3.9
.
In particular
l_{1}^{\alpha}=\partial_{a_{\alpha}}
, where
a_{\alpha}:I\to\Gamma_{1}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
is given by
a_{\alpha}(1)=\alpha(1):\mathcal{C}(1)\to\mathcal{P}(1)
.
We denote this shifted curved
\mathrm{L}_{\infty}
-algebra by
\hom^{\alpha}(C,A)
.

(2)

An
algebraic twisting morphism with respect to
\alpha
is a map
\varphi:C\rightarrow A
in
\hom^{\alpha}(C,A)
to degree
0
solution of the Maurer–Cartan equation

\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{1}{n!}l_{n}^{\alpha}(\varphi,\ldots,\varphi)=0.

We denote by
\mathrm{Tw}_{\alpha}(C,A)
the set of algebraic twisting morphisms with respect to
\alpha
.

Remark 3.23
.

•

Going through the isomorphisms, we get that the map
l_{k}^{\alpha}
applied to
\varphi_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\varphi_{k}
is given by the composite

C\xrightarrow{\Delta_{C}}\mathcal{C}(C)\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{C}(k)\hat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{S}_{k}}C^{\hat{\otimes}k}\xrightarrow{\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{k}}\alpha(k)^{\sigma}\otimes\varphi_{\sigma(1)}\otimes\cdots\otimes\varphi_{\sigma(k)}}\mathcal{P}(k)\hat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{S}_{k}}A^{\hat{\otimes}k}\xrightarrow{\gamma_{A}}A.

•

The presence of an infinite sum in the Maurer–Cartan equation doesn’t cause any trouble since it corresponds to the application of an infinite sum to different terms of a convergent sum.

As for operadic curved twisting morphisms, we have the following theorem which generalises the bar-cobar adjunction given in
[
GJ94
, Proposition 2.18]
when there are no curvatures.

Theorem 3.24
.

Let
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}
be an operadic curved twisting morphism.
For any coalgebra
C
over the curved altipotent cooperad
\mathcal{C}
and any algebra
A
over the curved sa operad
\mathcal{P}
, there are natural bijections

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}}(\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}C,A)\cong\mathrm{Tw}_{\alpha}(C,A)\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}\text{-}\mathsf{coalg}}(C,\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A).

Proof.

Let us denote by
\mathcal{P}^{g}
the underlying graded operad to the curved sa operad
\mathcal{P}
.
By definition, a morphism of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras is a morphism of the underlying graded
\mathcal{P}^{g}
-algebras commuting with the predifferentials.
Since
\mathcal{P}(C)
is a free
\mathcal{P}^{g}
-algebra, any morphism of
\mathcal{P}^{g}
-algebras
\Phi:\mathcal{P}(C)\to A
is characterised by its restriction
\varphi:C\to A
on
C
.
The morphism
\Phi
commutes with the predifferentials when
\Phi\cdot d_{o}=d_{A}\cdot\Phi
.
As already used implicitly in the proof of Proposition
1.16
, the difference
\Phi\cdot d_{o}-d_{A}\cdot\Phi
is a derivation.
Therefore to prove that it is zero, it is enough to prove that the restriction to
C
is zero.
We have

(\Phi\cdot d_{\alpha}^{l})_{|C}:C\xrightarrow{\Delta_{C}}\mathcal{C}(C)\xrightarrow{\alpha\circ\varphi}\mathcal{P}(A)\xrightarrow{\gamma_{A}}A.

It follows that
\Phi
commutes with the predifferentials if and only if
0=(\Phi\cdot d_{o}-d_{A}\cdot\Phi)_{|C}=\varphi\cdot d_{C}-\gamma\cdot(\alpha\circ\varphi)\cdot\Delta_{C}-d_{A}\cdot\varphi=-\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{1}{n!}l_{n}^{\alpha}(\varphi,\ldots,\varphi)
, that is
\varphi
is an algebraic twisting morphism with respect to
\alpha
.

Similarly, a
\mathcal{C}^{g}
-coalgebra morphism
C\to\mathcal{C}(A)
is characterised by its corestriction
\varphi:C\to A
to
A
and it commutes with the predifferentials if and only if
\varphi
is an algebraic twisting morphism with respect to
\alpha
.
∎

Example 3.25
.

(1)

Through the bar-cobar adjunction, the identity morphism
\operatorname{id}:\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A\to\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A
corresponds to the algebraic twisting morphism
\pi_{\alpha}:\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A\twoheadrightarrow A
.

(2)

Through the bar-cobar adjunction, the identity morphism
\operatorname{id}:\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}C\to\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}C
corresponds to the algebraic twisting morphism
\iota_{\alpha}:C\rightarrowtail\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}C
.

3.4.
Cofibrant resolution of an algebra over a curved operad

In this section, given a Koszul inhomogeneous quadratic curved operad
\mathcal{P}
, we provide a functorial cofibrant resolution
R\to A
of a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
A
. Explicitly,
\mathcal{P}(0)\to R
is a cofibration of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras and

R\to A
is a graded quasi-isomorphism and a strict surjection. This resolution is the counit of the bar-cobar adjunction.

Proposition 3.26
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a curved sa operad,
\mathcal{C}
be a curved altipotent cooperad and
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}
be an operadic curved twisting morphism.
We assume that
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}\circ_{\mathrm{Gr}\alpha}\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{C}\circ_{\mathrm{Gr}\alpha}\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}
(described for example in
[
HM12
, Section 5]
) is cofibrant as a
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}
-right module (in the model category structure described in
[
Fre09
]
) and that
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}\circ_{\mathrm{Gr}\alpha}\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{C}\circ_{\mathrm{Gr}\alpha}\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}\to\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}
is a quasi-isomorphism.

In this situation, the bar-cobar construction
\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A
is a resolution of the
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
A
, that is, the counit of the bar-cobar adjunction

\Phi_{\alpha}:\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A\xrightarrow{\sim}A

is a graded quasi-isomorphism and a strict surjection.

Proof.

The functor
\mathrm{Gr}
is strong monoidal since
\mathbf{K}
is a field of characteristic 0 so
\mathrm{Gr}(\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A)\cong\Omega_{\mathrm{Gr}\alpha}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{Gr}\alpha}\mathrm{Gr}A
as
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}
-algebras (by a careful inspection of the differentials). The map
\mathrm{Gr}\alpha:\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{C}\to\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}
is an operadic twisting morphism between the curved cooperad
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{C}
and the semi-augmented dg operad
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}
.
In the proof of Proposition 5.2.8 in
[
HM12
]
, we use Theorem 15.1.A in
[
Fre09
]
to prove that the bar-cobar construction is a quasi-isomorphism. Assuming that
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}\circ_{\mathrm{Gr}\alpha}\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{C}\circ_{\mathrm{Gr}\alpha}\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}
is cofibrant as a
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}
-right module permits to use
[
Fre09
, Theorem 15.1.A]
more directly and to prove, as in
[
HM12
, Proposition 5.2.8]
, that we have a quasi-isomorphism
\Omega_{\mathrm{Gr}\alpha}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{Gr}\alpha}\mathrm{Gr}A\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Gr}A
.
The morphism
\Phi_{\alpha}
is a strict surjection since it coincides with the projection
\mathcal{P}\hat{\circ}\mathcal{C}(A)\twoheadrightarrow I\circ(A)\cong A
.
∎

We recall that
\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A=(\mathcal{C}(A),d_{b}=d_{\mathcal{C}(A)}+d_{\alpha}^{r})
and that the predifferential on
\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A
is
d_{o}=d_{\mathcal{P}}\circ\operatorname{id}_{\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A}+\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}\circ^{\prime}d_{b}-d_{\alpha}^{l}
.

Proposition 3.27
.

Assume that
\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{K}
.
Let
\mathcal{P}
be a curved sa operad and
\mathcal{C}
be a curved altipotent cooperad. Let
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}
be an operadic curved twisting morphism.
Assume that the filtration
F_{\bullet}
on
\mathcal{C}(A)
comes from a graduation
\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}
(on the module level).
Assume that
\mathcal{C}(A)=\oplus_{l\geq 0}T_{l}
with
T_{0}=\{0\}
and the predifferential
d_{b}
admits a decomposition
d_{b}=\delta_{0}+\delta_{1}
such that for any
l\geq 0
, we have

(1)

\delta_{0}:\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}T_{l}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet+1}(\oplus_{k\geq l+1}T_{k})
and
\im\delta_{0}=\ker\delta_{0}
,

(2)

\delta_{1}-d_{\alpha}^{l}:T_{l}\to\mathcal{P}(\oplus_{k\leq l}T_{k})
with
\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}\delta_{1}:\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}T_{l}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}\mathcal{P}(\oplus_{k<l}T_{k})
.

Then the map
\mathcal{P}(0)\to\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A
is a cofibration in the model category structure on
\mathcal{P}
-algebras defined in
[
BMDC20
, Appendix C]
.

Proof.

We define on
\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A
a filtration
(S_{l})_{l\geq 0}
as follows

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcll}S_{0}&\coloneqq&\{0\},\\
S_{1}&\coloneqq&\ker(\mathrm{Gr}({d_{o}}_{|T_{1}}))\oplus d_{b}\ker(\mathrm{Gr}({d_{o}}_{|T_{1}})),&\\
S_{2}&\coloneqq&S_{1}\oplus W_{1}\oplus d_{b}W_{1}&\supset T_{1},\end{array}\right.

where
W_{1}
is a direct complement of
\ker({\mathrm{Gr}d_{o}}_{|T_{1}})
into
T_{1}
(such a direct complement exists since
\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{K}
is a field), so
T_{1}=\ker({\mathrm{Gr}d_{o}}_{|T_{1}})\oplus W_{1}
.
A direct sum appears in the definitions of
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
since
T_{1}\cap\ker\delta_{0}=T_{1}\cap\im{\delta_{0}}_{|T_{0}}=\{0\}
.
We remark that
T_{1}\oplus d_{b}T_{1}\subset S_{2}
.
For
l\geq 2
, we prove by induction that
S_{2(l-1)}\supset T_{0}\oplus\cdots\oplus T_{l-1}
and we fix by induction a direct complement
U_{l}\subset T_{l}
of
S_{2(l-1)}\cap(T_{0}\oplus\cdots\oplus T_{l})
in
T_{0}\oplus\cdots\oplus T_{l}
, which implies

T_{0}\oplus\cdots\oplus T_{l}\subset S_{2(l-1)}\oplus U_{l}.

Let also
W_{l}
be a direct complement of
\ker(\mathrm{Gr}({d_{o}}_{|U_{l}}))
into
U_{l}
, so

U_{l}=\ker(\mathrm{Gr}({d_{o}}_{|U_{l}}))\oplus W_{l}.

Then we define for each
l\geq 2

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcll}S_{2l-1}&\coloneqq&S_{2(l-1)}\oplus\ker({\mathrm{Gr}d_{o}}_{|U_{l}})\oplus d_{b}\ker({\mathrm{Gr}d_{o}}_{|U_{l}}),&\\
S_{2l}&\coloneqq&S_{2l-1}\oplus W_{l}\oplus d_{b}W_{l}&\supset T_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus T_{l}.\end{array}\right.

We remark that
T_{l}+d_{b}T_{l}\subset S_{2l}
.
Direct sums appear in the definitions of
S_{2l-1}
and
S_{2l}
since
U_{l}\cap\ker\delta_{0}=U_{l}\cap\im({\delta_{0}}_{|\oplus_{k\leq l-1}T_{k}})=\{0\}
.
Indeed, let
t\in\oplus_{k\leq l-1}T_{k}
such that
\delta_{0}(t)\in U_{l}
, then
d_{b}(t)=\delta_{0}(t)+\delta_{1}(t)\in S_{2(l-1)}
and, by assumption on
\delta_{1}
,
\delta_{1}(t)\in\oplus_{k\leq l-1}T_{k}\subset S_{2(l-1)}
. It follows that
\delta_{0}(t)\in S_{2(l-1)}\cap U_{l}=\{0\}
by definition of
U_{l}
.

We have thus constructed an exhaustive increasing filtration
(S_{l})_{l}
on
\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A
of free
\mathbf{K}
-modules such that
S_{l-1}\hookrightarrow S_{l}
are split monomorphisms of (complete) modules with cokernels isomorphic to a sum of (complete) modules

S_{l}/S_{l-1}\cong\oplus_{\alpha}\left(\xi^{\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{K}\oplus\zeta^{\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{K}\right)

where
\xi^{\alpha}
is in some homological degree
n_{\alpha}+1
and some filtration degree
q_{\alpha}
and
\zeta^{\alpha}
is in homological degree
n_{\alpha}
and filtration degree
q_{\alpha}+1
(because (1) and (2)).
The predifferential
d_{o}
is such that
d_{o}(\xi^{\alpha})+\zeta^{\alpha}\in\left(\mathcal{P}(S_{l-1}),\,d_{o}\right)
because
\delta_{0}
increases the filtration degree exactly by 1 and because of condition (
2
) (
\zeta^{\alpha}
is defined to be the sum of
\delta_{0}\xi^{\alpha}
with the filtration degree
q_{\alpha}+1
part of
(\delta_{1}-d_{\alpha}^{l})(\xi^{\alpha})
), and
d_{o}(\zeta^{\alpha})
is obtained by the fact that
{d_{o}}^{2}(\xi^{\alpha})=\theta\otimes\xi^{\alpha}
.
It follows by
[
BMDC20
, Corollary C.41]
that the map
\mathcal{P}(0)\to\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A
is a cofibration.
∎

Remark 3.28
.

In the previous Proposition, Conditions (1) and (2) and the fact that
\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A
is a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra imply that
\theta_{\mathcal{P}}\in\mathrm{Gr}_{1}\mathcal{P}
.

We now prove three corollaries corresponding to the following situations:

•

\pi:\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{P}
,

•

\iota:\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\to\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}=\mathcal{P}_{\infty}
, for
\mathcal{P}=\mathrm{cAs}
,
\mathrm{cuAs}
,
\mathrm{cLie}
or
\mathrm{cCx}
,

•

\kappa:\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\to\mathcal{P}

for
\mathcal{P}=\mathrm{cAs}
,
\mathrm{cuAs}
,
\mathrm{cLie}
or
\mathrm{cCx}
.

Corollary 3.29
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a curved sa operad such that:

(1)

\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}
is a connected bounded below weight filtered operad,

(2)

there exists
d\geq 0
such that for each
w
,
(\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P})^{(\leq w)}
is concentrated in homological degree greater than or equal to
-dw
.

Then its bar construction
\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}
is a curved altipotent cooperad such that
\mathrm{Gr}\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}\cong\mathrm{B}\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}
is connected bounded below weight filtered (by the weight filtration induced by the one on
\mathcal{P}
).

Let
\pi:\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{P}
be the operadic curved twisting morphism defined in Example
1.27
.
Let
A
be a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra.
We assume that
\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{K}
.
Assume moreover that:

(3)

the filtration
F_{\bullet}
on
\mathcal{P}
and on
A
comes from a graduation
\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}
(on the module level) so that the induced filtration on
\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}(A)
also comes from a graduation,

(4)

\theta_{\mathcal{P}}\in\mathrm{Gr}_{1}\mathcal{P}
.

Then the counit of the bar-cobar adjunction

\Phi_{\pi}:\hat{\Omega}_{\pi}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\pi}A\to A

is a cofibrant resolution, that is an acyclic fibration such that
\mathcal{P}(0)\to\hat{\Omega}_{\pi}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\pi}A
is a cofibration.

Proof.

Using Assumption (1), we obtain by
[
HM12
, Proposition 5.1.5]
that
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}\circ_{\mathrm{Gr}\pi}\mathrm{B}\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}\circ_{\mathrm{Gr}\pi}\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}\to\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}
is a quasi-isomorphism.
Moreover, by Assumption (2), both domain and codomain are cofibrant as
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}
-right modules.
For the domain, this follows from
[
Fre09
, Proposition 14.2.2]
with the filtration on
K\coloneqq\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}\circ_{\mathrm{Gr}\pi}\mathrm{Gr}\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}
(starting at
\lambda\geq 0
) given by

K_{\lambda}\coloneqq\oplus_{m+dw\leq\lambda}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}\circ_{\mathrm{Gr}\pi}\mathrm{Gr}\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P})^{(\leq w)}_{m}.

Indeed, the differentials on
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}
and
\mathrm{B}\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}
decreases the homological degree by 1 and do not increase the weight degree so gives a map
K_{\lambda}\to K_{\lambda-1}
and the differentials
d_{\mathrm{Gr}\alpha}^{l}
and
d_{\mathrm{Gr}\alpha}^{r}
can at most increase the homological degree by
dw-1
when it decreases the weight degree by
w
(for
d_{\mathrm{Gr}\alpha}^{r}
) so it gives a map
K_{\lambda}\to K_{\lambda-1}
.
It follows by Proposition
3.26
that
\Phi_{\alpha}
is a graded quasi-isomorphism and a strict surjection.

Then, assuming (3) and (4), we have to check the hypotheses of Proposition
3.27
.
We define
\tilde{T}_{l+1}\coloneqq\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}^{(l)}(A)
the module built from trees with
l
vertices and

T_{2l-1}\coloneqq\im\left((d_{1}\circ\operatorname{id}_{A}+\operatorname{id}_{\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}}\circ^{\prime}d_{A})_{|\tilde{T}_{l}}\right)\subset\tilde{T}_{l}

Then let
T_{2l}
be a direct complement of
T_{2l-1}
in
\tilde{T}_{l}
.
We therefore have obtained a decomposition of
\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}(A)
as the direct sum
\oplus_{l\geq 0}T_{l}
with
T_{0}=\{0\}
and satisfying the following properties.
We fix
\delta_{0}\coloneqq d_{0}\circ\operatorname{id}_{A}
and
\delta_{1}\coloneqq(d_{1}+d_{2})\circ\operatorname{id}_{A}+\operatorname{id}_{\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}}\circ^{\prime}d_{A}
.
Then
\delta_{0}
satisfies
\delta_{0}:\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}T_{l}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet+1}(\oplus_{k\geq l+1}T_{k})
since it increases the number of vertices in
\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}
(and
\theta_{\mathcal{P}}\in\mathrm{Gr}_{1}\mathcal{P}
).
Moreover, using the homotopy
h:\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}\to\hat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}
defined in Theorem
1.30
, we get that
h\circ\operatorname{id}_{A}
is a homotopy for the differential
d_{0}\circ\operatorname{id}_{A}
.
It follows that
\im\delta_{0}=\ker\delta_{0}
.
We also have
\delta_{1}-d_{\pi}^{l}:T_{l}\to\mathcal{P}(\oplus_{k\leq l}T_{k})
with
\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}\delta_{1}:\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}T_{l}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}\mathcal{P}(\oplus_{k<l}T_{k})
by construction (since
{d_{1}}^{2}
and
{d_{A}}^{2}
increases the filtration degree by 1).
It follows that Proposition
3.27
applies so
\hat{\Omega}_{\pi}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\pi}A
is cofibrant.
∎

To prove the next corollary, we require the following proposition.

Proposition 3.30
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a curved coaugmented conilpotent cooperad which is connected bounded below weight filtered. The operadic curved twisting morphism
\iota:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\Omega\mathcal{C}
provides a quasi-isomorphism

\xi:\Omega\mathcal{C}\circ_{\iota}\mathcal{C}\circ_{\iota}\Omega\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Omega\mathcal{C}.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the proof of
[
HM12
, Proposition 5.1.5]
.
We first remark that the curvature
\theta^{c}:\mathcal{C}\to I
strictly decreases the weight because the weight filtration on
\mathcal{C}
is connected (
\mathcal{C}^{(\leq 0)}=I
).
The weight filtration on
\mathcal{C}
induces a weight filtration on
\Omega\mathcal{C}
given by the total weight. This gives a weight filtration
(\Omega\mathcal{C}\circ_{\iota}\mathcal{C}\circ_{\iota}\Omega\mathcal{C})^{(\leq p)}
on
\Omega\mathcal{C}\circ_{\iota}\mathcal{C}\circ_{\iota}\Omega\mathcal{C}
and a weight filtration
\Omega\mathcal{C}^{(\leq p)}
on
\Omega\mathcal{C}
. These weight filtrations are filtrations of chain complexes since the differentials either preserve or decrease the weight. The filtrations are exhaustive and bounded below and the map
\Omega\mathcal{C}\circ_{\iota}\mathcal{C}\circ_{\iota}\Omega\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\Omega\mathcal{C}
preserves the filtrations. We apply the classical theorem of convergence of spectral sequences (cf. Theorem 5.5.1 of
[
Wei94
]
) to obtain

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}E_{p,q}^{\bullet}\Rightarrow\mathrm{H}_{p+q}(\Omega\mathcal{C}\circ_{\iota}\mathcal{C}\circ_{\iota}\Omega\mathcal{C})\\
{E^{\prime}}_{p,q}^{\bullet}\Rightarrow\mathrm{H}_{p+q}(\Omega\mathcal{C}).\end{array}\right.

Since the differential of
E_{\bullet,\bullet}^{0}
is the weight preserving part of the differential of
\Omega\mathcal{C}\circ_{\iota}\mathcal{C}\circ_{\iota}\Omega\mathcal{C}
, the isomorphism of graded vector spaces
E_{\bullet,\bullet}^{0}\cong\Omega\mathcal{C}^{(\bullet)}\circ_{\iota}\mathcal{C}^{(\bullet)}\circ_{\iota}\Omega\mathcal{C}^{(\bullet)}
, where
\mathcal{C}^{(\bullet)}=\mathcal{C}^{(\leq\bullet)}/\mathcal{C}^{(\leq\bullet-1)}
is a dg coaugmented conilpotent cooperad (since
\theta^{c}
strictly decreases the weight), is an isomorphism of dg modules. Because
\mathcal{C}^{(\bullet)}
is a dg coaugmented conilpotent cooperad, it remains to prove that
E_{\bullet,\bullet}^{0}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Omega\mathcal{C}^{(\bullet)}
for a dg coaugmented conilpotent cooperad
\mathcal{C}^{(\bullet)}
.
This follows from the same proof as Theorem 4.18 in
[
Val07
]
(where the author proves the quasi-isomorphism
\Omega\mathcal{C}\circ_{\iota}\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{\sim}I
) with a slight modification on the homotopy map which has to be symmetrise in order to apply to non reduced cooperad (
\mathcal{C}(0)\neq\{0\}
in general, see the proof of Theorem 3.4.4 in
[
HM12
]
for an explicit description).
As a consequence,
E^{1}_{p,q}=\mathrm{H}_{p+q}(\Omega\mathcal{C}^{(p)})={E^{\prime}}_{p,q}^{1}
.
Then
E_{p,q}^{r}
and
{E^{\prime}}_{p,q}^{r}
coincide for
r\geq 1
and
\xi
induces an isomorphism between
E_{p,q}^{\infty}
and
{E^{\prime}}_{p,q}^{\infty}
. This concludes the proof.
∎

Corollary 3.31
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a curved altipotent cooperad such that:

(1)

\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{C}
is a connected bounded below weight filtered cooperad,

(2)

there exists
d\geq 0
such that for each
w
,
(\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{C})^{(\leq w)}
is concentrated in homological degree greater than or equal to
-dw
.

Then its cobar construction
\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{C}
is a curved sa operad such that
\mathrm{Gr}\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{C}\cong\Omega\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{C}
is connected bounded below weight filtered (by the weight filtration induced by the one on
\mathcal{C}
).

Let
\iota:\mathcal{C}\to\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{C}
be the operadic curved twisting morphism defined in Example
1.27
.
Let
A
be a
\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{C}
-algebra.
Then the counit of the bar-cobar adjunction

\Phi_{\iota}:\hat{\Omega}_{\iota}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A\to A

is a resolution, that is an acyclic fibration.

We assume that
\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{K}
.
Assume moreover that:

(3)

\mathcal{P}
be one of the curved sa operad
\mathrm{cAs}
,
\mathrm{cuAs}
,
\mathrm{cLie}
or
\mathrm{cCx}
, and
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}
is the curved altipotent Koszul dual cooperad, and

(4)

the filtration
F_{\bullet}
on the
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}
-algebra
A
comes from a graduation
\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}
(on the module level) so that the induced filtration on
\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}(A)
also comes from a graduation.

In these situations, the filtration on
\mathcal{P}
comes from a graduation
\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}
,
\theta_{\mathcal{P}}\in\mathrm{Gr}_{1}\mathcal{P}
, and the filtration
F_{\bullet}
on
\mathcal{C}
also comes from a graduation
\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}
(on the module level).
The underlying
\mathbb{S}
-module of
\mathcal{C}
is also weight graded by the number of generators describing the elements in
\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}
.

Then
\hat{\Omega}_{\iota}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A
is cofibrant, that is
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}(0)\to\hat{\Omega}_{\iota}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A
is a cofibration.

Finally, in these situations
\Phi_{\iota}:\hat{\Omega}_{\iota}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A\to A
is a cofibrant resolution of the
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}
-algebra
A
.

Proof.

Because
\mathrm{Gr}\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{C}\cong\Omega\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{C}
and
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{C}
is a curved coaugmented conilpotent cooperad that is connected bounded below weight filtered (Assumption (1)),
\mathrm{Gr}\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{C}\circ_{\mathrm{Gr}\iota}\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{C}\circ_{\mathrm{Gr}\iota}\mathrm{Gr}\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{C}\to\mathrm{Gr}\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{C}
is a quasi-isomorphism by Proposition
3.30
.
Moreover both domain and codomain are cofibrant as
\mathrm{Gr}\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{C}
-right modules (by Assumption (2)).
As in Corollary
3.29
, for the domain, this follows from
[
Fre09
, Proposition 14.2.2]
with the filtration on
K\coloneqq\mathrm{Gr}\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{C}\circ_{\mathrm{Gr}\iota}\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{C}
(starting at
\lambda\geq 0
) given by

K_{\lambda}\coloneqq\oplus_{m+dw\leq\lambda}(\mathrm{Gr}\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{C}\circ_{\mathrm{Gr}\iota}\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{C})^{(\leq w)}_{m}.

It follows by Proposition
3.26
that
\Phi_{\alpha}
is a graded quasi-isomorphism and a strict surjection.
The four examples satisfy the conditions of the corollary so
(1)
applies and
\hat{\Omega}_{\iota}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A\to A
is a resolution.

Then we have to check that under Assumptions (3) and (4), the hypotheses of Proposition
3.27
.
Remark that in the four examples
d_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}}=0
.
We define
\tilde{T}_{l+1}\coloneqq\mathcal{C}^{(l)}(A)
and

T_{2l-1}\coloneqq\im\left((\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{C}}\circ^{\prime}d_{A})_{|\tilde{T}_{l}}\right)\subset\tilde{T}_{l}

Then let
T_{2l}
be a direct complement of
T_{2l-1}
in
\tilde{T}_{l}
.
We therefore have obtained a decomposition of
\mathcal{C}(A)
as the direct sum
\oplus_{l\geq 0}T_{l}
with
T_{0}=\{0\}
.
For the four examples, the partial decomposition product of
\mathcal{C}
, used in the definition of
d_{\iota}^{r}
decomposes as
\Delta_{(1)}=\Delta_{(1)}^{0}+\Delta_{(1)}^{1}
where
\Delta_{(1)}^{0}:\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}\mathcal{C}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}\circ_{(1)}\mathcal{C})
and
\Delta_{(1)}^{1}:\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}\mathcal{C}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet+1}(\mathcal{C}\circ_{(1)}\mathcal{C})
.
This gives a decomposition
d_{\iota}^{r}=d_{\iota}^{r,0}+d_{\iota}^{r,1}
.
We fix
\delta_{0}\coloneqq d_{\iota}^{r,1}
and
\delta_{1}\coloneqq\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{C}}\circ^{\prime}d_{A}+d_{\iota}^{r,0}
.
Then
\delta_{0}
satisfies
\delta_{0}:\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}T_{l}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet+1}(\oplus_{k\geq l+1}T_{k})
since it increases the weight in
\mathcal{C}
by 1.

Let us describe the case
\mathcal{P}=\mathrm{cAs}
and
\mathcal{C}=\mathrm{cAs}^{\text{!`}}
. We denote by
\theta_{A}
the degree
-2
element
\gamma_{A}(\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\raise-3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-6.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-6.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-4.25pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\lx@xy@point}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\raise-6.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-9.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern
