6.0pt\raise-12.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}})\in A
. Let
V\subset A
such that
A=\mathbf{K}\theta_{A}\oplus V
. On
\mathcal{C}(A)
, we define

\displaystyle h\left(\tilde{\mu}_{n}^{c}(\theta_{A},a_{2},\ldots,a_{n})\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mu}_{n-1}^{c}(a_{2},\ldots,a_{n});

\displaystyle h\left(\tilde{\mu}_{n}^{c}(a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{n})\right)

\displaystyle=0\text{ when }a_{1}\in V.

A direct computation shows that this is a homotopy for
\delta_{0}
.
It follows that
\im\delta_{0}=\ker\delta_{0}
.
We also have
\delta_{1}-d_{\iota}^{l}:T_{l}\to\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{C}(\oplus_{k\leq l}T_{k})
with
\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}\delta_{1}:\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}T_{l}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}\mathcal{P}(\oplus_{k<l}T_{k})
by construction (since
{d_{1}}^{2}
and
{d_{A}}^{2}
increases the filtration degree by 1).
It follows by Proposition
3.27
that
\hat{\Omega}_{\iota}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A
is cofibrant.

The cases
\mathcal{P}=\mathrm{cuAs}
,
\mathrm{cLie}
and
\mathrm{cCx}
are similar (for
\mathrm{cLie}
and
\mathrm{cCx}
, we stresses that
\theta_{A}
as homological degree
-1
and that the generators of
\mathcal{C}
are based on the cocommutative corolla
\mu_{n}^{c}
of homological degree 0 which satisfies therefore
\mu_{n}^{c}(\ldots,\theta_{A},\ldots,\theta_{A},\ldots)=0
).
∎

Corollary 3.32
.

We assume that
\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{K}
.
Let
\mathcal{P}
be one of the curved sa operad
\mathrm{cAs}
,
\mathrm{cuAs}
,
\mathrm{cLie}
or
\mathrm{cCx}
,
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}
be the curved altipotent Koszul dual cooperad and
\kappa:\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\to\mathcal{P}
be the associated operadic curved twisting morphism (see Definition
1.33
).

In these situations, the filtration
F_{\bullet}
on
\mathcal{C}
comes from a graduation
\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}
(on the module level) and the underlying
\mathbb{S}
-module of
\mathcal{C}\cong\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}\mathcal{C}
is also weight graded by the number of generators describing the elements in
\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}
.
Let
A
be a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra whose filtration
F_{\bullet}
comes from a graduation
\mathrm{Gr}_{\bullet}
(on the module level) so that the induced filtration on
\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}(A)
also comes from a graduation.

Then the counit of the bar-cobar adjunction

\Phi_{\alpha}:\hat{\Omega}_{\kappa}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\kappa}A\to A

is a cofibrant resolution, that is an acyclic fibration such that
\mathcal{P}(0)\to\hat{\Omega}_{\kappa}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\kappa}A
is a cofibration.

Proof.

The proof follows the same lines as the two previous ones (Corollaries
3.29
and
3.31
).
The fact that
\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}\circ_{\mathrm{Gr}\kappa}\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\circ_{\mathrm{Gr}\kappa}\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}\to\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}
is a quasi-isomorphism is proved in
[
HM12
, Proposition 5.1.6]
.
∎

Remark 3.33
.

The corollaries
3.29
,
3.31
and
3.32
extend to the context of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras in
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}
by adding the assumption that
A
is a cofibrant as an
\mathbf{R}
-module (where
\mathbf{R}
is a
\mathbf{K}
-cdga) because Theorem 15.1.A in
[
Fre09
]
works with this assumption (and because
\mathbf{K}
is a field).

3.5.
\infty
-morphisms between curved algebras

Given a curved
\mathcal{P}
as in Theorem
1.35
, we obtain an
\mathbb{S}
-cofibrant resolution
\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\to\mathcal{P}
and we note
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}\coloneqq\hat{\Omega}\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}
.
Let
A
and
B
be two
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}
-algebras such that
\hat{\Omega}_{\iota}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A\to A
is a graded quasi-isomorphism, then we can compare
A
and
B
in the homotopy category of
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}
-algebras (instead of the category of
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}
-algebras) by means of the zig-zag

A\xleftarrow{\sim}\hat{\Omega}_{\iota}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A\to B.

By the adjunction presented in Theorem
3.24
, the map
\hat{\Omega}_{\iota}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A\to B
is characterised by a
\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}
-coalgebra morphism
\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A\to\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}B
.

We therefore define the notion of
\infty
-morphism as follows.

Definition 3.34
.

Let
A
and
B
be two
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}
-algebras. An
\infty
-morphism
A\rightsquigarrow B
is a
\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}
-coalgebra map

F:\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A\to\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}B.

By Theorem
3.24
, this is equivalent to an algebraic twisting morphism
\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A\to B
with respect to
\iota
.
We denote by
\infty\text{-}\mathrm{mor}(A,B)
the set of
\infty
-morphisms from
A
to
B
.

4.
Cohomology of curved algebras

André–Quillen cohomology is a cohomology theory first developed for commutative algebras
[
And74
,
Qui70
]
and then extended to algebras over an operad
[
GH00
,
Mil11
]
.
In this section, we developed the theory for algebras over a curved operad.
We make some general computations that we use in the next section to compute the André–Quillen cohomology in the context of
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
-algebras.

4.1.
André–Quillen cohomology

We adapt the definitions given in
[
GH00
]
and
[
Mil11
]
to the curved setting.

Let
(\mathcal{P},\gamma_{\mathcal{P}},\eta_{\mathcal{P}},\varepsilon_{\mathcal{P}},d_{\mathcal{P}},\theta_{\mathcal{P}})
be a semi-augmented curved operad.
Let
(A,\gamma_{A},\eta_{A},d_{A})
be a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra (whose underlying gr-dg module is a
\mathbf{R}
-module).

4.1.1.
Module over a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra

Let
(M,d_{M})
be a gr-dg
\mathbf{R}
-module.
We define
\mathcal{P}(A,M)
to be the submodule of
\mathcal{P}(A\oplus M)
made of terms linear in
M
, that is

\mathcal{P}(A,M)\coloneqq\bigoplus_{n}\mathcal{P}(n)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\left(A\hat{\otimes}\cdots\hat{\otimes}\underbrace{M}_{j\text{th position}}\hat{\otimes}\cdots\hat{\otimes}A\right).

(It is also well-defined when
\mathcal{P}
is a gr-dg
\mathbb{S}
-module without a curved operad structure.)

Definition 4.1
.

An
A
-module

(M,\gamma_{M},\iota_{M},d_{M})
, or
A
-module over
\mathcal{P}
, is a gr-dg
\mathbf{R}
-module
(M,d_{M})
endowed with two maps

\gamma_{M}:\mathcal{P}(A,M)\to M\text{ and }\iota_{M}:M\to\mathcal{P}(A,M)

such that the following diagrams of associativity and unitarity commute

\textstyle{(\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P})(A,M)\cong\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{P}A,\mathcal{P}(A,M))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 54.0pt\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}(\gamma_{A},\gamma_{M})}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{\mathcal{P}}(\operatorname{id}_{A},\operatorname{id}_{M})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}(A,M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{M}}
and
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}(A,M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 14.0pt\gamma_{M}}
\textstyle{M}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-12.0pt\iota_{M}}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}(A,M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{M}}
\textstyle{M.}

We moreover require the following equation to hold:
{d_{M}}^{2}=\gamma_{M}(\theta_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{M})
.

A
morphism of
A
-modules

f:(M,\gamma_{M},\iota_{M},d_{M})\to(N,\gamma_{N},\iota_{N},d_{N})
is an
\mathbf{R}
-modules map
f:(M,d_{M})\to(N,d_{N})
such that the following diagrams commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}(A,M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}(A,N)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}(\operatorname{id}_{A},f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{N}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}(A,M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}(A,N).}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}(\operatorname{id}_{A},f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{N}}

We denote by
\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P}}
(resp. by
\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P},0}
) the category of
A
-modules over the operad
\mathcal{P}
in which morphisms are morphisms of
A
-modules (resp. in which morphisms are degree 0 gr-dg morphisms of
A
-modules).

Example 4.2
.

Let
\mathbf{K}=\mathbf{R}=\mathbb{R}
,
\mathcal{P}=\mathrm{cCx}
and
A
be a
\mathrm{cCx}
-algebra. Then
A
is a Lie algebra (with a bracket of cohomological degree
-1
) and a
\mathbb{C}
-module such that the Lie bracket is
\mathbb{C}
-linear. An
A
-module is a
\mathbb{C}
-module endowed with a
\mathbb{C}
-linear action of the Lie algebra
A
.

Let’s now construct what will be the free
A
-module on a gr-dg
\mathbf{R}
-module
(N,d_{N})
. We define the coequalizer in
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}

A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}N\coloneqq\textrm{coeq}\left(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 28.11807pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-28.11807pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{P}(A),N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 36.03125pt\raise 8.76389pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.61111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\gamma_{\mathcal{P}}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 78.11807pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 36.30904pt\raise-7.99165pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.83888pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}(\gamma_{A},\operatorname{id}_{N})}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 78.11807pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 78.11807pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{P}(A,N)}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\right),

where

\overline{\mathcal{P}(A,N)}\coloneqq\mathcal{P}(A,N)/\left(\mathcal{P}(A,N)\cap\left(\im\left(\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes({d_{A\oplus N}}^{2})-\theta_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{A\oplus N}\right)\right)\right)

and

\widetilde{\gamma_{\mathcal{P}}}:\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{P}(A),N)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{P}A,\mathcal{P}(A,N))\xrightarrow{\gamma_{\mathcal{P}}(\operatorname{id}_{A},\operatorname{id}_{N})}\mathcal{P}(A,N)\twoheadrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{P}(A,N)}.

With respect to the dg situation, the quotient
\overline{\mathcal{P}(A,N)}
appears to get the equation
{d_{M}}^{2}=\gamma_{M}(\theta_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{M})
that an
A
-module
M=A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}N
have to satisfy.
The predifferentials
d_{A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}N}
is induced by the predifferentials on
\mathcal{P}
,
A
and
N
.

As in the classical case (see for example Lemma 12.3.3 in
[
LV12
]
), the
A
-module structure is obtained from the map

\mathcal{P}(A,\mathcal{P}(A,N))\rightarrowtail\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{P}A,\mathcal{P}(A,N))\xrightarrow{\gamma_{\mathcal{P}}(\operatorname{id}_{A},\operatorname{id}_{N})}\mathcal{P}(A,N)

and the map

\mathcal{P}(A,N)\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathcal{P}(A,I\circ N)\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}(\operatorname{id}_{A},\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\circ\operatorname{id}_{N})}\mathcal{P}(A,\mathcal{P}(A,N))

which pass to the quotient
\mathcal{P}(A,N)\twoheadrightarrow A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}N
(by associativity of
\gamma_{\mathcal{P}}
and
\gamma_{A}
, and by the fact that
d_{\mathcal{P}}
and
d_{A}
are derivations with respect to the composition structures).

We denote by
(\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}})^{0}
the category of gr-dg modules with morphisms the degree 0 gr-dg morphisms.

Proposition 4.3
.

The forgetful functor
U:\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P},0}\to(\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}})^{0}
has a left adjoint, denoted by

(N,d_{N})\mapsto(A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}N,d_{A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}N}).

That is, we have an isomorphism of
\mathbf{R}
-modules

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P},0}}\left(A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}N,M\right)\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{(\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}})^{0}}\left(N,UM\right),

for all
N\in(\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}})^{0}
and
M\in\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P},0}
.

Proof.

To any morphism
f:A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}N\to M
, we associate its restriction

N\cong I\circ N\xrightarrow{\eta_{\mathcal{P}}(\operatorname{id}_{A},\operatorname{id}_{N})}\mathcal{P}(A,N)\twoheadrightarrow A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}N\xrightarrow{f}M.

In the other direction, given
g:N\to U(M)
, we define the map

\mathcal{P}(A,N)\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}(\operatorname{id}_{A},g)}\mathcal{P}(A,M)\twoheadrightarrow A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}M\to M.

This map factors through the quotient map
\mathcal{P}(A,N)\twoheadrightarrow A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}N
(because
g
commutes with
d_{N}
) to give the wanted map of
A
-modules.

The two processes are natural linear isomorphisms between the spaces of modules maps. Provided
f
(resp.
g
) commutes with the predifferential, the same is true for the restriction (resp. extension) since
\gamma_{\mathcal{P}}
,
\gamma_{A}
and
\gamma_{M}
commute with the predifferentials.
∎

We also define a quasi-free
A
-module.
Let
N
be an
\mathbf{R}
-module and
\tau:N\to\mathcal{P}(A,N)
be a degree
-1
map which induces a gr-differential still denoted
\tau
on
\mathcal{P}(A,N)
.
Similarly as above, we define the coequalizer in
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}

A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\tau}N\coloneqq\textrm{coeq}\left(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 28.11807pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-28.11807pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{P}(A),N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.07822pt\raise 8.76389pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.61111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\gamma_{\mathcal{P}}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 78.11807pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.356pt\raise-7.99165pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.83888pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}(\gamma_{A},\operatorname{id}_{N})}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 78.11807pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 78.11807pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{P}(A,N)}^{\tau}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\right),

where

\overline{\mathcal{P}(A,N)}^{\tau}\coloneqq\mathcal{P}(A,N)/\left(\mathcal{P}(A,N)\cap\left(\im\left(\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes(d_{A}+\tau)^{2}-\theta_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{A\oplus N}\right)\right)\right).

It is endowed with the gr-differential
d_{A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\tau}N}
depending on
d_{A}
,
d_{\mathcal{P}}
and
\tau
.

Proposition 4.4
.

For all
N\in\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}
and
M\in\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P}}
, we have an isomorphism of
\mathbf{R}
-modules

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P},0}}\left(A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\tau}N,M\right)\cong\ker\left(\partial_{\tau}:\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}}^{0}\left(N,UM\right)\to\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}}^{1}\left(N,UM\right)\right),

where
\partial_{\tau}(f)\coloneqq d_{M}\cdot f-(-1)^{|f|}\gamma_{M}\cdot(\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}(\operatorname{id}_{A},f))\cdot\tau
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the proof of Proposition
4.3
. The only differences come from the gr-differential
\tau
.
∎

Given two
A
-modules over a curved operad
\mathcal{P}
,
(N,d_{N},\gamma_{N})
and
(M,d_{M},\gamma_{M})
, the graded space of maps
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P}}}(M,N)
is naturally endowed with a predifferential
\partial
defined by
\partial(f)\coloneqq d_{N}\cdot f-(-1)^{|f|}f\cdot d_{M}
.
Gr-dg morphisms coincide with maps in the kernel of
\partial
.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
(N,d_{N},\gamma_{N})
and
(M,d_{M},\gamma_{M})
be two
A
-modules over a curved operad
\mathcal{P}
.
The predifferential
\partial
on the graded
\mathbf{R}
-module
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P}}}(N,M)
is in fact a differential, that is
\partial^{2}=0
.

Proof.

Since
N
and
M
are
A
-modules, we have for any
f:N\to M
of
A
-modules

\partial^{2}(f)={d_{N}}^{2}\cdot f-f\cdot{d_{M}}^{2}=\gamma_{N}(\theta_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes f)-f\cdot\gamma_{M}(\theta_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{M})=0.

∎

Remark 4.6
.

This lemma is not true when
(N,d_{N})
and
(M,d_{M})
are only assumed to be gr-dg
\mathbf{R}
-modules.

Lemma 4.7
.

The forgetful functor
\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P},0}\to(\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}})^{0}
creates all limits and colimits, and
\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P},0}
is an additive subcategory of
(\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}})^{0}
.

Proof.

Because the direct sum and the tensor product preserve colimits in
(\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}})^{0}
, it follows that
M\mapsto\mathcal{P}(A,M)
preserves colimits in
(\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}})^{0}
.
If
M:I\to\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P},0}
is any diagram, the colimit in
(\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}})^{0}
,
\colim_{I}M
, acquires a map

\mathcal{P}(A,\colim_{I}M)\cong\colim_{I}\mathcal{P}(A,M)\to\colim_{I}F_{\mathcal{P}}(A,M)\to\colim_{I}M.

Because
{d_{A}}^{2}=\gamma_{A}(\theta_{\mathcal{P}},-)
and
{d_{M(i)}}^{2}=\gamma_{M(i)}(\theta_{\mathcal{P}},-)
for all
i\in I
, the same equation is satisfied by
\colim_{I}M
.
This endows
\colim_{I}M
with an
A
-module structure over
\mathcal{P}
. With this structure,
\colim_{I}M
becomes the colimit in
\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P},0}
.
The case of limits can be treated in a similar way since the limit in
(\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}})^{0}
is a sub-
\mathbf{R}
-module of the direct product of the modules involved and
M\mapsto\mathcal{P}(A,M)
preserves products.
It follows that the forgetful functor creates all limits and colimits.

Then, the category
\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P},0}
is additive since the direct sum of two objects is made in
(\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}})^{0}
.
∎

Lemma 4.8
.

The category
\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P},0}
is isomorphic to the category of algebras over the monad
U(A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}-)
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
4.7
and from Beck’s Theorem (see
[
Mac98
, VI, §7]
).
∎

Let
A
be a curved associative algebra. By making explicit the notion of
A
-modules over
\mathrm{cAs}
, we get that it coincides, for an
A
-module
M
, with the following data:

\gamma_{l}:A\hat{\otimes}M\to M\text{ and }\gamma_{r}:M\hat{\otimes}A\to M,

satisfying the usual associativity relations and such that

{d_{M}}^{2}=\gamma_{l}(\theta_{A}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{M})-\gamma_{r}(\operatorname{id}_{M}\otimes\theta_{A})=[\theta_{A},-].

It is natural to extract from this definition the notions left-
A
-module and right-
A
-module. The gr-dg module
M
is a left-
A
-module if there exists a map
\gamma_{l}:A\hat{\otimes}M\to M
satisfying the usual associativity relation such that
{d_{M}}^{2}=\gamma_{l}(\theta_{A}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{M})=\theta_{A}\cdot-
.
The notion of right-
A
-module is similar but a minus sign appears in the relation that
d_{M}
satisfies:
{d_{M}}^{2}=-(-\cdot\theta_{A})
.
These notions already appear in the literature, see for example
[
PP05
, Section 4 of Chapter 5]
.

We now define the enveloping algebra of a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
A
. It is a curved associative algebra.

The coequaliser
U_{\mathcal{P}}(A)\coloneqq\textrm{coeq}\left(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 27.03474pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-27.03474pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{P}(A),\mathbf{R})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.61111pt\raise 8.76389pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.61111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\gamma_{\mathcal{P}}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 77.03474pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.00386pt\raise-8.65277pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}(\gamma_{A},\operatorname{id}_{\mathbf{R}})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 77.03474pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 77.03474pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{P}(A,\mathbf{R})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\right)
is endowed with a filtration coming from the one on
\mathcal{P}
and
A
, and a multiplication coming from the map

\mathcal{P}(A,\mathbf{R})\otimes\mathcal{P}(A,\mathbf{R})\cong\mathcal{P}(A,\mathcal{P}(A,\mathbf{R}))\to\mathcal{P}(A,\mathbf{R}).

This multiplication is associative since
\gamma_{\mathcal{P}}
and
\gamma_{A}
are. It has a unit coming from the map
\mathbf{R}\to\mathcal{P}(A,\mathbf{R})
and a predifferential
d_{U_{\mathcal{P}}(A)}
induced by the predifferentials
d_{\mathcal{P}}
and
d_{A}
and the differential
d_{\mathbf{R}}
.
The element
\theta_{U_{\mathcal{P}}(A)}\coloneqq\overline{\theta_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes 1_{\mathbf{R}}}\in U_{\mathcal{P}}(A)
has homological degree
-2
and filtration degree 1 (as
\theta_{\mathcal{P}}
) and we have
{d_{U_{\mathcal{P}}(A)}}^{2}=[\theta_{U_{\mathcal{P}}(A)},-]
because
\mathcal{P}
and
A
are curved.

Definition 4.9
.

Let
A
be a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra. The
enveloping algebra
of the
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
A
is the curved associative algebra

U_{\mathcal{P}}(A)\coloneqq\left(\textrm{coeq}\left(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 27.03474pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-27.03474pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{P}(A),\mathbf{R})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.61111pt\raise 8.76389pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.61111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\gamma_{\mathcal{P}}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 77.03474pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.00386pt\raise-8.65277pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}(\gamma_{A},\operatorname{id}_{\mathbf{R}})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 77.03474pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 77.03474pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{P}(A,\mathbf{R})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\right),d_{U_{\mathcal{P}}(A)},\theta_{U_{\mathcal{P}}(A)}\right).

Proposition 4.10
.

The category of
A
-modules over
\mathcal{P}
is isomorphic to the category of left-modules over the (curved associative) enveloping algebra
U_{\mathcal{P}}(A)
.

Proof.

We have

U_{\mathcal{P}}(A)\hat{\otimes}M\cong\textrm{coeq}\left(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 28.95142pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-28.95142pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{P}(A),M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.52779pt\raise 8.76389pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.61111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\gamma_{\mathcal{P}}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 78.95142pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.19475pt\raise-8.65277pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}(\gamma_{A},\operatorname{id}_{M})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 78.95142pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 78.95142pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{P}(A,M)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\right)\eqqcolon\textrm{coeq}

therefore a left-
U_{\mathcal{P}}(A)
-module structure
\gamma_{l}
gives a map
\gamma_{M}:\mathcal{P}(A,M)\twoheadrightarrow\textrm{coeq}\xrightarrow{\gamma_{l}}M
which endows
M
with a structure of
A
-module over
\mathcal{P}
(the associativity diagram is satisfied by the fact that the map factors through
coeq
).
The equality
{d_{M}}^{2}=\gamma_{l}(\theta_{U_{\mathcal{P}}(A)},-)
implies the equality
{d_{M}}^{2}=\gamma_{M}(\theta_{\mathcal{P}},-)
.

Conversely, a structure
\gamma_{M}
of
A
-module over
\mathcal{P}
is precisely a map which factors through
coeq
to give a map
\gamma_{l}
, and which satisfies
{d_{M}}^{2}=\gamma_{l}(\theta_{U_{\mathcal{P}}(A)},-)
(because
{d_{M}}^{2}=\gamma_{M}(\theta_{\mathcal{P}},-)
and associativity of
\gamma_{A}
).
This endows
M
with a left-
U_{\mathcal{P}}(A)
-module structure.
∎

Remark 4.11
.

Compared with the situation when there is no curvature, we emphasise that in the curved setting the free
A
-module on
\mathbf{R}
is a quotient of the enveloping algebra
U_{\mathcal{P}}(A)
.

Proposition 4.12
.

For any curved operad
\mathcal{P}
, the enveloping algebra construction provides a functor

U_{\mathcal{P}}:\mathcal{P}\textsf{-Alg}\to\mathrm{cuAs}\textsf{-Alg},

where
\mathrm{cuAs}
is the operad encoding curved unital associative algebras.

Proof.

Let
B\to A
be a morphism of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras. The map
U_{\mathcal{P}}(f):U_{\mathcal{P}}(B)\to U_{\mathcal{P}}(A)
induced by
\mathcal{P}(B,\mathbf{R})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}(f,\operatorname{id}_{\mathbf{R}})}\mathcal{P}(A,\mathbf{R})
is a map of unital associative algebras.
This map commutes with the predifferentials and preserves the curvature by construction.
∎

Let
f:B\to A
be a morphism of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras and
(N,\gamma_{N})\in\mathsf{Mod}_{B}^{\mathcal{P}}
.
The composite

\mathcal{P}(A,\mathcal{P}(B,N))\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}(\operatorname{id}_{A},\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}(f,\operatorname{id}_{N}))}\mathcal{P}(A,\mathcal{P}(A,N))\rightarrowtail(\mathcal{P}\hat{\circ}\mathcal{P})(A,N)\\
\xrightarrow{\gamma_{\mathcal{P}}(\operatorname{id}_{A},\operatorname{id}_{N})}\mathcal{P}(A,N)\twoheadrightarrow A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}N

factors through the quotient
A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}(B\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}N)
to produce a map
\rho_{l}:A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}(B\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}N)\to A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}N
.
Similarly, the composite

\mathcal{P}(A,\mathcal{P}(B,N))\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}(\operatorname{id}_{A},\gamma_{N})}\mathcal{P}(A,N)\twoheadrightarrow A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}N,

induces a second map
\rho_{r}:A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}(B\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}N)\to A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}N
.
Using these two maps, we define the
A
-module
A\hat{\otimes}_{B}^{\mathcal{P}}N
as the following coequalizer

A\hat{\otimes}_{B}^{\mathcal{P}}N\coloneqq\textrm{coeq}\left(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 30.67538pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-30.67538pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}(B\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.22903pt\raise 7.34027pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\rho_{l}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.67538pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.66478pt\raise-7.34026pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\rho_{r}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.67538pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 54.67538pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}N}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\right).

Proposition 4.13
.

Let
f:B\to A
be a morphism of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras. The functor of restriction of scalars

f^{*}:\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\to\mathsf{Mod}_{B}^{\mathcal{P}}

admits a left adjoint

f_{!}\coloneqq A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}_{B}-:\mathsf{Mod}_{B}^{\mathcal{P}}\to\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P}}.

We mean that we have natural isomorphisms of gr-dg
\mathbf{R}
-modules

\left(\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P}}}\left(f_{!}(N),M\right),\partial\right)\cong\left(\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{B}^{\mathcal{P}}}\left(N,f^{*}(M)\right),\partial\right),

for any
A
-module
M
and any
B
-module
N
.

The same functors restrict to the kernels of
\partial
to provide isomorphisms of
\mathbf{R}
-modules

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P},0}}\left(f_{!}(N),M\right)\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{B}^{\mathcal{P},0}}\left(N,f^{*}(M)\right).

Moreover, for any gr-dg
\mathbf{R}
-module
L
and map
\tau:L\to\mathcal{P}(B,L)
, we have the isomorphism of gr-dg
\mathbf{R}
-modules

A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}_{B}(B\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\tau}L)\cong A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\tau^{\prime}}L,

where
\tau^{\prime}
is the composite
L\xrightarrow{\tau}\mathcal{P}(B,L)\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}(f,\operatorname{id}_{L})}\mathcal{P}(A,L)
.

Proof.

The proof is analog to the proof of Proposition
4.3
.
To any morphism
h:A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}_{B}N\to M
, we associate its restriction

N\to A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}N\twoheadrightarrow A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}_{B}N\xrightarrow{h}M.

In the other direction, given
g:N\to f^{*}(M)
, we define the map

A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}N\xrightarrow{g}A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}M\to M.

This maps factors through the quotient map
A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}N\twoheadrightarrow A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}_{B}N
since
g
is a morphism of
B
-modules to give the wanted map of
A
-modules.

The two processes are natural (graded) linear isomorphisms between the spaces of (graded) modules maps. Moreover, they commute with the differentials
\partial
(see Lemma
4.5
for the fact that they are differentials) since we consider morphisms compatible with the structures of modules over
\mathcal{P}
(and
\gamma_{\mathcal{P}}
,
\gamma_{A}
and
\gamma_{M}
commute with the predifferentials).
Finally, when
h
(resp.
g
) commutes with the predifferentials, the same is true for the restriction (resp. the extension).

The last isomorphism follows from the adjunctions just shown and from Proposition
4.3
by the Yoneda Lemma.
∎

4.1.2.
Quasi-free
\mathcal{P}
-algebra

Let
(M,d_{M})
be a gr-dg
\mathbf{R}
-module.
We recall from
[
BMDC20
, Proposition C.35]
that the free
\mathcal{P}
-algebra on a gr-dg module
M
is given by

F_{\mathcal{P}}(M)=\mathcal{P}(M)/\left(\im\left(\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes({d_{M}}^{2})-\theta_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{M}\right)\right)

where
\left(\im\left(\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes({d_{M}}^{2})-\theta\otimes\operatorname{id}_{M}\right)\right)
is the ideal of
\mathcal{P}(M)
generated by the image of
\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes({d_{M}}^{2})-\theta\otimes\operatorname{id}_{M}
.

The bar-cobar resolutions are not free
\mathcal{P}
-algebras since the predifferential in theses cases isn’t internal to the generators.
We define the notion of quasi-free
\mathcal{P}
-algebras in order to include these examples.

Definition 4.14
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a curved operad.
We say that a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
S
is
quasi-free
when there exists a complete filtered
\mathbf{R}
-module
V
and a predifferential
d:\mathcal{P}(V)\to\mathcal{P}(V)
such that

S\cong\left(\mathcal{P}(V),\bar{d}\right)/\left(\im\left(d^{2}-\theta_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{V}\right)_{|V}\right),

where
\bar{d}
is induced by
d
.

We remark that even if we do not assume that
d
is a gr-differential in the above definition
, a quasi-free
\mathcal{P}
-algebra is nevertheless a gr-dg
\mathbf{R}
-module.
The notion of quasi-free
\mathcal{P}
-algebra is different from the notion of free
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
since the predifferential
d
isn’t a priori induced by a map
V\to V
.

4.1.3.
Derivation and Kähler differential forms

We call a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra over
A
a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
B
endowed with a map of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras
B\xrightarrow{f}A
.

Definition 4.15
.

Let
B
be a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra over
A
and
M
be an
A
-module. An
A
-derivation from
B
to
M
is a linear map
d:B\to M
such that the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{\mathcal{P}(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-28.45274pt\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}\circ^{\prime}d}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}(B,M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}\circ(f,\operatorname{id}_{M})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}(A,M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{M}}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{M,}

where the infinitesimal composite of morphisms
\circ^{\prime}
is defined in
[
LV12
, Section 6.1.3]
. We denote by
\mathrm{Der}_{A}(B,M)
(resp. by
\mathrm{Der}_{A}^{0}(B,M)
) the set of
A
-derivations from
B
to
M
(resp. which moreover have degree 0 and commute with the predifferentials
d_{B}
and
d_{M}
).

Lemma 4.16
.

Let
B
be a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra over
A
and
M
be an
A
-module.
The predifferential
\partial
on the graded
\mathbf{R}
-module
\mathrm{Der}_{A}(B,M)
, defined by

\partial(D)=d_{M}\cdot D-(-1)^{|D|}D\cdot d_{B}

for
D\in\mathrm{Der}_{A}(B,M)
, is in fact a differential, that is
\partial^{2}=0
.

Proof.

We compute

\displaystyle\partial^{2}(D)

\displaystyle={d_{M}}^{2}\cdot D-D\cdot{d_{B}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{M}(\theta_{\mathcal{P}},D(-))-D\cdot\gamma_{B}(\theta_{\mathcal{P}},-)=0

since
D
is an
A
-derivation from
B
to
M
.
∎

When
\mathcal{M}=\{\mathcal{M}(0),\mathcal{M}(1),\ldots\}
is a gr-dg
\mathbb{S}
-module, we denote by
\mathcal{M}_{>0}
the gr-dg
\mathbb{S}
-module
\{\{0\},\mathcal{M}(1),\ldots\}
.

Proposition 4.17
.

The category of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras admits finite products.

(1)

The terminal object is given by
(\{0\},\gamma=0,d=0)
.

(2)

The product of two
\mathcal{P}
-algebras
(A,\gamma_{A})
and
(B,\gamma_{B})
is given by
A\wedge B\coloneqq(A\oplus B,\gamma)
where
\gamma
is defined by the composite

\mathcal{P}(A\wedge B)\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{P}(0)\oplus\mathcal{P}_{>0}(A)\oplus\mathcal{P}_{>0}(B)\xrightarrow{(\gamma_{A}+\gamma_{B})+\gamma_{A}+\gamma_{B}}A\oplus B.

The maps from
A\wedge B
to
A
and
B
are the projections.

Similarly, given a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
A
, the category
\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}/A
of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras over
A
admits finite products.

(1)

The terminal object is the
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
A
.

(2)

The product of two
\mathcal{P}
-algebras
(B,\gamma_{B})
and
(B^{\prime},\gamma_{B}^{\prime})
over
A
is given by
B\wedge_{A}B^{\prime}\coloneqq(B\oplus_{A}B^{\prime},\gamma)
where
\gamma
is defined by the composite

\mathcal{P}(B\wedge_{A}B^{\prime})\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{P}(0)\oplus\mathcal{P}_{>0}(B)\oplus_{\mathcal{P}_{>0}(A)}\mathcal{P}_{>0}(B^{\prime})\xrightarrow{(\gamma_{B}+_{A}\gamma_{B^{\prime}})+\gamma_{B}+\gamma_{B^{\prime}}}B\oplus_{A}B^{\prime}.

Proof.

We first prove the universal property satisfied by
A\wedge B
. Let
f_{A}:C\to A
and
f_{B}:C\to B
be two
\mathcal{P}
-algebra maps. Using the fact that
A\oplus B
is the product in modules, we get that the requested map
C\to A\wedge B
is necessarily
f_{A}+f_{B}
. It is then a direct check to verify that
f_{A}+f_{B}
is a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra map.

We can show similarly that
B\wedge_{A}B^{\prime}
satisfies the universal property of the product.
∎

Definition 4.18
.

Let
A
be a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra and let
M
be an
A
-module. The
abelian extension of
A
by
M
, denoted by
A\ltimes M
, is the
\mathcal{P}
-algebra over
A
whose underlying
\mathbf{R}
-module is
A\oplus M
and whose
\mathcal{P}
-algebra structure is given by

\mathcal{P}(A\oplus M)\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{P}(A)\oplus\mathcal{P}(A,M)\xrightarrow{\gamma_{A}+\gamma_{M}}A\oplus M.

The morphism
A\ltimes M
is the projection on the first summand.

Proposition 4.19
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a curved operad and
A
be a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra. There are
\mathbf{R}
-linear isomorphisms

\mathrm{Der}_{A}^{0}(B,M)\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}/A}\left(B,A\ltimes M\right),

for any
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
B
over
A
and any
A
-module
M
.

Proof.

Let
f:B\to A
be the structural morphism of
B\in\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}/A
. Any morphism
B\to A\ltimes M
in
\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}/A
is of the form

\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f+d}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{A\ltimes M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A,}

where
d:B\to M
.
Then the map
f+d
is a morphism of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras if and only if
d
is an
A
-derivation.
∎

We denote by
\mathsf{Ab(\mathcal{P}\text{-}alg}/A)
the category of abelian group objects in the category
(\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}/A,\wedge_{A})
of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras over
A
.

Proposition 4.20
.

Let
A
be a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra. There is a canonical equivalence between the category of
A
-modules and the category of abelian group objects in
\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}/A

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathsf{Ab(\mathcal{P}\text{-}alg}/A)&\simeq&\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P},0},\\
(B\xrightarrow{f}A)&\mapsto&\ker(f).\end{array}\right.

Proof.

As we will see, the standard proof works in the setting of curved operads.
Let
B\xrightarrow{f}A
be an abelian group object in
\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}/A
, that is there is a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra map
\eta:A\to B
such that
f\cdot\eta=\operatorname{id}_{A}
and an abelian multiplication
m:B\wedge_{A}B\to B
.
We define on
\ker(f)
an
A
-module structure
\gamma_{\ker}
by the composite

\mathcal{P}(A,\ker(f))\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}\circ(f,\mathrm{incl})}\mathcal{P}(B,B)\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{P}(B)\xrightarrow{\gamma_{B}}B.

Using the fact that
f
is a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra map, we obtain
\im(\gamma_{\ker})\subset\ker(f)
. Moreover, since
B
is a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra and
\eta
is a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra map, we get the commutativity of the associativity and unitarity diagrams.
For
b\in\ker(f)
, we also have
{d_{\ker(f)}}^{2}(b)=\gamma_{B}(\theta_{B},b)=\gamma_{B}(f(\theta_{A}),b)=\gamma_{\ker(f)}(\theta_{A},b)
as required.

We have the
\mathbf{R}
-module isomorphism
B\wedge_{A}B\simeq A\oplus\ker(f)\oplus\ker(f)
. By the fact that
m
is a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra map, we obtain that
m
is the (abelian)
\mathbf{R}
-module sum structure and that the
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
\gamma_{B}
on
B\simeq A\oplus\ker(f)
vanishes on the module
\hat{\oplus}_{k\geq 2}\mathcal{P}(k)\hat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{S}_{k}}(\ker(f)^{\hat{\otimes}2}\hat{\otimes}B^{\hat{\otimes}k-2})
.
This says that
B
is an abelian extension of
A
by
\ker(f)
. Conversely, every abelian extension provides an abelian group object and the two processes are opposite to each other.
∎

As in the dg context, we define now the
A
-module of Kähler differential forms in order to represent the module of
A
-derivations.
It is given by the following coequalizer

(4.1)

\textstyle{A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}\mathcal{P}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447ptA\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}\gamma_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{P},(1)}}
\textstyle{A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega_{\mathcal{P}}A,}

where
\tilde{\gamma}_{\mathcal{P},(1)}
is the map induced by the composite

\mathcal{P}(A,\mathcal{P}(A))\to\mathcal{P}(A,\mathcal{P}(A,A))\rightarrowtail(\mathcal{P}\hat{\circ}\mathcal{P})(A,A)\xrightarrow{\gamma_{\mathcal{P}}(\operatorname{id}_{A},\operatorname{id}_{A})}\mathcal{P}(A,A)\twoheadrightarrow A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}A

since it factors through
A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}\mathcal{P}(A)
.

Proposition 4.21
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be any curved operad and
A
be any
\mathcal{P}
-algebra.
There are natural gr-dg
\mathbf{R}
-linear isomorphisms

\left(\mathrm{Der}_{A}(B,M),\partial\right)\cong\left(\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{B}^{\mathcal{P}}}(\Omega_{\mathcal{P}}B,f^{*}(M)),\partial\right),

for any
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
B
over
A
and any
A
-module
M
.
The isomorphisms restrict to the kernels of
\partial
to provide isomorphisms of
\mathbf{R}
-modules

\mathrm{Der}_{A}^{0}(B,M)\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{B}^{\mathcal{P},0}}(\Omega_{\mathcal{P}}B,f^{*}(M)).

Moreover, when
B
is a quasi-free
\mathcal{P}
-algebra on
(V,\tau:\mathcal{P}(V)\to\mathcal{P}(V))
, we get
\Omega_{\mathcal{P}}B\cong B\otimes^{\mathcal{P}}_{\tau}V
as gr-dg
\mathbf{R}
-modules.

Proof.

Let
d:B\to M
be an
A
-derivation. Then the composite

B\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}B\xrightarrow{B\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}d}B\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}M\to M

factors through the quotient
B\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}B\twoheadrightarrow\Omega_{\mathcal{P}}B
since
d
is an
A
-derivation
B\to M
and since the last map is induced by the
B
-module structure on
f^{*}(M)
. The resulting map is a map of
B
-modules since the above composite is.

In the other way round, given a
B
-module map
\rho:\Omega_{\mathcal{P}}B\to f^{*}(M)
, the restriction
B\cong I\hat{\circ}B\xrightarrow{\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\circ\operatorname{id}_{B}}\Omega_{\mathcal{P}}B\xrightarrow{\rho}f^{*}(M)
gives a map
B\to M
.
Using the definition of
\Omega_{\mathcal{P}}B
, the module structure on
f^{*}(M)
and the fact that
\rho
is a map of
B
-modules, we get that its restriction is an
A
-derivation.

The two processes are natural (graded) linear isomorphisms between the spaces of (graded) modules maps. As in Proposition
4.13
, they are compatible with the differentials
\partial
.

When

B=\mathcal{P}(V)/\left(\im(\tau^{2}-\theta_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{V})_{|V}\right)

is a quasi-free
\mathcal{P}
-algebra on
(V,\tau)
, we can compute
\Omega_{\mathcal{P}}B
by computing the coequaliser (
4.1
). It says that elements in
\Omega_{\mathcal{P}}B
are elements
\mu(b_{1},\ldots,db_{j},\ldots,b_{n})\in B\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}B
subject to the relation

\mu(b_{1},\ldots,b_{j-1},d(\nu(v_{1},\ldots,v_{m})),b_{j+1},\ldots,b_{n})\approx\\
\sum_{i=1}^{m}(\mu\circ_{j}\nu)(b_{1},\ldots,b_{j-1},v_{1},\ldots,v_{i-1},dv_{i},v_{i+1},\ldots,v_{m},b_{j+1},\ldots,b_{n}).

The relation
\tau^{2}=\gamma_{B}(\theta_{\mathcal{P}},\operatorname{id}_{B})
in
B
doesn’t create any extra relation since we already have the relation
\eta_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes{d_{B}}^{2}=\theta_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{B}
in
B\otimes^{\mathcal{P}}B
.
This gives the isomorphism
\Omega_{\mathcal{P}}B\cong B\otimes^{\mathcal{P}}_{\tau}V
.
∎

4.1.4.
Cotangent complex and André–Quillen cohomology

We consider here that
\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{K}
is a field of characteristic 0.
It follows that every curved operad
\mathcal{P}
is
\mathbb{S}
-split.
Then Theorem C.39 in
[
BMDC20
]
(see Theorem
A.3
) endows the category of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras with a model category structure.
By Theorem 7.6.5 in
[
Hir03
]
, the category
\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}/A
of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras over
A
admits a model category structure such that the weak equivalences (resp. fibrations) are the graded quasi-isomorphisms (resp. strict surjections) when we forget the maps over
A
.
We use the same notation
\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}/A
for the category of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras over
A
endowed with this model structure.
Similarly, we prove in Theorem
A.4
that the category of
A
-modules over
\mathcal{P}
is endowed with a model category structure by transferring the model category structure from gr-dg
\mathbf{R}
-modules.
Every objet in
\mathsf{Ab(\mathcal{P}\text{-}alg}/A)
is fibrant since fibration are strict surjections.

Theorem 4.22
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a curved operad and
A
be a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra.
The following two functors

A\hat{\otimes}_{-}^{\mathcal{P}}\Omega_{\mathcal{P}}-\ :\ \mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}/A\rightleftharpoons\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P},0}\cong\mathsf{Ab(\mathcal{P}\text{-}alg}/A)\ :\ A\ltimes-

form an adjoint pair which is a Quillen adjunction.
The two functors represent the bi-functor of
A
-derivations,
i.e.
there is an isomorphism of (graded) modules

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P},0}}\left(A\hat{\otimes}_{B}^{\mathcal{P}}\Omega_{\mathcal{P}}B,M\right)\cong\emph{Der}_{A}^{0}(B,M)\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}/A}\left(B,A\ltimes M\right),

for all
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
B
over
A
and for all
A
-module
M
.

Proof.

The part on the fact that we have an adjoint pair of functors follows from Propositions
4.21
,
4.3
,
4.13
,
4.19
and
4.20
.

To prove that we have a Quillen adjunction, it is enough by Lemma 1.3.4 of
[
Hov99
]
to show that the right adjoint functor
A\ltimes-
preserves the fibrations and the acyclic fibrations. Since
A\ltimes M=A\oplus M
, the proof is straightforward form the definition of the two model category structures considered here.
∎

This adjunction provides therefore an adjunction on the level of the homotopy categories. We define the cohomology of an algebra over a curved operad
\mathcal{P}
by means of its derived abelianisation.

Definition 4.23
.

The
cotangent complex
of a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
A
, denoted by
\mathbb{L}_{A}
, is defined to be the derived abelianisation
\mathbb{L}_{A}\coloneqq\mathbb{L}(A\hat{\otimes}_{-}^{\mathcal{P}}\Omega_{\mathcal{P}}-)(A)
of
A
.

Given an
A
-module
M
in
\mathsf{Ab(\mathcal{P}\text{-}alg}/A)\cong\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P},0}
, the
André–Quillen cohomology of
A
with coefficients in
M
is defined by

H_{AQ}^{n}\left(A,M\right)\coloneqq\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Ho}(\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P},0})}\left(\mathbb{L}_{A},M[n]\right)=H^{n}\left(\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P}}}\left(\mathbb{L}_{A},M\right),\partial\right).

(The last equality follows from the fact that
\partial f=0
if and only if
f
is a morphism of gr-dg modules, that is commutes with the predifferentials, and because the homotopy relation between two maps in the category of
A
-modules is given by the existence of an
h
such that
\partial h
is the difference of the two maps.)

4.2.
Computing the André–Quillen cohomology

We can make explicit one representative of the cotangent complex by means of a cofibrant replacement
R\xrightarrow{\sim}A
in the category of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras over
A
. It is given by the class of

\mathbb{L}_{R/A}\coloneqq A\hat{\otimes}_{R}^{\mathcal{P}}\Omega_{\mathcal{P}}R\in\mathsf{Ho}\left(\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P}}\right).

As in
[
BM18
]
, we use the bar-cobar resolutions
\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A\xrightarrow{\sim}A
described in Section
3.4
to describe the complex computing the André–Quillen cohomology.

Theorem 4.24
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a curved sa operad,
\mathcal{C}
be a curved altipotent cooperad and
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}
be an operadic curved twisting morphism. Let
A
be a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra. Assume that
R=\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A
be a cofibrant resolution of
A
.
We denote by
\tau
the composite
\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A\xrightarrow{\Delta_{(1)}\circ\operatorname{id}_{A}}(\mathcal{C}\hat{\circ}_{(1)}\mathcal{C})(A)\xrightarrow{-(\alpha\circ_{(1)}\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{C}})\circ\operatorname{id}_{A}}\mathcal{P}(A,\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A)
.
Then the corresponding cotangent complex has the form

\mathbb{L}_{\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A/A}\cong(A\otimes^{\mathcal{P}}_{\tau}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A,d_{\alpha}),

where
A\otimes^{\mathcal{P}}_{\tau}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A
is defined before Proposition
4.4
and the predifferential
d_{\alpha}
is induced by
d_{\mathcal{P}(A,\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A)}+\tau
.

Proof.

The
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
R=\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A
is quasi-free on
\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A
.
It follows that the cotangent complex is isomorphic to

\begin{array}[]{llll}A\otimes_{R}^{\mathcal{P}}\Omega_{\mathcal{P}}R&\cong&A\otimes_{R}^{\mathcal{P}}(R\otimes^{\mathcal{P}}_{\tau}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A)&(\textrm{Proposition \ref{prop: derivation and Kahler differential form}})\\
&\cong&A\otimes^{\mathcal{P}}_{\tau}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A&(\textrm{Proposition \ref{prop: extension and restriction of scalars}}).\end{array}

The map
\tau
is induced by the map
d_{\alpha}^{l}
on the cobar construction and the isomorphism
A\otimes_{R}^{\mathcal{P}}(R\otimes^{\mathcal{P}}_{\tau}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A)\cong A\otimes^{\mathcal{P}}_{\tau}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A
is obtained (on the lifts) by the maps
\eta_{\mathcal{P}}
and the projection
\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A
.
The Theorem finally follows from the definition of the cobar predifferential
d_{o}
.
∎

Proposition 4.25
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a curved sa operad,
\mathcal{C}
be a curved altipotent cooperad and
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}
be an operadic curved twisting morphism. Let
A
be a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra. Assume that
R=\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A
be a cofibrant resolution of
A
.
Let
M
be an
A
-module.
Then the André–Quillen cohomology is equal to

H_{AQ}^{n}(A,M)=H^{n}\left(\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P}}}\left(A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\tau}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A,M\right),\partial\right),

where
\partial f=d_{M}\cdot f-(-1)^{|f|}f\cdot d_{\alpha}
.

Proof.

This follows from Definition
4.23
and Theorem
4.24
.
∎

We propose another description for the André–Quillen cohomology that we use in the next section.

Theorem 4.26
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a curved sa operad,
\mathcal{C}
be a curved altipotent cooperad and
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}
be an operadic curved twisting morphism. Let
A
be a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra and
M
be an
A
-module.

There is an isomorphism of
\mathbf{R}
-modules

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P},0}}\left(A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\tau}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A,M\right)\cong\\
\ker\left(\partial_{\tau}:\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}}^{0}\left(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A,UM\right)\to\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}}^{1}\left(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A,UM\right)\right),

where
\partial_{\tau}\coloneqq\partial+\delta_{\alpha}^{l}
with
\delta_{\alpha}^{l}(f)
given by the composite

\mathcal{C}(A)\xrightarrow{\Delta_{(1)}\circ\operatorname{id}_{A}}(\mathcal{C}\hat{\circ}_{(1)}\mathcal{C})(A)\xrightarrow{(\alpha\circ_{(1)}\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{C}})\circ\operatorname{id}_{A}}(\mathcal{P}\hat{\circ}_{(1)}\mathcal{C})(A)\to\mathcal{P}(A,\mathcal{C}(A))\\
\xrightarrow{(-1)^{|f|}\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{P}}(\operatorname{id}_{A},f)}\mathcal{P}(A,M)\xrightarrow{\gamma_{M}}M.

The predifferential
\partial_{\tau}
satisfies

{\partial_{\tau}}^{2}=\delta_{\Theta+\partial\alpha+\frac{1}{2}[\alpha,\alpha]}^{l}=0

since
\alpha
is an operadic twisting morphism, so it is a differential.
It follows that when
R=\hat{\Omega}_{\alpha}\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A
is a cofibrant resolution of
A
, we have

H_{AQ}^{n}\left(A,M\right)=H^{n}\left(\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}}\left(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A,M\right),\partial_{\tau}\right).

Proof.

The theorem follows from Propositions
4.25
and
4.4
.
The equality
{\partial_{\tau}}^{2}=\delta_{\Theta+\partial\alpha+\frac{1}{2}[\alpha,\alpha]}^{l}
comes from the equalities:

\partial^{2}=\delta_{\Theta}^{l},\qquad\partial\delta_{\alpha}^{l}+\delta_{\alpha}^{l}\partial=\delta_{\partial\alpha}^{l}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad{\delta_{\alpha}^{l}}^{2}=\delta_{\frac{1}{2}[\alpha,\alpha]}^{l}

using the coassociativity of
\Delta
and the fact that
\alpha
has degree
-1
for the last equality.
∎

5.
The case of
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
-algebras and some perspectives

5.1.
Bar construction of a
\mathrm{Cx}_{\infty}
-algebra

In this section, we fix
\mathbf{K}=\mathbf{R}=\mathbb{R}
. We make the bar construction associated with the operadic twisting morphism
\iota:\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}\to\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}=\hat{\Omega}\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}
explicit. We recall that it is a functor

\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}:\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}\textsf{-algebras}\rightarrow\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}\textsf{-coalgebras}.

Proposition 5.1
.

We have

\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A\cong\left(\mathrm{Sym}\left(\hat{\oplus}_{k\geq 0}A[-k]\right),d_{b}\right),

where
A[k]
is the graded module shifted in cohomological degree as follows:
A[k]^{n}\coloneqq A^{n-k}
.
In the specific case where
d_{A}=0
and the
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
-algebra structure is a
\mathrm{cCx}
-algebra structure given by a Lie bracket
\{-,-\}
of cohomological degree
-1
and an endomorphism
j
of cohomological degree
0
, we have that

d_{b}\left(a_{1}[k_{1}]\cdots a_{n}[k_{n}]\right)=\\
\sum_{i<j}\pm\alpha_{k_{i},k_{j}}\{a_{i},a_{j}\}[k_{i}+k_{j}]\cdot a_{1}[k_{1}]\cdots\check{a}_{i}[k_{i}]\cdots\check{a}_{j}[k_{j}]\cdots a_{n}[k_{n}]\quad+\\
\sum_{i}\pm j(a_{i})[k_{i}-1]\cdot a_{1}[k_{1}]\cdots\check{a}_{i}[k_{i}]\cdots a_{n}[k_{n}],

where the sign is given by the Koszul sign rule for the elements
a_{i}[k_{i}]
.

In general, we have
d_{b}=d_{\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}(A)}+d_{\iota}^{r}
with

d_{\iota}^{r}\left(a_{1}[k_{1}]\cdots a_{n}[k_{n}]\right)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p+q=n+1\\
p,q\geq 1\end{subarray}}\sum_{\sigma\in Sh_{q,p-1}}\sum_{K}\alpha^{\sigma}_{k^{\prime}_{j},k^{\prime\prime}_{j}}\times\\
\hat{\jmath}_{k^{\prime},k_{\sigma(q+1)},\ldots,k_{\sigma(n)}}^{c}(\hat{\jmath}_{k^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,k^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma(q)}}^{c}(a_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,a_{\sigma(q)}),a_{\sigma(q+1)},\ldots,a_{\sigma(n)}),

where the coefficient
\alpha^{\sigma}_{k^{\prime}_{j},k^{\prime\prime}_{j}}
is given in Corollary
2.11
.

The endomorphism
d_{b}
is a coderivation satisfying

{d_{b}}^{2}=-(\theta_{\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}}\circ id_{\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A})\cdot\Delta_{\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A}.

Proof.

Theorem
2.9
gives the isomorphism
\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}(A)\cong\mathrm{Sym}\left(\hat{\oplus}_{k\geq 0}A[-k]\right)
.
Then we recall from Section
3.2.1
that the predifferential
d_{\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}(A)}
is induced by the predifferentials on
\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}
and on
A
, and

d_{\iota}^{r}:\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}(A)\xrightarrow{\Delta_{(1)}\circ id_{A}}(\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}\hat{\circ}_{(1)}\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}})(A)\xrightarrow{(id_{\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}}\circ_{(1)}\iota)\circ id_{A}}\\
\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}\hat{\circ}\mathrm{cCx}(A)\xrightarrow{id_{\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}}\circ\gamma_{A}}\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}(A).

By calculating the coefficients by means of Corollary
2.11
, we get the advertised formula.
∎

5.2.
A resolution of the
\infty
-morphisms between some
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
-algebras

In this section, we again fix
\mathbf{K}=\mathbf{R}=\mathbb{R}
, we consider
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
-algebras and the operadic twisting morphism
\iota:\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}\to\hat{\Omega}\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}
.

Lemma 5.2
.

(1)

The multiplication by
i
on the
\mathbb{R}
-algebra on
\mathbb{C}
endows the
\mathbb{R}
-module
\mathbb{C}
with a
\mathrm{cCx}
-algebra structure denoted
\gamma_{\mathbb{C}}
: the action of the shifted Lie bracket on
\mathbb{C}
is zero and the action of

\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{\bullet}

is given by the multiplication by
i
.

(2)

Let
(A,\gamma_{A})
and
(B,\gamma_{B})
be
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
-algebras and let
f:A\to B
be a
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
-algebra morphism.
Then we endow
B
with an
A
-module structure
\gamma_{B}^{A}
defined by the restriction of the
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
-algebra structure
\gamma_{B}
along
f
, that is

\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}(A,B)\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}_{\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}}(f,\operatorname{id}_{B})}\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}(B,B)\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}(B)\xrightarrow{\gamma_{B}}B.

(3)

In the case where
B=\mathbb{C}
and
f=0
, we obtain that
\mathbb{C}
is endowed with the
A
-module structure
\gamma_{\mathbb{C}}^{A}
defined on the generators of
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
by

\gamma_{\mathbb{C}}^{A}(j_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}\otimes a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes c\otimes\otimes a_{n-1})\coloneqq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}c&\text{when }(n,k_{1})=(1,0),\\
i\cdot c&\text{when }(n,k_{1})=(1,1),\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

Proof.

(1)

The assertion follows for example from Lemma
2.8
.

(2)

The described map defines an
A
-module map since
f
is a
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
-algebra morphism between
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
-algebras.

(3)

This is an explicitation of the previous definition in the specified situation.

∎

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
(A,\gamma_{A})
be a
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
-algebra and
\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A
its bar construction.
The
\mathbb{R}
-module
\mathbb{C}
is endow with its
\mathrm{Cx}
-algebra structure
\gamma_{\mathbb{C}}
(which can also be viewed as a
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
structure) and with its trivial
A
-module structure
\gamma_{\mathbb{C}}^{A}
described in the previous lemma.
We have the isomorphism of
\mathbb{R}
-modules

Z^{0}_{AQ}((A,\gamma_{A}),(\mathbb{C},\gamma_{\mathbb{C}}^{A}))\cong\infty\text{-}\mathrm{mor}((A,\gamma_{A}),(\mathbb{C},\gamma_{\mathbb{C}}))

where
Z^{0}_{AQ}((A,\gamma_{A}),(\mathbb{C},\gamma_{\mathbb{C}}^{A}))
is

\ker\left(\partial_{\tau}:\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}}^{0}\left(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A,U\mathbb{C}\right)\to\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}}^{1}\left(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}A,U\mathbb{C}\right)\right)

described in Theorem
4.26
.

Proof.

Let
\varphi\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}}^{0}(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A,\mathbb{C})
such that
0=\partial_{\tau}(\varphi)=\partial(\varphi)+\delta_{\iota}^{l}(\varphi)
, where
\delta_{\iota}^{l}(\varphi)
is the composite

\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}(A)\xrightarrow{\Delta_{(1)}\circ\operatorname{id}_{A}}(\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}\hat{\circ}_{(1)}\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}})(A)\xrightarrow{(\iota\circ_{(1)}\operatorname{id}_{\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}})\circ\operatorname{id}_{A}}(\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}\hat{\circ}_{(1)}\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}})(A)\to\\
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}(A,\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}(A))\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}_{\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}}(\operatorname{id}_{A},\varphi)}\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}(A,\mathbb{C})\xrightarrow{\gamma_{\mathbb{C}}^{A}}\mathbb{C}.

The map
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}(A,\mathbb{C})\xrightarrow{\gamma_{\mathbb{C}}^{A}}\mathbb{C}
factors through
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}(A,\mathbb{C})\to I\circ\mathbb{C}\oplus{\tiny\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise-1.7014pt\hbox{$\textstyle{1}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 11.68057pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.7014pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-4.7604pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.7014pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 5.84029pt\raise-4.7604pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise-1.7014pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 11.68057pt\raise-9.14235pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.7014pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-13.52429pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.7014pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.7014pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}}\otimes\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{C}
.
Similarly, the composition product
\gamma_{\mathbb{C}}:\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}(\mathbb{C})\to\mathbb{C}
factors through
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}(\mathbb{C})\to I\circ\mathbb{C}\oplus{\tiny\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise .
We therefore have

0=\partial_{\tau}(\varphi)=\partial(\varphi)+\delta_{\iota}^{l}(\varphi)=\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{1}{n!}l_{n}^{\iota}(\varphi,\ldots,\varphi)

and
\varphi
is an algebraic twisting morphism
\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A\to\mathbb{C}
, or equivalently an
\infty
-morphism
A\rightsquigarrow\mathbb{C}
.
∎

Remark 5.4
.

In the light of Theorem 4.1 in
[
BM18
]
, when
A
is the algebra encoding a complex structure on a formal pointed manifold (in particular, it is concentrated in degree 0), we get that the 0-th André–Quillen cohomology group computes formal holomorphic functions on it:

H^{0}_{AQ}((A,\gamma_{A}),(\mathbb{C},\gamma_{\mathbb{C}}^{A}))\cong\\
\ker\left(\partial_{\tau}:\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}}^{0}\left(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A,U\mathbb{C}\right)\to\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}}^{1}\left(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A,U\mathbb{C}\right)\right)\cong\\
\infty\text{-}\mathrm{mor}((A,\gamma_{A}),(\mathbb{C},\gamma_{\mathbb{C}}))

since
\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A
is concentrated in non positive cohomological degrees (and therefore
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}}^{-1}\left(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A,U\mathbb{C}\right)=\{0\}
).

5.3.
A
\mathbb{C}
-cdga structure on the André–Quillen complex in the context of
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
-algebras

As in the two previous sections, we fix
\mathbf{K}=\mathbf{R}=\mathbb{R}
.
In the following proposition, we use the usual associative and commutative structure on
\mathbb{C}
to endow the André–Quillen cochain complex
\left(\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}}(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A,\mathbb{C}),\partial_{\tau}\right)
with a
\mathbb{C}
-cdga structure.

Proposition 5.5
.

Let
A
be a
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
-algebra and
\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A
its bar construction.
We endow
\mathbb{C}
with its trivial
A
-module structure
\gamma_{\mathbb{C}}^{A}
obtained by restriction of
A\xrightarrow{0}\mathbb{C}
(and described above).
The dg module
\left(\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}}(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A,\mathbb{C}),\partial_{\tau}\right)
is endowed with a structure of
\mathbb{C}
-cdga denoted by
m
and defined, for
\varphi_{1}
, …,
\varphi_{n}
in
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}}(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A,\mathbb{C})
, by

\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A\cong\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}(A)\xrightarrow{\Delta_{\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}}\circ\operatorname{id}_{A}}\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A)\twoheadrightarrow\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{Lie}^{\text{!`}}(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A)\cong\mathrm{Com}^{c}(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A)\\
\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{Com}^{c}(n)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A)^{\otimes n}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\varphi_{n}}\mathrm{Com}^{c}(n)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\mathbb{C}^{\otimes n}\xrightarrow{m_{\mathbb{C}}}\mathbb{C},

where
m_{\mathbb{C}}
is the usual (commutative) multiplication on
\mathbb{C}
(we recall that
\mathrm{Com}^{c}(n)\cong\mathbb{R}
).
The
\mathbb{C}
-action is given by the action of
\mathbb{C}
on the codomain
\mathbb{C}
.

Proof.

The morphism
\mathrm{Com}(\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}}(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A,\mathbb{C}))\to\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}}(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A,\mathbb{C})
described above defines a commutative and associative structure on
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}}(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A,\mathbb{C})
because the projection
\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathpzc{s}\mathrm{Lie}^{\text{!`}}\cong\mathrm{Com}^{c}
is a morphism of curved cooperads.
The compatibility with the
\mathbb{C}
-action is direct to check.

It remains to show that the differential
\partial_{\tau}
is a derivation with respect to the
\mathrm{Com}
-algebra structure
m
.
We have

\partial\cdot m(\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{n})=m(\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{n})\cdot d_{\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A}=\sum_{h}m(\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{h}\cdot d_{\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A},\cdots,\varphi_{n})

since
\Delta_{\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}}
is coassociative and
d_{\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}}=0
.
Moreover, using the fact that the action
\gamma_{\mathbb{C}}^{A}
is trivial and the fact that
\iota_{|I}=0
, we get that the trees appearing in the computation of
\delta_{\iota}^{l}(m(\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{n}))
have the form

t_{1}={\tiny\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern Similarly, the trees appearing in the computation of
\sum_{j}m(\varphi_{1},\ldots,\delta_{\iota}^{l}(\varphi_{h}),\ldots,\varphi_{n})
have the form

t_{2}={\tiny\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 10.421pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\cr&&&\cr&&&\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-10.421pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern
