0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-1.7014pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-42.62463pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-1.7014pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 20.23697pt\raise-42.62463pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-1.7014pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 44.3268pt\raise-42.62463pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-1.7014pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 70.78424pt\raise-42.62463pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-1.7014pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}}.

Because
\gamma_{\mathbb{C}}^{A}
is
\mathbb{C}
-linear, applying the maps
\varphi_{j}
and the action
\gamma_{\mathbb{C}}^{A}
, the two corresponding trees give the same result.
It remains to compute the coefficients in front of these trees. These coefficients appear after applying the decomposition map two times.
We shortly recall the formula (given in Theorem
2.9
) here

\Delta^{\theta}\left(\jmath_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}^{c}\right)=\\
\sum\frac{1}{p!}\beta^{\sigma}_{k^{\prime}_{j},k^{\prime\prime}_{j}}\times\left(\jmath_{l^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,l^{\prime}_{p}}^{c};\jmath_{k^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,k^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma(q_{1})}}^{c},\jmath_{k^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma(q_{1}+1)},\ldots,k^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma(q_{1}+q_{2})}}^{c},\ldots\right)^{\sigma^{-1}}

where the sum
\sum
is given by
\sum_{q_{1}+\cdots+q_{p}=n}\sum_{\sigma\in Sh_{q_{1},\ldots,q_{p}}}\sum_{k^{\prime}_{j}+k^{\prime\prime}_{j}=k_{j}}
, the integers
l^{\prime}_{i}
are defined by
l^{\prime}_{i}:=k^{\prime}_{\sigma(q_{1}+\cdots+q_{i-1}+1)}+\cdots+k^{\prime}_{\sigma(q_{1}+\cdots+q_{i})}
and the number
\beta^{\sigma}_{k^{\prime}_{j},k^{\prime\prime}_{j}}
is equal to

\beta^{\sigma}_{k^{\prime}_{j},k^{\prime\prime}_{j}}\coloneqq\operatorname{sgn}_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}\sigma\times\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}_{j},k^{\prime\prime}_{j}}^{\sigma}\times\prod_{i=1}^{p}\alpha_{k^{\prime}_{\sigma(q_{1}+\cdots+q_{i-1}+1)},\,\ldots,k^{\prime}_{\sigma(q_{1}+\cdots+q_{i})}}

where
\operatorname{sgn}_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}\sigma
is the signature of the restriction of
\sigma
to the indices
j
such that
k_{j}
is odd (after relabeling the remaining
\sigma(j)
in a way that the order of the
\sigma(j)
does not change) and where

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}_{j},\,k^{\prime\prime}_{j}}^{\sigma}&\coloneqq&(-1)^{\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{\sigma(i)}^{\prime\prime}(k_{\sigma(i+1)}^{\prime}+\cdots+k_{\sigma(n)}^{\prime})}\\
\alpha_{k^{\prime}_{1},\,\ldots,\,k^{\prime}_{q}}&\coloneqq&\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\in Sh_{k^{\prime}_{1},\,\cdots,\,k^{\prime}_{q}}}\operatorname{sgn}\sigma^{\prime},\,\textrm{with convention }\alpha_{0,\,\ldots,\,0}:=1.\end{array}\right.

First we compute the coefficient in front of the term
t_{1}
.
We calculate
\Delta^{\theta}\left(\jmath_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}^{c}\right)
and compute the terms having the form
(\jmath_{0,\ldots,0}^{c};\jmath_{k_{1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots}^{c},\ldots)
.
We get the sum

\sum_{q_{1}+\cdots+q_{p}=n}\sum_{\sigma\in Sh_{q_{1},\ldots,q_{p}}}\frac{1}{p!}\operatorname{sgn}_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}\sigma\times\varepsilon_{0,k_{j}}^{\sigma}\times\prod_{i=1}^{p}1\times\\
\left(\jmath_{0,\ldots,0}^{c};\jmath_{k_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,k_{\sigma(q_{1})}}^{c},\jmath_{k_{\sigma(q_{1}+1)},\ldots,k_{\sigma(q_{1}+q_{2})}}^{c},\ldots\right)^{\sigma^{-1}}

=\sum_{q_{1}+\cdots+q_{p}=n}\sum_{\sigma\in Sh_{q_{1},\ldots,q_{p}}}\frac{1}{p!}\operatorname{sgn}_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}\sigma\times\left(\jmath_{0,\ldots,0}^{c};\jmath_{k_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,k_{\sigma(q_{1})}}^{c},\ldots\right)^{\sigma^{-1}}.

Then we apply the infinitesimal decomposition on a term
\jmath_{k_{\sigma(q_{1}+\ldots+q_{s}+1)},\ldots}^{c}
and compute the terms having the form of
t_{1}
.
In front of the tree
(j_{1}^{c};\jmath_{k^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma(q_{1}+\ldots+q_{s}+1)},\ldots}^{c})
, we obtain the coefficient

\varepsilon_{k_{\sigma(q_{1}+\ldots+q_{s}+j)}^{\prime},k_{\sigma(q_{1}+\ldots+q_{s}+j)}^{\prime\prime}}^{\operatorname{id}}

corresponding to the Koszul sign coming from the fact that the tree

\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{\bullet}

goes down and where only one
k^{\prime}_{j}
is equal to 1 and the others are equal to 0.
Finally, every tree
t_{1}
which appears has a coefficient equal to the sign given by the Koszul sign rule (due to the fact that we permute the generators

\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{\bullet}

).

We now compute the coefficient in front of the term
t_{2}
.
In the sum
\Delta^{\theta}\left(\jmath_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}^{c}\right)
, the coefficient in front of the tree
(\jmath_{1}^{c};\jmath_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{h}-1,\ldots,k_{n}}^{c})
is
\varepsilon_{k_{j}^{\prime},k_{j}^{\prime\prime}}^{\operatorname{id}}
corresponding to the Koszul sign coming from the fact that the tree

\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{\bullet}

goes down. (All the other coefficients in the formula are equal to 1.)
In the decompositions
k^{\prime}_{j}+k^{\prime\prime}_{j}=k_{j}
, only
k^{\prime}_{h}
is equal to 1 and the others
k_{j}^{\prime}
are equal to 0.
Then applying
\Delta^{\theta}
to
\jmath_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{h}-1,\ldots,k_{n}}^{c}
and considering the terms
(\jmath_{0,\ldots,0}^{c};\ldots)
gives the sum

\sum_{q_{1}+\cdots+q_{p}=n}\sum_{\sigma\in Sh_{q_{1},\ldots,q_{p}}}\frac{1}{p!}\operatorname{sgn}_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{h}-1,\ldots,k_{n}}\sigma\times\left(\jmath_{0,\ldots,0}^{c};\jmath_{k_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,k_{\sigma(q_{1})}}^{c},\ldots\right)^{\sigma^{-1}}.

Again, every tree
t_{2}
which appears has a coefficient equal to the sign given by the Koszul sign rule due to the fact that we permute the generators

\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{\bullet}

.

It follows form the previous explanations that

\delta_{\iota}^{l}(m(\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{n}))=\sum_{h}m(\varphi_{1},\ldots,\delta_{\iota}^{l}(\varphi_{h}),\ldots,\varphi_{n}).

As a consequence, the differential
\partial_{\tau}
is a derivation with respect to the
\mathrm{Com}
-algebra structure
m
. This concludes the proof.
∎

5.4.
Relation with dg algebras with entire functional calculus

We conclude this section by showing that the functor given by the André–Quillen cochains from
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
-algebras with
\infty
-morphisms to EFC-dga in the sense of
[
Pri20
, Definitions 1.4 and 2.10]
is fully faithful.

We recall the definition of dg algebra with entire functional calculus.

Definition 5.6
.

(1)

Let
\mathsf{EFC}
the category with objects
\{\mathbb{C}^{n}\}_{n\geq 0}
and morphisms consisting of complex-analytic maps.

(2)

A
\mathbb{C}
-algebra
A
is a
EFC
\mathbb{C}
-algebra
if it endowed with a product-preserving set-valued functor
\Psi:\mathsf{EFC}\to\mathsf{Set}
.

(3)

A chain complex
(A,d_{A})
of
\mathbb{Q}
-vector spaces is an
EFC-dga
if it is a dg commutative algebra such that
Z^{0}A=\ker(d_{A}:A^{0}\to A^{1})
is an EFC
\mathbb{C}
-algebra.

Theorem 5.7
.

The functor

\begin{array}[]{rccl}C_{AQ}^{\bullet}:&(\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}\textsf{-alg.},\infty\text{-}\mathrm{mor})&\to&\textsf{EFC-dga},\\
&(A,\gamma_{A},d_{A})&\mapsto&\left(\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}}(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A,\mathbb{C}),\partial_{\tau}\right)\end{array}

is well-defined and fully faithful.

Proof.

Proposition
5.5
ensures that
C_{AQ}^{\bullet}(A)
is endowed with a
\mathbb{C}
-cdga structure.
By Proposition
5.3
, we obtain that
Z^{0}C_{AQ}^{\bullet}(A)\cong\infty\text{-}\mathrm{mor}(A,\mathbb{C})
.
From
[
BM18
, Theorem 4.1]
, we know that complex-analytic (or equivalently holomorphic) functions on a formal pointed manifold endowed with a complex structure coincide with
\infty
-morphisms
A\rightsquigarrow\mathbb{C}
, where
A
is the
\mathrm{Cx}_{\infty}
-algebra encoding the (formal) complex structure. Because we can compose
\infty
-morphisms, we get that
Z^{0}C_{AQ}^{\bullet}(A)
is endowed with an EFC
\mathbb{C}
-algebra structure.
Indeed,
B_{\iota}
is a right adjoint functor so
\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}(\mathbb{C}^{n})\cong(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}\mathbb{C})^{n}
and given a complex-analytic function
f:\mathbb{C}^{n}\to\mathbb{C}
, its Taylor series at 0 is given by an
\infty
-morphism
T_{0}^{f}:\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}\mathbb{C}^{n}\to\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}\mathbb{C}
.
Given
\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}
-coalgebra morphisms
\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{n}:\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A\to\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}\mathbb{C}
, the composition
T_{0}^{f}\cdot(\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{n})\cdot\Delta^{n-1}_{diag}
is the desired
\infty
-morphism (where
\Delta^{n-1}_{diag}:\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A\to(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A)^{n}
is the set diagonal morphism).
It is compatible with the composition of complex-analytic functions since the equivalence given
[
BM18
, Theorem 4.1]
is functorial.
The association
C_{AQ}^{\bullet}
is functorial since the bar construction and the Hom-functor are and since the compatibility with the differentials follows from the fact that morphisms of algebras preserve the algebra structures and the predifferentials.
It follows that
C_{AQ}^{\bullet}
is a well-defined functor.

The functor
C_{AQ}^{\bullet}
is fully faithful because

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}}(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A,\mathbb{C})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}}(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A,\mathbb{R})\oplus\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}}(\hat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}A,\mathbb{R})

and
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathsf{gr}}}(-,\mathbb{R})
is fully faithful. (We recall that we consider
\infty
-morphisms between
\mathrm{cCx}_{\infty}
-algebras, that is
\mathrm{cCx}^{\text{!`}}
-coalgebra morphisms between the bar constructions.)
∎

Appendix A
Model category structures

In this section, we recall the model category structure on gr-dg modules, on curved operads and on algebras over a curved operad presented in
[
BMDC20
, Appendix C]
, and we extend it by replacing the category of gr-dg
\mathbf{K}
-modules by the category of gr-dg
\mathbf{R}
-modules (where
\mathbf{R}
is a unital
\mathbf{K}
-algebra).

A.1.
Reminder on a model category structures on gr-dg modules

Let
\mathbf{K}
be a field.
In
[
BMDC20
, Appendix C]
, the authors endow the category of gr-dg
\mathbf{K}
-modules with a proper cofibrantly generated symmetric monoidal model category structure.
We recall this structure.

We denote by
\mathbf{K}^{(q)}
the complete
\mathbf{K}
-module given by

\mathbf{K}^{(q)}=F_{0}\mathbf{K}^{(q)}=F_{q}\mathbf{K}^{(q)}\supset F_{q+1}\mathbf{K}^{(q)}=0.

(The filtration is induced by a graduation concentrated in degree
q
.)
The notation
\mathbf{K}_{n}^{(q)}
means that we consider it in degree
n
within a (complete) filtered complex.

We define, for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
and
q\in\mathbb{N}
, the complete gr-dg
\mathbf{K}
-modules

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{Z}}^{0,\infty}_{q,n}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{K}^{(q)}_{n}\xrightarrow{1}\mathbf{K}^{(q)}_{n-1}\xrightarrow{1}\mathbf{K}^{(q+1)}_{n-2}\xrightarrow{1}\mathbf{K}^{(q+1)}_{n-3}\xrightarrow{1}\mathbf{K}^{(q+2)}_{n-4}\to\dots\right)^{\wedge}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{\oplus}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbf{K}_{n-k}^{\left(q+\lfloor\frac{k}{2}\rfloor\right)},

and

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{Z}}^{1,\infty}_{q,n}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{K}^{(q)}_{n}\xrightarrow{1}\mathbf{K}^{(q+1)}_{n-1}\xrightarrow{1}\mathbf{K}^{(q+1)}_{n-2}\xrightarrow{1}\mathbf{K}^{(q+2)}_{n-3}\xrightarrow{1}\mathbf{K}^{(q+2)}_{n-4}\to\dots\right)^{\wedge}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{\oplus}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbf{K}_{n-k}^{\left(q+\lceil\frac{k}{2}\rceil\right)}.

We also define the complete gr-dg
\mathbf{K}
-module

\hat{\mathcal{B}}^{1,\infty}_{q,n}\coloneqq\hat{\mathcal{Z}}^{0,\infty}_{q,n+1}\oplus\hat{\mathcal{Z}}^{0,\infty}_{q+1,n}.

We denote by
\varphi^{\infty}_{q,n}:\hat{\mathcal{Z}}^{1,\infty}_{q,n}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}^{1,\infty}_{q,n}
the morphism of complete gr-dg
\mathbf{K}
-modules defined by the following diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{K}_{n}^{(q)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\tiny\begin{pmatrix}1\\
1\end{pmatrix}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}_{n-1}^{(q+1)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\tiny\begin{pmatrix}1\\
1\end{pmatrix}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}_{n-2}^{(q+1)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\tiny\begin{pmatrix}1\\
1\end{pmatrix}}}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}_{n+1}^{(q)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}_{n}^{(q)}\oplus\mathbf{K}_{n}^{(q+1)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}_{n-1}^{(q+1)}\oplus\mathbf{K}_{n-1}^{(q+1)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}_{n-2}^{(q+1)}\oplus\mathbf{K}_{n-2}^{(q+2)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots.}

We fix the sets

\displaystyle I_{0}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\{\varphi^{\infty}_{q,n}:\hat{\mathcal{Z}}^{1,\infty}_{q,n}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}^{1,\infty}_{q,n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z},\,q\in\mathbb{N}}

\displaystyle J_{0}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\{0\to\hat{\mathcal{Z}}^{0,\infty}_{q,n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z},\,q\in\mathbb{N}}.

We say that a map
p:(X,\,F)\to(Y,\,F^{\prime})
is
strict
when it satisfies
p(F_{q}X)=p(X)\cap F^{\prime}_{q}Y
for all
q
. When
p
is a surjection, this means that
p(F_{q}X)=F^{\prime}_{q}Y
for all
q
.

Then (Theorems C.15 and Proposition C.21 in
[
BMDC20
]
), the category of (complete) gr-dg
\mathbf{K}
-modules is endowed with a symmetric monoidal model category structure where:

(1)

weak equivalences are graded quasi-isomorphisms (that is a quasi-isomorphism after application of the graded functor),

(2)

fibrations are strict surjections, and

(3)

I_{0}^{\infty}
and
J_{0}^{\infty}
are the sets of generating cofibrations and generating acyclic cofibrations respectively.

Let
\mathbf{R}
be a unital
\mathbf{K}
-algebra.

Proposition A.1
.

By means of the free-forgetful adjunction

\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\otimes_{\mathbf{K}}-:\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{K}}^{\mathrm{gr}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{gr}}:U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

we can transfer the model category structure presented above on the category
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{gr}}
of gr-dg
\mathbf{K}
-modules to the category
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{gr}}
of gr-dg
\mathbf{R}
-modules.
Weak equivalences (resp. fibrations) are graded quasi-isomorphisms (resp. strict surjections) and the model structure is right proper.

Proof.

We apply Theorem 3.3 in
[
Cra95
]
to the above adjunction.
The category
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\mathrm{gr}}
admits limits and colimits which are computed in sets.
It is enough to remark that
U
preserve all colimits so filtered
\aleph_{1}
-colimits and that it maps relative
\mathbf{R}\otimes_{\mathbf{K}}J_{0}^{\infty}
-cell complexes to weak equivalences (
\mathbf{K}
is a field so
\mathbf{R}
is a free
\mathbf{K}
-module).

Every object is fibrant, so by
[
Hir03
, Corollary 13.1.3]
the model category structure is right proper.
∎

A.2.
Model category structures on curved operads and algebras over them

Applying Proposition
A.1
to the ring
\mathbf{K}[\mathbb{S}_{m}]
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
, we obtain a right proper cofibrantly generated model category structure on the category
\mathcal{M}_{m}
of complete gr-dg
\mathbf{K}[\mathbb{S}_{m}]
-modules.
Considering this collection
(\mathcal{M}_{m},\,W_{m},\,I_{m},\,J_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
of cofibrantly generated model category structures, we have that the product

\mathbb{S}\textrm{-}\mathsf{Mod}\coloneqq\left(\prod_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{M}_{m},\,\prod_{m\in\mathbb{N}}W_{m},\,\prod_{m\in\mathbb{N}}I_{m}\right)

is also a cofibrantly generated model category structure (see for example
[
Hir03
, 11.6]
). A morphism
f:M\to N
in
\mathcal{M}
is a weak equivalence (resp. fibration) if the underlying collection of morphisms
\{M(m)\to N(m)\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
consists of weak equivalences (resp. fibrations). Moreover the set
I
(resp.
J
) of generating cofibrations (resp. acyclic cofibrations) can be described explicitly as follows:

\texttt{I}=\{\hat{\mathcal{Z}}^{1,\infty}_{q,n}(m)\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}^{1,\infty}_{q,n}(m)\}\text{ and }\texttt{J}=\{0\to\hat{\mathcal{Z}}^{0,\infty}_{q,n}(m)\},

where
\hat{\mathcal{Z}}^{k,\infty}_{q,n}(m)
(resp.
\hat{\mathcal{B}}^{1,\infty}_{q,n}(m)
) is the complete gr-dg
\mathbb{S}
-module obtained by the complete free gr-dg
\mathbf{K}[\mathbb{S}_{m}]
-module
\hat{\mathcal{Z}}^{k,\infty}_{q,n}\otimes\mathbf{K}[\mathbb{S}_{m}]
(resp.
\hat{\mathcal{B}}^{1,\infty}_{q,n}\otimes\mathbf{K}[\mathbb{S}_{m}]
) put in arity
m
.
We denote by
\mathcal{W}
the subcategory of weak equivalences.
Notice that the domains of elements of
I
or
J
are small (
\aleph_{1}
-small) in the category
\mathbb{S}\textrm{-}\mathsf{Mod}
.

The free curved operad is described in
[
BMDC20
, Section 2]
and is described as the left adjoint of the free-forgetful adjunction

\textstyle{\mathsf{c}\mathcal{T}:\mathbb{S}\textrm{-}\mathsf{Mod}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{Curved\ operads}:U,\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

where
\mathsf{c}\mathcal{T}(M,d_{M})=\left(\mathcal{T}(M\oplus\vartheta I)/\left(\im({d_{M}}^{2}-[\vartheta,\,-])\right),\,\bar{d}_{M},\,\bar{\vartheta}\right)
for
\vartheta
is a formal parameter in homological degree
-2
and in filtration degree
1
, the map
\bar{d}_{M}
is the derivation induced by
d_{M}
and
\left(\im({d_{M}}^{2}-[\vartheta,\,-])\right)
is the ideal generated by the image of the map
{d_{M}}^{2}-[\vartheta,\,-]:M\to\mathcal{T}(M\oplus\vartheta I)
.
By means of Theorem 3.3 of
[
Cra95
]
, we transfer along this adjunction the cofibrantly generated model category structure on
\mathbb{S}\textrm{-}\mathsf{Mod}
to the category of curved operads.

Theorem A.2

(Theorem C.30 in
[
BMDC20
]
)
.

The category of curved operads is endowed with a cofibrantly generated model category structure where the generating (resp. acyclic) cofibrations are
\mathsf{c}\mathcal{T}\texttt{I}
(resp.
\mathsf{c}\mathcal{T}\texttt{J}
). A map
f:\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{Q}
is a
weak equivalence
if and only if, in every arity, it is a graded quasi-isomorphism of gr-dg
\mathbb{S}
-modules.
Moreover
(\mathsf{c}\mathcal{T},\,U)
is a Quillen pair with respect to the cofibrantly model structures.

Following Hinich
[
Hin97
]
, we similarly endow the category
\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}
of algebras over an
\mathbb{S}
-split curved operad
(\mathcal{P},\,d,\,\theta)
with a model category structure. We apply Theorem 3.3 of
[
Cra95
]
to the free-forgetful functor adjunction given in
[
BMDC20
, Proposition C.35]
between the categories of gr-dg
\mathbf{K}
-modules and
\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}

\textstyle{F_{\mathcal{P}}:\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbf{K}}^{\mathrm{gr}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}:\#,\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

where

(V,\,d_{V})\mapsto F_{\mathcal{P}}(V,\,d_{V})\coloneqq\left(\mathcal{P}(V)/\left(\im\left(\eta\otimes({d_{V}}^{2})-\theta\otimes\operatorname{id}_{V}\right)\right),\,d_{\overline{\mathcal{P}(V)}}\right).

Theorem A.3

(Theorem C.39 in
[
BMDC20
]
)
.

Let
(\mathcal{P},\,d,\,\theta)
be a curved operad which is
\mathbb{S}
-split. The category
\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}
of
(\mathcal{P},\,d,\,\theta)
-algebras is a cofibrantly generated model category with generating cofibrations
F_{\mathcal{P}}(I_{0}^{\infty})
and generating acyclic cofibrations
F_{\mathcal{P}}(J_{0}^{\infty})
. The weak equivalences are the maps that are graded quasi-isomorphisms.

By the same arguments, we prove that the category of
A
-module over
\mathcal{P}
, for
\mathcal{P}
a curved operad and
A
a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra, is endowed with a similar model structure.

Theorem A.4
.

The category
\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P},0}
of
A
-modules over
\mathcal{P}
, where
\mathcal{P}
is a curved operad and
A
a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra, is endowed with a cofibrantly generated model category structure such that

•

the weak equivalences are the graded quasi-isomorphisms,

•

the fibrations are the strict surjections.

Proof.

We transfer the model category structure from the category of gr-dg
\mathbf{K}
-modules to
A
-modules over
\mathcal{P}
by means of Theorem 3.3 in
[
Cra95
]
using the free-forgetful adjunction given in Proposition
4.3
.
The category
\mathsf{Mod}_{A}^{\mathcal{P}}
is complete and cocomplete by Lemma
4.7
.
It follows that the forgetful functor
U
preserves all colimits; in particular it preserves filtered
\aleph_{1}
-colimits.
We finally have to show that
U
maps relative
A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}(J_{0}^{\infty})
-cell complexes to weak equivalences.
We have
\mathrm{Gr}(A\hat{\otimes}^{\mathcal{P}}-)\cong\mathrm{Gr}A\otimes^{\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}}\mathrm{Gr}(-)\cong U_{\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}}(\mathrm{Gr}A)\otimes\mathrm{Gr}(-)
which is exact since
\mathbf{K}
is a field (so
U_{\mathrm{Gr}\mathcal{P}}(\mathrm{Gr}A)
is flat).
This concludes the proof.
∎
